### 6.7 Output Encoding and Rendering Controls


**Systemic weaknesses:** [W-009](#w-009)
**Verdict:** 🔴 Unsafe

<!-- The line below is mechanically derived from the controls table — LLM must not re-author it. -->
**Controls covered:**

- [6.7.1 Output Encoding / XSS Prevention](#671-output-encoding--xss-prevention)

**Implemented controls:** `frontend/src/app/search-result/search-result.component.ts:110`,143; `frontend/src/app/administration/administration.component.ts:73`,91; `frontend/src/app/last-login-ip/last-login-ip.component.ts:39`; `frontend/src/app/track-result/track-result.component.ts:48`; `server.ts:188`.

**Assessment:** Angular's template engine escapes output by default, providing a framework-level XSS barrier. Four components call `bypassSecurityTrustHtml()` on attacker-controlled strings, explicitly disabling that barrier for those bindings. Each call creates an independent stored-XSS execution path.

<a id="output-encoding-xss-prevention"></a>
#### 6.7.1 Output Encoding / XSS Prevention

**Status:** 🔴 Unsafe - Angular DomSanitizer bypasses defeat the framework XSS barrier at multiple active rendering paths.

`frontend/src/app/search-result/search-result.component.ts:110`,143; `frontend/src/app/administration/administration.component.ts:73`,91; `frontend/src/app/last-login-ip/last-login-ip.component.ts:39`; `frontend/src/app/track-result/track-result.component.ts:48`; `server.ts:188`

**Security assessment**

Angular template bindings are safe by default, but four components call `bypassSecurityTrustHtml()` on user-controlled strings, each creating an independent XSS execution path:

- `frontend/src/app/search-result/search-result.component.ts:110,143` - product description and the search term reflected in the results heading.
- `frontend/src/app/administration/administration.component.ts:73,91` - user email address and feedback comment rendered in the admin panel.
- `frontend/src/app/last-login-ip/last-login-ip.component.ts:39` - the `lastLoginIp` value stored in the JWT payload and in the database.
- `frontend/src/app/track-result/track-result.component.ts:48` - order tracking display.

`server.ts:188` applies `sanitize-html` to the product description before returning it, but the Angular component ignores that sanitization and marks the raw received HTML as trusted.

**Relevant findings**

- 🟠 [F-017](#f-017) — Stored XSS
- 🟠 [F-018](#f-018) — Stored XSS
- 🟠 [F-019](#f-019) — Stored XSS

### 6.8 Browser and Cross-Origin Controls


**Systemic weaknesses:** [W-007](#w-007)
**Verdict:** 🔴 Unsafe

<!-- The line below is mechanically derived from the controls table — LLM must not re-author it. -->
**Controls covered:**

- [6.8.1 CORS Policy](#681-cors-policy)

**Implemented controls:** `server.ts:181-183`; `lib/startup/registerWebsocketEvents.ts:20`.

**Assessment:** `server.ts:181-183` installs the `cors()` middleware with no configuration, setting `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *` on all API responses. No `Content-Security-Policy` header is emitted. The WebSocket server at `lib/startup/registerWebsocketEvents.ts:20` inherits the same open-origin policy, extending it to real-time connections.

<a id="cors-policy"></a>
#### 6.8.1 CORS Policy

**Status:** 🔴 Unsafe - Unsafe - express `cors()` with no options allows all origins on all API endpoints.

`server.ts:181-183`; `lib/startup/registerWebsocketEvents.ts:20`

**Security assessment**

`server.ts:181-183` calls `cors()` without an `origin` or `credentials` option, which configures every API response with `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *`. Combined with JWT storage in `localStorage` (rather than a `SameSite` cookie), this allows any origin to issue credentialed API calls on behalf of a logged-in user via fetched credentials. `lib/startup/registerWebsocketEvents.ts:20` establishes the Socket\.IO server without an origin allowlist, extending the open-CORS policy to WebSocket upgrade handshakes. No `Content-Security-Policy` header was observed in any response path during the scan.

**Relevant findings**

- 🟠 [F-009](#f-009) — OAuth CSRF
- 🟡 [F-055](#f-055) — Wildcard CORS Permits Credentialed Cross-Origin Requests

### 6.9 Cryptography Secrets and Data Protection


**Systemic weaknesses:** [W-010](#w-010), [W-013](#w-013)
**Verdict:** 🔴 Unsafe

<!-- The line below is mechanically derived from the controls table — LLM must not re-author it. -->
**Controls covered:**

- [6.9.1 Password Storage](#691-password-storage)
- [6.9.2 Secret and Key Management](#692-secret-and-key-management)
- [6.9.3 Transport Encryption](#693-transport-encryption)

**Implemented controls:** `lib/insecurity.ts:43`; `models/user.ts:77`; `routes/login.ts:34`; `lib/insecurity.ts:23`,44; `routes/checkKeys.ts:10`; `server.ts:131`.

**Assessment:** Three distinct cryptographic failures converge here. Passwords are hashed with unsalted `MD5` - a non-KDF with no work factor. The RSA private key used to sign all JWTs and the HMAC key used to hash security-question answers are both hardcoded literals in `lib/insecurity.ts`. Transport encryption is available but HTTP access is not redirected to HTTPS.

<a id="password-storage"></a>
#### 6.9.1 Password Storage

**Status:** 🔴 Unsafe - Unsafe - `MD5` is not a password KDF; all hashes immediately crackable offline.

`lib/insecurity.ts:43`; `models/user.ts:77`; `routes/login.ts:34`

**Security assessment**

Two code paths both hash passwords with `MD5`:

- `lib/insecurity.ts:43` hashes `req.body.password` with `md5()` inline before the Sequelize upsert.
- `models/user.ts:77` applies a second `MD5` hash in the Sequelize `beforeCreate` hook.

`MD5` produces a 128-bit digest with no salt and no iteration count. A dump of the `Users` table yields hashes that can be reversed against published rainbow tables or cracked with a GPU at several billion candidates per second. The login route at `routes/login.ts:34` compares the submitted `md5(password)` directly against the stored hash, confirming the algorithm is load-bearing.

**Relevant findings**

- 🟠 [F-011](#f-011) — Hardcoded RSA private key enables offline JWT forgery
- 🟠 [F-029](#f-029) — Hardcoded BIP39 mnemonic enables forged challenge
- 🟠 [F-030](#f-030) — Hardcoded testing credentials in production bundle

<a id="secret-and-key-management"></a>
#### 6.9.2 Secret and Key Management

**Status:** 🔴 Unsafe - Unsafe - publicly known hardcoded keys; any deployment using these keys is immediately compromisable.

`lib/insecurity.ts:23`,44; `routes/checkKeys.ts:10`

**Security assessment**

Three signing and authentication secrets are hardcoded as string literals:

- `lib/insecurity.ts:23` embeds the full RSA private key used to sign all session JWTs. The same key is committed to the public repository at `https://github.com/juice-shop/juice-shop` - any caller can clone the repository, read the key, and sign arbitrary tokens the server accepts as valid.
- `lib/insecurity.ts:44` hardcodes the HMAC key used to hash security-question answers in `routes/resetPassword.ts`. An attacker who reads this key can precompute answer hashes for common questions offline and reverse stored hashes from a database dump.
- `routes/checkKeys.ts:10` contains a BIP-39 mnemonic as a hardcoded string used for the Web3 challenge.

**Relevant findings**

- 🟠 [F-011](#f-011) — Hardcoded RSA private key enables offline JWT forgery
- 🟠 [F-029](#f-029) — Hardcoded BIP39 mnemonic enables forged challenge
- 🟠 [F-030](#f-030) — Hardcoded testing credentials in production bundle

<a id="transport-encryption"></a><a id="transport-encryption-tls"></a>
#### 6.9.3 Transport Encryption

**Status:** 🟡 Partial - HTTPS is supported but not enforced; the server binds an HTTP listener with no redirect, exposing session tokens and credentials to interception in transit.

Transport encryption protects sensitive data - credentials, session tokens, and payment details - from interception between the browser and server. Juice Shop can be started with TLS certificates for deployments that require encrypted transport.

**Security assessment**

`server.ts:131` initializes an HTTPS listener when `process.env.CERT` and `process.env.KEY` are set. The server also binds an HTTP listener on the standard port with no redirect to HTTPS. Users who access the application over HTTP transmit their login credentials and `Authorization` bearer tokens in cleartext. No `Strict-Transport-Security` header is set in any observed response path, so browsers do not automatically upgrade connections.

**Relevant findings**

- 🟠 [F-011](#f-011) — Hardcoded RSA private key enables offline JWT forgery
- 🟠 [F-029](#f-029) — Hardcoded BIP39 mnemonic enables forged challenge
- 🟠 [F-030](#f-030) — Hardcoded testing credentials in production bundle

### 6.10 File Parser and Outbound Request Controls

<a id="ctrl-file-parser-and-outbound-request-controls"></a>

_No trust boundary in this model depends on this control class._


**Systemic weaknesses:** [W-001](#w-001), [W-005](#w-005)
**Verdict:** 🟠 Weak

<!-- The line below is mechanically derived from the controls table — LLM must not re-author it. -->
**Controls covered:**

- [6.10.1 File Upload Validation](#6101-file-upload-validation)

**Implemented controls:** `routes/fileUpload.ts:19`; `server.ts:309`.

**Assessment:** `routes/fileUpload.ts:19` uses `multer` with a file-size limit as the primary upload guard. Three distinct parsing vulnerabilities exist downstream: XXE from `libxmljs2` with `noent: true`, zip-slip from unvalidated archive entry paths, and YAML bomb from an unbounded expansion via `js-yaml`. The size limit does not prevent any of these because the attacks operate within normal file sizes.

<a id="file-upload-validation"></a>
#### 6.10.1 File Upload Validation

**Status:** 🟡 Partial - file-size limit via `multer` is applied but MIME type and archive content are not validated before being handed to XML, zip, and YAML parsers.

`routes/fileUpload.ts:19`; `server.ts:309`

**Security assessment**

`routes/fileUpload.ts:19` limits upload size to 50 MB via `multer`. Three dangerous downstream configurations exist:

- `routes/fileUpload.ts:83` calls `libxml.parseXml(fileContents, { noent: true })`, enabling external entity resolution - a crafted DTD can read arbitrary server files such as `/etc/passwd` or environment files.
- `routes/fileUpload.ts:44` extracts ZIP entries without resolving the entry path against the target directory; a `../../cron.d/payload` entry writes to an arbitrary filesystem path.
- `routes/fileUpload.ts:117` parses YAML with `js-yaml` at default settings, allowing a billion-laughs anchor expansion that exhausts Node\.js event-loop CPU.

**Relevant findings**

- 🔴 [F-006](#f-006) — Server-Side Code Execution
- 🔴 [F-007](#f-007) — XXE
- 🟠 [F-022](#f-022) — Path traversal filesystem access from request input

### 6.11 Operations Runtime and Supply Chain Controls


**Systemic weaknesses:** [W-008](#w-008)
**Verdict:** 🔴 Missing

<!-- The line below is mechanically derived from the controls table — LLM must not re-author it. -->
**Controls covered:**

- [6.11.1 Third-Party CI/CD Action Pinning](#6111-third-party-cicd-action-pinning)
- [6.11.2 Automated SCA scanning](#6112-automated-sca-scanning)
- [6.11.3 Automated dependency updates](#6113-automated-dependency-updates)
- [6.11.4 Lockfile hygiene](#6114-lockfile-hygiene)

**Implemented controls:** `.github/workflows/ci.yml:202`.

**Assessment:** `.github/workflows/ci.yml:202` pins one action to a full commit SHA - the single positive supply-chain control. All other third-party actions reference mutable version tags. `package-lock.json` is excluded from the repository via `.gitignore:10`, making installs non-reproducible and blocking `npm audit`. No automated dependency-scanning step exists in any workflow file, and no Dependabot or Renovate configuration is present.

<a id="third-party-cicd-action-pinning"></a>
#### 6.11.1 Third-Party CI/CD Action Pinning

**Status:** 🟡 Partial - one action is pinned to a full SHA in `ci.yml:202`; all other third-party actions reference mutable version tags and can be silently replaced.

Pinning CI/CD workflow actions to full commit SHAs prevents a compromised or updated action from injecting malicious code into the build pipeline. An action pinned by SHA always refers to the same immutable code regardless of tag movements or repository takeovers.

**Security assessment**

`.github/workflows/ci.yml:202` pins one action to a commit SHA - the only adequate pin in the pipeline. `.github/workflows/codeql-analysis.yml:23` references `github/codeql-action` and `coverallsapp/github-action` by version tags such as `v2`, which are mutable pointers. A supply-chain compromise of either action repository would silently replace the code executed against this repository's source and pipeline secrets. The `GITHUB_TOKEN` permission scope is not restricted at the workflow level (no `permissions:` block at root of `ci.yml`), granting write access to repository contents and packages by default.

**Relevant findings**

- 🟡 [F-002](#f-002) — Missing Security Event Logging
- 🟠 [F-025](#f-025) — CI/CD Workflow Supply-Chain Risk
- 🟠 [F-034](#f-034) — Package Install Scripts Run as Root

<a id="automated-sca-scanning"></a>
#### 6.11.2 Automated SCA scanning

**Status:** 🔴 Missing - no software composition analysis step exists in any workflow file; vulnerable dependencies cannot be detected or blocked at merge time.

Automated SCA scanning flags vulnerable direct and transitive dependencies in CI before they reach production. An SCA step in the pull-request workflow allows developers to identify and remediate vulnerable packages as part of the normal development flow.

**Security assessment**

No `npm audit`, `snyk test`, `trivy`, or equivalent SCA step appears in any workflow file under `.github/workflows/`. The absence of `package-lock.json` (excluded in `.gitignore:10`) also prevents `npm audit` from resolving the full transitive dependency tree when run manually. Known-vulnerable transitive dependencies cannot be detected or blocked at merge time, and there is no mechanism to alert when a CVE is published against an already-installed version.

**Relevant findings**

- 🟡 [F-002](#f-002) — Missing Security Event Logging
- 🟠 [F-025](#f-025) — CI/CD Workflow Supply-Chain Risk
- 🟠 [F-034](#f-034) — Package Install Scripts Run as Root

<a id="automated-dependency-updates"></a>
#### 6.11.3 Automated dependency updates

**Status:** 🔴 Missing - no Dependabot or Renovate configuration exists; dependencies are updated only through manual intervention.

Automated dependency update tools open pull requests when upstream packages release security fixes, keeping the dependency tree current without manual tracking. This narrows the window between a vulnerability disclosure and a patch landing in the codebase.

**Security assessment**

Neither a `.github/dependabot.yml` configuration nor a `renovate.json` file exists in the repository. `package.json` uses semver ranges rather than pinned versions for several dependencies, meaning `npm install` can resolve different versions at different times. Without a lockfile or automated update tooling, a newly disclosed CVE in a dependency goes unnoticed until a developer manually runs `npm audit` or observes a downstream incident.

**Relevant findings**

- 🟡 [F-002](#f-002) — Missing Security Event Logging
- 🟠 [F-025](#f-025) — CI/CD Workflow Supply-Chain Risk
- 🟠 [F-034](#f-034) — Package Install Scripts Run as Root

<a id="lockfile-hygiene"></a>
#### 6.11.4 Lockfile hygiene

**Status:** 🔴 Missing - `package-lock.json` is excluded by `.gitignore:10`, making installs non-reproducible and preventing `npm audit` from resolving the full dependency graph.

A committed lockfile guarantees that every developer install and CI run resolves the exact same dependency tree. It also enables `npm audit` to evaluate specific resolved versions against the vulnerability database rather than inferring risk from semver ranges.

**Security assessment**

`.gitignore:10` explicitly excludes `package-lock.json`. CI runs `npm install` rather than `npm ci`, so each build may resolve a later patch or minor release than the previous one. A dependency update that introduces a vulnerability is invisible until someone generates a fresh lockfile and audits it. `Dockerfile:1` references an unpinned `node` base image tag, and `test/smoke/Dockerfile:1` does the same - extending non-determinism to the container build layer.

**Relevant findings**

- 🟡 [F-002](#f-002) — Missing Security Event Logging
- 🟠 [F-025](#f-025) — CI/CD Workflow Supply-Chain Risk
- 🟠 [F-034](#f-034) — Package Install Scripts Run as Root

### 6.12 Real-time and Not Applicable Controls

**Verdict:** 🔴 Missing

<!-- The line below is mechanically derived from the controls table — LLM must not re-author it. -->
**Controls covered:**

- [6.12.1 LLM Prompt Injection Defence](#6121-llm-prompt-injection-defence)

**Implemented controls:** `routes/chat.ts:106`,201,205.

**Assessment:** `routes/chat.ts` integrates an LLM-backed chatbot that receives user messages, assembles an order-data context, and returns model-generated responses. No system-prompt boundary, input filter, or output validation guards against prompt injection, and the model context includes PII from the caller's order history.

<a id="llm-prompt-injection-defence"></a>
#### 6.12.1 LLM Prompt Injection Defence

**Status:** 🔴 Missing - no input sanitization, output validation, or prompt-boundary guard is applied in `routes/chat.ts` before user messages reach the LLM.

`routes/chat.ts:106`,201,205

**Security assessment**

`routes/chat.ts:106` forwards the caller's message directly to the LLM API without stripping or escaping instruction-like tokens. `routes/chat.ts:177` interprets the LLM's response as a coupon-discount command without server-side validation of the resulting discount value - a crafted user message that injects a discount instruction into the model's output can grant arbitrary discounts on any order. `routes/chat.ts:164` populates the LLM context with the caller's full order history including email addresses; a secondary prompt-injection attack can coerce the model to repeat that PII to the caller or to a third party embedded in a product description.

**Relevant findings**

- No dedicated finding routed in this assessment.

### 6.13 Defense-in-Depth Summary

**Verdict:** -

Individual controls in place include TOTP second-factor authentication via `routes/2fa.ts` (the only adequately-implemented authentication mechanism in the catalog), Sequelize ORM parameterized finders on the majority of non-injection-path data routes, the `express-jwt` middleware protecting routes in the `server.ts:401-420` block, and a single SHA-pinned GitHub Actions step at `ci.yml:202`.

Restoring layered defense requires four systemic repairs that address the control boundaries where multiple attack paths converge. Parameterizing the queries at `routes/login.ts:34` and `routes/search.ts:23` would close the two SQL injection entry points that currently chain with `MD5` password storage into immediate account takeover. Loading the RSA signing key at `lib/insecurity.ts:23` from a runtime environment variable or secret store - rather than source code - would invalidate the JWT forgery path that depends on public repository access. Adding an `algorithms: ['RS256']` allowlist to the `expressjwt()` call at `lib/insecurity.ts:54` would prevent algorithm-confusion forgery even if the public key remains observable. Moving JWT storage from `localStorage` to an `HttpOnly` cookie would sever the XSS-to-session-hijack chain that currently flows from any of the four `bypassSecurityTrustHtml()` bypass sites through stored-XSS to token exfiltration.

<a id="w-001"></a>
### W-001 — Application data crosses into executable code

🔴 **Critical** · implementation weakness · confirmed · 1 finding

An interpreter evaluates application values as code. The affected path mixes data processing with executable program construction instead of constraining callers to predefined operations.

**Confirmed findings:**

- 🔴 [F-006](#f-006) — Server-Side Code Execution (`routes/userProfile.ts:61`)

**Affected components:** [C-02](#c-02)

**Remediation:**

- **Structural** — replace evaluation of application values with a data parser or a fixed set of operations whose arguments remain data
- **Tactical** — ● [M-021](#m-021) — Remove server-side evaluation of untrusted input

<a id="w-002"></a>
### W-002 — Database access relies on concatenated queries

🔴 **Critical** · design weakness · confirmed · 2 findings

Database queries are assembled from application values instead of passing those values through an enforced parameterised data-access path. This leaves every call site responsible for preserving query structure.

**Architectural anti-pattern - Raw SQL string interpolation.** Multiple routes build SQL queries by concatenating request values rather than using parameterized queries, so any new route that follows the same pattern inherits injection risk without a per-route code review.

**Confirmed findings:**

- 🔴 [F-003](#f-003) — SQL injection (`routes/login.ts:34`)
- 🔴 [F-005](#f-005) — SQL Injection in Product Search (`routes/search.ts:23`)

**Practice sites:**

- `routes/login.ts:34`
- `routes/search.ts:23`

**Architecture evidence:** Parameterized Queries, ORM / Repository Layer, Source evidence (`routes/vulnCodeSnippet.ts:46`)

**Affected components:** [C-01](#c-01), [C-02](#c-02)

**Remediation:**

- **Structural** — provide one parameterised repository or query-builder path and prohibit application-value interpolation in database queries
- **Tactical** — ● [M-018](#m-018) — Use parameterized database queries, ● [M-020](#m-020) — Use parameterized database queries

<a id="w-003"></a>
### W-003 — Authorization is implemented route by route

🔴 **Critical** · design weakness · confirmed · 6 findings

Authorization depends on per-handler checks instead of a policy boundary that consistently enforces role, ownership, and tenant scope. New routes can bypass protection by omitting a local check.

**Architectural anti-pattern - Missing server-side authorization layer.** Authorization is implemented individually on selected routes rather than enforced by a shared middleware layer, so new routes and routes with missing annotations are unauthenticated and unprivileged by default.

**Confirmed findings:**

- 🔴 [F-004](#f-004) — Insecure Direct Object Reference
- 🟠 [F-037](#f-037) — Unauthenticated access to all order records (`routes/trackOrder.ts:18`)
- 🟠 [F-045](#f-045) — Sensitive Routes Registered Without Authentication Middleware
- 🟠 [F-046](#f-046) — Missing permissions block default GITHUB_TOKEN scope
- 🟠 [F-047](#f-047) — Free Deluxe Membership (`routes/deluxe.ts:24`)
- 🟠 [F-048](#f-048) — IDOR: review update without ownership check (`routes/updateProductReviews.ts:16`)

**Architecture evidence:** Centralised AuthZ Policy, Role / Scope Enforcement, Ownership Check, Source evidence (`server.ts:370`), Source evidence (`server.ts:388`), Source evidence (`server.ts:389`) (+66 more)

**Affected components:** [C-08](#c-08), [C-02](#c-02)

**Remediation:**

- **Structural** — enforce authorization through a shared server-side policy layer and make ownership and tenant scope mandatory inputs to data access
- **Tactical** — ● [M-019](#m-019) — Enforce object-level (ownership) authorization, ◕ [M-050](#m-050) — Enforce server-side authorization on every endpoint, ◑ [M-006](#m-006) — Enforce server-side authorization on every endpoint, ◕ [M-058](#m-058) — Enforce server-side authorization on every endpoint, ◕ [M-059](#m-059) — Enforce server-side authorization on every endpoint, ◕ [M-060](#m-060) — Enforce server-side authorization on every endpoint, ◑ [M-007](#m-007) — Enforce object-level (ownership) authorization, ◕ [M-061](#m-061) — Enforce object-level (ownership) authorization

<a id="w-004"></a>
### W-004 — Endpoints are reachable without enforced authentication

🟠 **High** · design weakness · confirmed · 2 findings

Sensitive API routes and real-time channels are exposed without an enforced authentication check at the endpoint boundary. Access control depends on each handler (or the caller) remembering to require a session, so an unauthenticated client can reach privileged operations directly.

**Confirmed findings:**

- 🟠 [F-016](#f-016) — Unauthenticated WebSocket Channel
- 🟠 [F-049](#f-049) — Missing authentication on key submission endpoint (`routes/checkKeys.ts:6`)

**Architecture evidence:** Route Authentication Middleware, Server-Side Session Enforcement, Source evidence (`server.ts:309`), Source evidence (`server.ts:312`), Source evidence (`server.ts:401`), Source evidence (`server.ts:403`) (+4 more)

**Affected components:** [C-06](#c-06), [C-07](#c-07)

**Remediation:**

- **Structural** — enforce authentication centrally at the routing and channel boundary so every exposed endpoint requires a verified session unless explicitly marked public
- **Tactical** — ◕ [M-031](#m-031) — Require authentication on every exposed endpoint, ◕ [M-062](#m-062) — Require authentication on every exposed endpoint

<a id="w-005"></a>
### W-005 — Input handling lacks enforced boundary validation

🟠 **High** · design weakness · confirmed · 2 findings

Request handlers do not validate input against one enforced server-side schema or allowlist at the boundary - validation is either absent on the vulnerable parameters or limited to rejecting selected bad patterns (a blacklist). New encodings and unanticipated input forms can therefore reach downstream parsers or interpreters.

**Confirmed findings:**

- 🟠 [F-022](#f-022) — Path traversal filesystem access from request input (`routes/dataErasure.ts:104`)
- 🟠 [F-026](#f-026) — ZIP Slip Path Traversal in File Upload (`routes/fileUpload.ts:44`)

**Architecture evidence:** Schema Validation, Allowlist Validation, Source evidence (`server.ts:204`), Source evidence (`server.ts:246`), Source evidence (`server.ts:410`), Source evidence (`server.ts:411`) (+4 more)

**Affected components:** [C-02](#c-02)

**Remediation:**

- **Structural** — enforce server-side schemas and domain-specific allowlists before input reaches parsing, persistence, or command construction
- **Tactical** — ◑ [M-003](#m-003) — Constrain file paths to a safe base directory, ◕ [M-037](#m-037) — Constrain file paths to a safe base directory, ◕ [M-040](#m-040) — Constrain file paths to a safe base directory

<a id="w-006"></a>
### W-006 — Document-database queries take their structure from application values

🟠 **High** · implementation weakness · confirmed · 1 finding

Document-store queries take operators or JavaScript predicates from application values instead of fixed query structures with typed values. Callers can change what a query selects, for example through injected operators or evaluated expressions.

**Confirmed findings:**

- 🟠 [F-001](#f-001) — NoSQL Injection in MarsDB Query Layer

**Affected components:** [C-02](#c-02)

**Remediation:**

- **Structural** — build document queries from fixed operator structures, coerce request values to the expected scalar types, and disable server-side JavaScript evaluation in the database
- **Tactical** — ◕ [M-016](#m-016) — Use parameterized database queries

<a id="w-007"></a>
### W-007 — OAuth sign-in requests are not bound to the user's browser session

🟠 **High** · implementation weakness · confirmed · 1 finding

The client starts OAuth or OpenID Connect sign-in without a per-request state value that its callback verifies. A forged authorization response can then complete sign-in in the victim's browser with the attacker's grant.

**Confirmed findings:**

- 🟠 [F-009](#f-009) — OAuth CSRF (`login.component.ts:152`)

**Affected components:** [C-03](#c-03)

**Remediation:**

- **Structural** — start every sign-in through a maintained OAuth/OIDC client that generates and verifies state (and PKCE) for each authorization request
- **Tactical** — ◕ [M-024](#m-024) — Add anti-CSRF protection to state-changing requests

<a id="w-008"></a>
### W-008 — Build pipeline trusts mutable third-party references

🟠 **High** · design weakness · confirmed · 2 findings

CI/CD workflows resolve third-party actions and other build dependencies to mutable tags or branches instead of immutable commit digests, so a retagged or compromised upstream runs inside the pipeline with its token and secret scope.

**Confirmed findings:**

- 🟠 [F-025](#f-025) — CI/CD Workflow Supply-Chain Risk
- 🟡 [F-054](#f-054) — Npm install --unsafe-perm in Docker build stage

**Architecture evidence:** Commit-SHA Action Pinning, Dependency Provenance Verification, Source evidence (`.github/workflows/ci.yml:202`)

**Affected components:** [C-08](#c-08)

**Remediation:**

- **Structural** — pin every third-party action and build dependency to an immutable commit SHA (or a vetted internal mirror) and enforce SHA-pinning in CI
- **Tactical** — ◕ [M-039](#m-039) — Pin third-party dependencies to immutable versions, ◑ [M-067](#m-067) — Set least-privilege CI workflow permissions

<a id="w-009"></a>
### W-009 — Frontend rendering lacks enforced output encoding

🟠 **High** · design weakness · confirmed · 7 findings

Browser rendering paths write HTML or DOM content directly without one enforced contextual-encoding or sanitisation boundary. Safety depends on each individual component using the right API.

**Confirmed findings:**

- 🟠 [F-017](#f-017) — Stored XSS (`about.component.ts:119`)
- 🟠 [F-018](#f-018) — Stored XSS (`administration.component.ts:73`)
- 🟠 [F-019](#f-019) — Stored XSS (`administration.component.ts:91`)
- 🟠 [F-020](#f-020) — Stored XSS (`search-result.component.ts:110`)
- 🟠 [F-021](#f-021) — DOM XSS (`search-result.component.ts:143`)
- 🟡 [F-051](#f-051) — Stored XSS (`last-login-ip.component.ts:39`)

**Practice sites:**

- 🟡 [F-056](#f-056) — Challenge-gated weak sanitization stores XSS HTML in Users (`models/user.ts:50`) (`models/user.ts:50`)
- `frontend/src/app/about/about.component.ts:119`
- `frontend/src/app/administration/administration.component.ts:73`
- `frontend/src/app/administration/administration.component.ts:91`
- `frontend/src/app/data-export/data-export.component.ts:57`
- `frontend/src/app/last-login-ip/last-login-ip.component.ts:39`

**Architecture evidence:** Template Autoescape, Output Encoding, Content Security Policy, Source evidence (`frontend/src/app/about/about.component.ts:119`), Source evidence (`frontend/src/app/last-login-ip/last-login-ip.component.ts:39`), Source evidence (`frontend/src/app/administration/administration.component.ts:73`) (+5 more)

**Affected components:** [C-03](#c-03), [C-02](#c-02)

**Remediation:**

- **Structural** — use framework-safe rendering by default and isolate any required raw HTML behind one reviewed sanitisation and Trusted Types policy
- **Tactical** — ◕ [M-032](#m-032) — Encode output instead of bypassing the framework sanitizer, ◕ [M-033](#m-033) — Encode output instead of bypassing the framework sanitizer, ◕ [M-034](#m-034) — Encode output instead of bypassing the framework sanitizer, ◕ [M-035](#m-035) — Encode output instead of bypassing the framework sanitizer, ◕ [M-036](#m-036) — Encode output instead of bypassing the framework sanitizer, ◑ [M-064](#m-064) — Encode output instead of bypassing the framework sanitizer, ◑ [M-008](#m-008) — Encode output instead of bypassing the framework sanitizer, ◑ [M-069](#m-069) — Encode output instead of bypassing the framework sanitizer

<a id="w-010"></a>
### W-010 — Secrets are committed to source instead of a managed store

🟠 **High** · design weakness · confirmed · 4 findings

Cryptographic keys, credentials, and other high-entropy secrets are embedded as literals in source or config rather than resolved at runtime from a managed secret store, so anyone with repository read access obtains reusable signing material and credentials.

**Architectural anti-pattern - Secrets hardcoded in source.** RSA private keys, HMAC secrets, BIP39 mnemonics, and test credentials are committed to the source tree, meaning any actor with repository read access can forge tokens, recover security answers, and authenticate without a real account.

**Confirmed findings:**

- 🟠 [F-011](#f-011) — Hardcoded RSA private key enables offline JWT forgery (`lib/insecurity.ts:23`)
- 🟠 [F-029](#f-029) — Hardcoded BIP39 mnemonic enables forged challenge (`routes/checkKeys.ts:10`)
- 🟠 [F-030](#f-030) — Hardcoded testing credentials in production bundle (`login.component.ts:63`)
- 🟠 [F-032](#f-032) — Hardcoded HMAC key exposes security answer hashes to offline attack

**Architecture evidence:** Managed Secret Store, Runtime Secret Injection, Source evidence (`routes/checkKeys.ts:10`), Source evidence (`lib/insecurity.ts:23`)

**Affected components:** [C-03](#c-03), [C-01](#c-01), [C-07](#c-07)

**Remediation:**

- **Structural** — move every secret to a managed secret store or injected environment configuration, rotate the exposed values, and add secret-scanning to CI
- **Tactical** — ◕ [M-026](#m-026) — Move cryptographic keys to a managed secret store, ◕ [M-043](#m-043) — Move secrets to a managed secret store, ◕ [M-044](#m-044) — Move secrets to a managed secret store, ◕ [M-046](#m-046) — Move cryptographic keys to a managed secret store

<a id="w-011"></a>
### W-011 — Browser scripts retain reusable session credentials

🟠 **High** · implementation weakness · confirmed · 1 finding

Browser code retains reusable authentication credentials in script-readable storage. Script execution in the same origin can obtain the credentials and reuse the authenticated session.

**Architectural anti-pattern - SPA without BFF.** The Angular SPA stores session credentials in browser-accessible storage, where any of the six confirmed cross-site scripting instances can steal them; moving token custody to a server-side Backend-for-Frontend with an HttpOnly Secure SameSite cookie is the only structural fix.

**Confirmed findings:**

- 🟠 [F-031](#f-031) — JWT stored in localStorage (`login.component.ts:105`)

**Affected components:** [C-03](#c-03)

**Remediation:**

- **Structural** — move reusable credentials behind a server-managed session boundary and keep browser-visible state free of reusable authentication secrets
- **Tactical** — ◕ [M-045](#m-045) — Store session tokens in HttpOnly, Secure cookies

<a id="w-012"></a>
### W-012 — Application data crosses into template source

🟡 **Medium** · implementation weakness · observed-practice · 1 finding

Template construction treats application values as template source. Rendering can therefore interpret data as template instructions.

**Architectural anti-pattern - Server-side eval of untrusted input.** User-supplied profile data and template strings reach server-side evaluation functions, making remote code execution available to any authenticated attacker without requiring a memory-safety exploit.

**Practice sites:**

- 🟠 [F-024](#f-024) — Input compiled as template source (`routes/userProfile.ts:87`) (`routes/userProfile.ts:87`)

**Affected components:** [C-02](#c-02)

**Remediation:**

- **Structural** — compile fixed templates and pass application values only through their data-binding interface
- **Tactical** — ◑ [M-004](#m-004) — Remove server-side evaluation of untrusted input, ◕ [M-038](#m-038) — Remove server-side evaluation of untrusted input

<a id="w-013"></a>
### W-013 — Security-sensitive data uses weak cryptographic primitives

🟡 **Medium** · implementation weakness · observed-practice · 1 finding

Password, token, or integrity protection uses a weak hash, predictable random source, or insufficient work factor. The application may use a standard library, but the selected primitive does not provide the required security property.

**Practice sites:**

- 🟠 [F-033](#f-033) — MD5 password hashing without salt enables offline (`lib/insecurity.ts:43`) (`lib/insecurity.ts:43`)

**Affected components:** [C-01](#c-01)

**Remediation:**

- **Structural** — standardise on a password KDF, a CSPRNG for secrets, and modern authenticated cryptographic primitives with centrally reviewed parameters
- **Tactical** — ◕ [M-047](#m-047) — Hash passwords with a strong, salted algorithm

### 🔴 Critical (6)

<a id="t-003"></a><a id="f-003"></a>
#### F-003 · SQL injection (routes/login.ts:34)

**Severity:** 🔴 Critical  ·  **Component:** [C-01](#c-01) - Authentication & Session Surface  ·  **Location:** `routes/login.ts:34`

**Weakness:** [W-002](#w-002) - Database access relies on concatenated queries

**Trust boundary gap:** [tb-4](#tb-4) - express-api → sqlite-db · Query construction: tb-4's data-interpretation leg assumes Sequelize parameterized binding; the raw query at `routes/login.ts:34` directly violates that assumption by concatenating user input.

**Issue:** An attacker submits a crafted email value such as \"' OR '1'='1\" to POST `/rest/user/login`; the value is directly concatenated into a raw SQL string, causing the query to return the first user row and grant authentication as that user without a matching password. Unauthenticated remote attacker can log in as any user, including admin, by injecting SQL into the email field.

Full authentication bypass, read access to all user sessions, and potential data modification via stacked queries (SQLite-dependent) are in scope. Declared sensitive assets - JWT signing key, TOTP secrets, `MD5`-hashed passwords - are all accessible after session establishment.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `models.sequelize.query()` is called at `routes/login.ts:34` with a template literal that interpolates `req.body.email` and the `MD5` hash of `req.body.password` directly into the SQL string without parameterized binding or escaping.

```typescript
  31 │
  32 │   return (req: Request, res: Response, next: NextFunction) => {
  33 │     verifyPreLoginChallenges(req) // vuln-code-snippet hide-line
→ 34 │     models.sequelize.query(`SELECT * FROM Users WHERE email = '${req.body.email || ''}' AND password = '${security.hash(req.body.password || '')}' AND deletedAt IS NULL`, { model: UserModel, plain: true }) // vuln-code-snippet vuln-line loginAdminChallenge loginBenderChallenge loginJimChallenge
  35 │       .then((authenticatedUser) => { // vuln-code-snippet neutral-line loginAdminChallenge loginBenderChallenge loginJimChallenge
  36 │         const user = utils.queryResultToJson(authenticatedUser)
  37 │         if (user.data?.id && user.data.totpSecret !== '') {
```

**Fix:** Switch all SQL execution to parameterised queries or ORM-bound parameters → ● [M-018](#m-018) — Use parameterized database queries (`login.ts:34`)

**Classification:** Injection · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-89](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/89.html) · [OWASP A05:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A05_2025-Injection/) · walkthrough [Walkthrough §3.4](#34-sql-injection-in-login)

<a id="t-004"></a><a id="f-004"></a>
#### F-004 · Insecure Direct Object Reference

**Severity:** 🔴 Critical  ·  **Component:** [C-02](#c-02) - Express\.js REST API Backend  ·  **Location:** Multiple locations (22)

**Weakness:** [W-003](#w-003) - Authorization is implemented route by route

**Instances (22):** 🟠 `routes/address.ts:18`, 🔴 `routes/address.ts:11`, 🔴 `routes/address.ts:29`, 🟠 `routes/basketItems.ts:68`, 🔴 `routes/dataExport.ts:26`, 🟠 `routes/delivery.ts:34`, 🔴 `routes/deluxe.ts:25`, 🔴 `routes/deluxe.ts:30` … (+14 more)

**Issue:** A signed-in attacker puts another user's ID into the owner field of the request (`req.body.UserId` and the like); the query trusts it instead of the session identity, so the attacker reads or changes every other user's records - horizontal privilege escalation across all accounts.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

```typescript
routes/address.ts
  15 │
  16 │ export function getAddressById () {
  17 │   return async (req: Request, res: Response) => {
→ 18 │     const address = await AddressModel.findOne({ where: { id: req.params.id, UserId: req.body.UserId } })
  19 │     if (address != null) {
  20 │       res.status(200).json({ status: 'success', data: address })
  21 │     } else {
```

**Fix:** Tie every object lookup to the requesting user's identity and reject cross-tenant references → ● [M-019](#m-019) — Enforce object-level (ownership) authorization (`address.ts:18`)

**Classification:** Broken Access Control · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-639](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/639.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/) · walkthrough [Walkthrough §3.3](#33-insecure-direct-object-reference-in-address)

<a id="t-005"></a><a id="f-005"></a>
#### F-005 · SQL Injection in Product Search (routes/search.ts:23)

**Severity:** 🔴 Critical  ·  **Component:** [C-02](#c-02) - Express\.js REST API Backend  ·  **Location:** `routes/search.ts:23`

**Weakness:** [W-002](#w-002) - Database access relies on concatenated queries

**Trust boundary gap:** [tb-4](#tb-4) - express-api → sqlite-db · Query construction: Attacker-controlled SQL clause crosses the data-interpretation leg of the express-api to sqlite-db boundary.

**Issue:** An unauthenticated attacker sends GET `/rest/products/search?q=` with a UNION SELECT payload. The query parameter is truncated to 200 characters then interpolated directly into the SQL string.

A UNION attack can retrieve all rows from Users, exposing every email address and `MD5`-hashed password. The endpoint requires no authentication.

Full exfiltration of all user email addresses, `MD5`-hashed passwords, and roles. Combined with `MD5` weakness enables offline account compromise for all users.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `routes/search.ts:23` interpolates the criteria variable (from `req.query.q`, truncated to 200 chars) directly into the SQL string without parameterization. The recon signal AUTHZ-001 at `search.ts:23` confirms this is a source-auth Critical finding.

```typescript
  20 │   return (req: Request, res: Response, next: NextFunction) => {
  21 │     let criteria: any = req.query.q === 'undefined' ? '' : req.query.q ?? ''
  22 │     criteria = (criteria.length <= 200) ? criteria : criteria.substring(0, 200)
→ 23 │     models.sequelize.query(`SELECT * FROM Products WHERE ((name LIKE '%${criteria}%' OR description LIKE '%${criteria}%') AND deletedAt IS NULL) ORDER BY name`) // vuln-code-snippet vuln-line unionSqlInjectionChallenge dbSchemaChallenge
  24 │       .then(([products]: any) => {
  25 │         const dataString = JSON.stringify(products)
  26 │         if (challengeUtils.notSolved(challenges.unionSqlInjectionChallenge)) { // vuln-code-snippet hide-start
```

**Fix:** Switch all SQL execution to parameterised queries or ORM-bound parameters → ● [M-020](#m-020) — Use parameterized database queries (`search.ts:23`)

**Classification:** Injection · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-89](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/89.html) · [OWASP A05:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A05_2025-Injection/) · walkthrough [Walkthrough §3.5](#35-sql-injection-in-product-search)

<a id="t-007"></a><a id="f-007"></a>
#### F-007 · XXE (routes/fileUpload.ts:83)

**Severity:** 🔴 Critical  ·  **Component:** [C-02](#c-02) - Express\.js REST API Backend  ·  **Location:** `routes/fileUpload.ts:83`

**Issue:** An unauthenticated attacker uploads an XML file to POST `/file-upload` containing an external entity declaration referencing `file:///etc/passwd` or an internal HTTP endpoint. `libxml.parseXml` is called with `noent:true` which resolves external entities, including file:// and http:// URIs.

The resolved entity content is included in the error response body returned to the attacker. Attacker reads arbitrary local files (credentials, configuration, `/etc/passwd`) and can probe internal HTTP services via SSRF using the parser as a proxy.

The endpoint is unauthenticated, making this reachable without any credentials.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `routes/fileUpload.ts:83` calls `vm.runInContext('libxml.parseXml(data, { noblanks: true, noent: true, nocdata: true })', sandbox)`. The `noent:true` option enables external entity resolution. The `xxeFileDisclosureChallenge` at line 85 confirms this attack path is present.

```typescript
  80 │       try {
  81 │         const sandbox = { libxml, data }
  82 │         vm.createContext(sandbox)
→ 83 │         const xmlDoc = vm.runInContext('libxml.parseXml(data, { noblanks: true, noent: true, nocdata: true })', sandbox, { timeout: 2000 })
  84 │         const xmlString = xmlDoc.toString(false)
  85 │         challengeUtils.solveIf(challenges.xxeFileDisclosureChallenge, () => { return (utils.matchesEtcPasswdFile(xmlString) || utils.matchesSystemIniFile(xmlString)) })
  86 │         res.status(410)
```

**Fix:** Disable external entity resolution on every XML parser and reject DOCTYPE declarations → ● [M-022](#m-022) — Disable XML external entity (XXE) resolution (`fileUpload.ts:83`)

**Classification:** Insecure File Handling · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-611](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/611.html) · [OWASP A06:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A06_2025-Insecure_Design/) · walkthrough [Walkthrough §3.6](#36-xxe-in-file-upload)

<a id="t-008"></a><a id="f-008"></a>
#### F-008 · Mass Assignment Allows Admin Role Injection (routes/verify.ts:52)

**Severity:** 🔴 Critical - reaches a privileged operation on an unauthenticated endpoint; verified attack-chain keystone in AC-T-002 (Bulk Data Exfiltration via Broken Object Authorization), AC-T-004 (Privilege Escalation via Mass-Assignment on Registration); see [§9](#9-abuse-cases)  ·  **Component:** [C-02](#c-02) - Express\.js REST API Backend  ·  **Location:** `routes/verify.ts:52`

**Trust boundary gap:** [tb-1](#tb-1) 🌐 External - external → express-api · Authorization: Mass assignment bypasses the authorization leg of the external trust boundary by injecting a privileged role field during account creation.

**Issue:** An unauthenticated attacker sends POST `/api/Users` with a JSON body containing `{"email": "attacker@x.com", "password": "pass", "passwordRepeat": "pass", "role": "admin"}`. The Finale-generated REST handler passes all body fields to the Sequelize model without stripping the role field.

The user is created with `role=admin`. The attacker then logs in and receives an admin-level JWT, gaining full administrative access to the application.

Any unauthenticated user can self-register with admin role, bypassing all access control for admin-only endpoints including user management, challenge administration, and financial operations.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `routes/verify.ts:52`: `challengeUtils.solveIf(challenges.registerAdminChallenge, () => { return req.body && req.body.role === security.roles.admin })` confirms the POST `/api/Users` endpoint accepts and processes a role field in the request body. `server.ts:362-365` only adds `denyAll` to PUT and DELETE for `/api/Users/:id`, leaving POST unrestricted for the role field.

```typescript
  49 │
  50 │ export const registerAdminChallenge = () => (req: Request, res: Response, next: NextFunction) => {
  51 │   challengeUtils.solveIf(challenges.registerAdminChallenge, () => {
→ 52 │     return req.body && req.body.role === security.roles.admin
  53 │   })
  54 │   next()
  55 │ }
```

**Fix:** ● [M-023](#m-023) — Allowlist client-controlled fields (`verify.ts:52`)

**Classification:** Broken Access Control · STRIDE: Elevation of Privilege · [CWE-915](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/915.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/) · walkthrough [Walkthrough §3.1](#31-mass-assignment-allows-admin-role-injection-in-express-js-rest-api-backend)

<a id="t-006"></a><a id="f-006"></a>
#### F-006 · Server-Side Code Execution (routes/userProfile.ts:61)

**Severity:** 🔴 Critical - verified attack-chain keystone in AC-T-006 (Remote Code Execution via Server-Side Injection); see [§9](#9-abuse-cases)  ·  **Component:** [C-02](#c-02) - Express\.js REST API Backend  ·  **Location:** `routes/userProfile.ts:61`

**Weakness:** [W-001](#w-001) - Application data crosses into executable code

**Issue:** An authenticated attacker sets their username to a string matching the pattern #{expression} via the profile update endpoint. When GET `/profile` is subsequently requested, `routes/userProfile.ts` extracts the expression from between the delimiters and passes it to `eval()` as server-side JavaScript.

Any Node\.js-accessible operation is executable, including file system access, environment variable exfiltration, and network requests. Remote code execution as the Node\.js process; attacker can exfiltrate all environment variables, database contents, and private keys, or pivot to internal network services.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `routes/userProfile.ts:54` matches username against /#{(.*)}/ and `routes/userProfile.ts:61` calls `eval(code)` where code is the captured group. The condition checks `utils.isChallengeEnabled(challenges.usernameXssChallenge)` but the challenge is enabled by default.

```typescript
  58 │         if (!code) {
  59 │           throw new Error('Username is null')
  60 │         }
→ 61 │         username = eval(code) // eslint-disable-line no-eval
  62 │       } catch (err) {
  63 │         username = '\\' + username
  64 │       }
```

**Fix:** Replace runtime code generation (eval/Function/template render) with a data-only execution path → ● [M-021](#m-021) — Remove server-side evaluation of untrusted input (`userProfile.ts:61`)

**Classification:** Code Execution via Unsafe Deserialization or Eval · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-94](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/94.html) · [OWASP A08:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A08_2025-Software_or_Data_Integrity_Failures/) · walkthrough [Walkthrough §3.2](#32-server-side-code-execution-in-user-profile)

### 🟠 High (42)

<a id="t-011"></a><a id="f-011"></a>
#### F-011 · Hardcoded RSA private key enables offline JWT forgery (lib/insecurity.ts:23)

**Severity:** 🟠 High - secret committed to the public source repo - extractable on clone, no prior access needed; elevated to Critical as a verified attack-chain keystone in AC-T-005 (Authentication Bypass via Exposed Secret Material); see [§9](#9-abuse-cases) _(raw Critical)_  ·  **Component:** [C-01](#c-01) - Authentication & Session Surface  ·  **Location:** `lib/insecurity.ts:23`

**Weakness:** [W-010](#w-010) - Secrets are committed to source instead of a managed store

**Issue:** An attacker reads the hardcoded RSA private key literal, signs a JWT payload claiming any user identity with `RS256`, and uses that token to authenticate as any user including admin without requiring the original credential. Permanent, offline authentication bypass for every user and role in the application.

Any actor who clones the repository can forge tokens granting admin privileges, access all user accounts, manipulate orders, and read all sensitive data - the key cannot be invalidated without a code change and deployment.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - The complete RSA private key is assigned as a string literal at `lib/insecurity.ts:23`; `authorize()` at line 56 signs all tokens with this key, and the key is never loaded from external configuration or a secret store.

**Fix:** Move the cryptographic key out of source control into a managed secret store and rotate it → ◕ [M-026](#m-026) — Move cryptographic keys to a managed secret store (`insecurity.ts:23`)

**Classification:** Cryptographic Failures · STRIDE: Spoofing · [CWE-321](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/321.html) · [OWASP A04:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A04_2025-Cryptographic_Failures/) · walkthrough [Walkthrough §3.7](#37-hardcoded-rsa-private-key-enables-offline-jwt-forgery)

<a id="t-029"></a><a id="f-029"></a>
#### F-029 · Hardcoded BIP39 mnemonic enables forged challenge (routes/checkKeys.ts:10)

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-07](#c-07) - Web3 / Wallet / NFT Surface  ·  **Location:** `routes/checkKeys.ts:10`

**Weakness:** [W-010](#w-010) - Secrets are committed to source instead of a managed store

**Issue:** An attacker reads the publicly committed mnemonic 'purpose betray marriage blame crunch monitor spin slide donate sport lift clutch', derives the Ethereum private key using any BIP39/HDNode library, and submits that key to POST `/rest/web3/submitKey`. The comparison at line 18 succeeds, marking the `nftUnlockChallenge` as solved without any legitimate key discovery.

Any reader of the source repository can immediately derive the private key and forge a successful challenge submission, undermining the integrity of the NFT unlock challenge. The wallet's associated Ethereum address and all funds it holds are also at risk.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - Line 10 embeds the full BIP39 mnemonic as a string literal committed to the repository. Lines 11-13 derive `privateKey`, `publicKey`, and address from it deterministically. Line 18 compares `req.body.privateKey` directly against this derived `privateKey`.

```typescript
   8 │     try {
   9 │       const { HDNodeWallet } = await import('ethers')
→ 10 │       const mnemonic = 'purpose betray marriage blame crunch monitor spin slide donate sport lift clutch'
  11 │       const mnemonicWallet = HDNodeWallet.fromPhrase(mnemonic)
  12 │       const privateKey = mnemonicWallet.privateKey
```

**Fix:** Move the credential out of source control into a secret store and rotate it → ◕ [M-043](#m-043) — Move secrets to a managed secret store (`checkKeys.ts:10`)

**Classification:** Cryptographic Failures · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-798](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/798.html) · [OWASP A04:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A04_2025-Cryptographic_Failures/)

<a id="t-030"></a><a id="f-030"></a>
#### F-030 · Hardcoded testing credentials in production bundle (login.component.ts:63)

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Angular Web Application (SPA)  ·  **Location:** `frontend/src/app/login/login.component.ts:63`

**Weakness:** [W-010](#w-010) - Secrets are committed to source instead of a managed store

**Issue:** An attacker downloads the compiled Angular JavaScript bundle, searches for `testingUsername` or `testingPassword` literals, recovers the credentials 'testing@juice-`sh.op`' / 'IamU**** (17 chars)', and authenticates to the application using those credentials. Any user who inspects the downloaded JavaScript bundle obtains working credentials for a real application account; this account can be used to browse, purchase, submit feedback, and probe authenticated API endpoints.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - LoginComponent class properties at `login.component.ts:63-64` declare `testingUsername` = `'testing@juice-``sh.op``'` and `testingPassword` = `'IamU**** (17 chars)'` as public string literals, which are compiled verbatim into the production JavaScript bundle served to all clients.

```typescript
  61 │   public oauthUnavailable = true
  62 │   public redirectUri = ''
→ 63 │   public testingUsername = 'testing@juice-sh.op'
  64 │   public testingPassword = 'IamU**** (17 chars)'
  65 │
```

**Fix:** Move the credential out of source control into a secret store and rotate it → ◕ [M-044](#m-044) — Move secrets to a managed secret store (`login.component.ts:63`)

**Classification:** Cryptographic Failures · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-798](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/798.html) · [OWASP A04:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A04_2025-Cryptographic_Failures/)

<a id="t-032"></a><a id="f-032"></a>
#### F-032 · Hardcoded HMAC key exposes security answer hashes to offline attack

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-01](#c-01) - Authentication & Session Surface  ·  **Location:** Multiple locations (2)

**Weakness:** [W-010](#w-010) - Secrets are committed to source instead of a managed store

**Instances (2):** 🟠 `lib/insecurity.ts:44`, 🟡 `models/securityAnswer.ts:46`

**Issue:** An attacker reads the hardcoded HMAC-SHA256 key 'pa4qacea4VK9t9nGv7yZtwmj', pre-computes HMAC values for a dictionary of common security answers offline, and compares them against the SecurityAnswers table hashes obtained via the SQL injection at `routes/login.ts:34`, enabling password reset on targeted accounts without guessing the answer through the API. An attacker who knows the HMAC key can pre-compute the hash for any candidate answer without rate limiting.

Combined with the SQL injection finding, all user security answers become recoverable offline. Every account using the password-reset flow is susceptible to takeover.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `lib/insecurity.ts:44` exports hmac = (data) => `crypto.createHmac('sha256', 'pa4qacea4VK9t9nGv7yZtwmj').update(data).digest('hex')`; the literal key is hardcoded. `resetPassword.ts:41` uses `security.hmac(answer)` === `data.answer` for authentication, making all stored security answers offline-crackable once the key is known.

**Fix:** Move the cryptographic key out of source control into a managed secret store and rotate it → ◕ [M-046](#m-046) — Move cryptographic keys to a managed secret store (`insecurity.ts:44`)

**Classification:** Cryptographic Failures · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-321](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/321.html) · [OWASP A04:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A04_2025-Cryptographic_Failures/)

<a id="t-039"></a><a id="f-039"></a>
#### F-039 · Payment card number stored as plaintext integer in Cards (models/card.ts:39)

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-04](#c-04) - SQLite Database (Sequelize ORM)  ·  **Location:** `models/card.ts:39`

**Issue:** The `cardNum` field is declared as `DataTypes.INTEGER` with range validation only. Card numbers are persisted in the Cards table without any encryption, tokenisation, or masking.

An attacker who reads the Cards table - via the SQL injection at `routes/login.ts:34`, via a backup exfiltration of data/juiceshop.sqlite, or via direct file-system access - obtains complete card numbers for all stored cards. Attacker obtains complete payment card numbers and expiry data for all users; this data enables card-not-present fraud against real-world payment processors if any production user submits actual card data.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `models/card.ts:39` declares `cardNum`: { type: `DataTypes.INTEGER`, validate: { `isInt: true`, min: ..., max: ... } } with no encryption setter; the column is written to and read from SQLite as a plain integer.

```typescript
  37 │       },
  38 │       fullName: DataTypes.STRING,
→ 39 │       cardNum: {
  40 │         type: DataTypes.INTEGER,
  41 │         validate: {
```

**Fix:** ◑ [M-052](#m-052) — Stop storing sensitive data in cleartext (`card.ts:39`)

**Classification:** Cryptographic Failures · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-312](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/312.html) · [OWASP A04:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A04_2025-Cryptographic_Failures/)

<a id="t-001"></a><a id="f-001"></a>
#### F-001 · NoSQL Injection in MarsDB Query Layer

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-02](#c-02) - Express\.js REST API Backend  ·  **Location:** Multiple locations (3)

**Weakness:** [W-006](#w-006) - Document-database queries take their structure from application values

**Trust boundary gap:** [tb-5](#tb-5) - express-api → marsdb-store · Query construction: Attacker-controlled JavaScript in \`$where` crosses the data-interpretation leg of the express-api to marsdb-store boundary.

**Instances (3):** `routes/showProductReviews.ts:36`, `routes/trackOrder.ts:18`, `routes/updateProductReviews.ts:18`

**Issue:** An attacker sends GET `/rest/products/:id/reviews` with a JavaScript expression in the :id parameter. When the `noSqlCommandChallenge` is enabled (the default), the id is only truncated to 40 characters and concatenated into a MarsDB \`$where` predicate.

An attacker can inject JavaScript to exfiltrate review documents or invoke the globally defined sleep function to block the Node\.js event loop. Attacker can read any review document regardless of product association, enumerate stored user emails from reviews, or inject `sleep(2000)` to block the synchronous event loop and deny service.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `routes/showProductReviews.ts:36` executes `db.reviewsCollection.find({ $where: 'this.product == ' + id })`. When `isChallengeEnabled` returns true (line 31) the id is `utils.trunc(req.params.id, 40)`, not a Number, enabling string-concatenated JavaScript injection.

```typescript
routes/showProductReviews.ts
  34 │     const t0 = new Date().getTime()
  35 │
→ 36 │     db.reviewsCollection.find({ $where: 'this.product == ' + id }).then((reviews: Review[]) => {
  37 │       const t1 = new Date().getTime()
  38 │       challengeUtils.solveIf(challenges.noSqlCommandChallenge, () => { return (t1 - t0) > 2000 })
```

**Fix:** Replace string concatenation in query operators with parameter binding → ◕ [M-016](#m-016) — Use parameterized database queries (`showProductReviews.ts:36`)

**Classification:** Injection · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-943](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/943.html) · [OWASP A05:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A05_2025-Injection/)

<a id="t-010"></a><a id="f-010"></a>
#### F-010 · Predictable password derived from Google OAuth email (oauth.component.ts:30)

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Angular Web Application (SPA)  ·  **Location:** `frontend/src/app/oauth/oauth.component.ts:30`

**Issue:** An attacker who knows a Google OAuth user's email address computes `btoa(email.split('').reverse().join(''))` - the exact formula at line 30 - and authenticates directly against `/rest/user/login` with that password, gaining full account access without going through Google. Any attacker with a victim's email address - frequently available from public profiles or data leaks - can compute the victim's exact password and log in to their Juice Shop account, accessing order history, personal data, and the ability to place purchases.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `oauth.component.ts:30` derives a password as `btoa(profile.email.split('').reverse().join(''))`; line 46 uses the identical formula to log in via the standard credential endpoint, meaning the derived password is the permanent account password and is reproducible by any party who knows the email address.

```typescript
  28 │     this.userService.oauthLogin(this.parseRedirectUrlParams().access_token).subscribe({
  29 │       next: (profile: any) => {
→ 30 │         const password = btoa(profile.email.split('').reverse().join(''))
  31 │         this.userService.save({ email: profile.email, password, passwordRepeat: password }).subscribe({
  32 │           next: () => {
```

**Fix:** ◕ [M-025](#m-025) — Replace email-derived password with server-generated random credential for OAuth accounts (`oauth.component.ts:30`)

**Classification:** OAuth / OIDC Misconfiguration · STRIDE: Spoofing · [CWE-1391](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/1391.html) · [OWASP A07:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A07_2025-Authentication_Failures/)

<a id="t-012"></a><a id="f-012"></a>
#### F-012 · Missing JWT algorithm whitelist

**Severity:** 🟠 High - verified attack-chain contributor in AC-T-005 (Authentication Bypass via Exposed Secret Material); see [§9](#9-abuse-cases) _(raw Critical)_  ·  **Component:** [C-01](#c-01) - Authentication & Session Surface  ·  **Location:** Multiple locations (5)

**Trust boundary gap:** [tb-1](#tb-1) 🌐 External - external → express-api · Authentication: tb-1's authentication leg relies on `expressJwt` at `lib/insecurity.ts:54`; the missing algorithms option undermines the boundary's authentication assumption.

**Instances (5):** `lib/insecurity.ts:54`, `lib/insecurity.ts:55`, `lib/insecurity.ts:58`, `lib/insecurity.ts:191`, `routes/verify.ts:119`

**Issue:** An attacker reads the RSA public key from the open-source repository, uses it as an HMAC-SHA256 secret to sign a forged JWT with `alg:HS256`, and presents the token to the `expressJwt` middleware which accepts it because no algorithms option is configured, granting authenticated session as any user. Complete authentication bypass: any attacker with read access to the public key can impersonate any user, including admin, across all JWT-protected API routes.

All authenticated operations - order placement, profile modification, admin panel access - become available without credentials.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `expressJwt` is called with only { secret: `publicKey` } and no algorithms option at line 54; jsonwebtoken accepts algorithm-downgrade tokens when the algorithms field is absent.

```typescript
lib/insecurity.ts
  52 │ }
  53 │
→ 54 │ export const isAuthorized = () => expressJwt(({ secret: publicKey }) as any)
  55 │ export const denyAll = () => expressJwt({ secret: '' + Math.random() } as any)
  56 │ export const authorize = (user = {}) => jwt.sign(user, privateKey, { expiresIn: '6h', algorithm: 'RS256' })
```

**Fix:** Pin the signature algorithm explicitly and reject `alg:none` and unknown algorithms → ◕ [M-027](#m-027) — Enforce JWT signature and algorithm verification (`insecurity.ts:54`)

**Classification:** Broken Authentication · STRIDE: Spoofing · [CWE-347](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/347.html) · [OWASP A07:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A07_2025-Authentication_Failures/)

**Classification:** Broken Authentication · STRIDE: Spoofing · [CWE-640](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/640.html) · [OWASP A07:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A07_2025-Authentication_Failures/)

<a id="t-014"></a><a id="f-014"></a>
#### F-014 · Rate Limit Bypass (server.ts:346)

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-02](#c-02) - Express\.js REST API Backend  ·  **Location:** `server.ts:346`

**Issue:** The rate limiter on POST `/rest/user/reset-password` uses headers['X-Forwarded-For'] as the rate-limit key with trust proxy enabled. An attacker rotates the X-Forwarded-For header on each request to present a fresh IP address, effectively bypassing the 100-requests/5-minute limit and enabling unlimited brute force of security question answers.

Attacker can brute-force security question answers without rate limiting, enabling account takeover via the password reset flow for any user whose email is known.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `server.ts:346`: `keyGenerator({ headers, ip })` { return headers['X-Forwarded-For'] ?? ip }. `server.ts:342`: `app.enable('trust proxy')` passes X-Forwarded-For as the trusted IP, making the limit trivially bypassable by changing this header.

```typescript
  344 │     windowMs: 5 * 60 * 1000,
  345 │     max: 100,
→ 346 │     keyGenerator ({ headers, ip }: { headers: any, ip: any }) { return headers['X-Forwarded-For'] ?? ip } // vuln-code-snippet vuln-line resetPasswordMortyChallenge
  347 │   }))
  348 │   // vuln-code-snippet end resetPasswordMortyChallenge
```

**Fix:** ◕ [M-029](#m-029) — Use `req.ip` as the rate-limit key (`server.ts:346`)

**Classification:** Broken Authentication · STRIDE: Spoofing · [CWE-290](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/290.html) · [OWASP A07:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A07_2025-Authentication_Failures/)

<a id="t-015"></a><a id="f-015"></a>
#### F-015 · Unverified JWT Decode in Chat Enables Order Impersonation (routes/chat.ts:45)

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-02](#c-02) - Express\.js REST API Backend  ·  **Location:** `routes/chat.ts:45`

**Trust boundary gap:** [tb-1](#tb-1) 🌐 External - external → express-api · Authentication: Chat endpoint skips the authentication leg of the external boundary; unverified token decode is accepted as identity proof.

**Issue:** The POST `/rest/chat` endpoint carries no `isAuthorized()` middleware and calls `security.decode(token)` which uses `jws.decode` without signature verification. An unauthenticated attacker constructs a JWT-format token claiming any user's id, sends it in the `Authorization` header, and triggers the `getOrderById` tool; the tool fetches that user's orders from the database, returning private order data to the attacker.

Any unauthenticated party can impersonate any user in the chatbot and retrieve that user's order details including email hash and product data by supplying a forged unsigned JWT.

**Evidence:** ◌ ambiguous - `routes/chat.ts:45` calls `security.decode(token)` which is `jws.decode` without verification. `server.ts:637` registers POST `/rest/chat` without `isAuthorized()` middleware. `routes/chat.ts:158` passes the unverified decoded `userId` to `UserModel.findByPk` for order authorization.

```typescript
  43 │   const token = utils.jwtFrom(req)
  44 │   if (!token) return undefined
→ 45 │   const decoded = security.decode(token) as { data?: { id?: number } } | undefined
  46 │   return decoded?.data?.id
  47 │ }
```

**Fix:** Pin the signature algorithm explicitly and reject `alg:none` and unknown algorithms → ◑ [M-002](#m-002) — Enforce JWT signature and algorithm verification (`chat.ts:45`) · ◕ [M-030](#m-030) — Enforce JWT signature and algorithm verification (`chat.ts:45`)

**Classification:** Broken Authentication · STRIDE: Spoofing · [CWE-347](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/347.html) · [OWASP A07:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A07_2025-Authentication_Failures/)

<a id="t-016"></a><a id="f-016"></a>
#### F-016 · Unauthenticated WebSocket Channel

**Severity:** 🟠 High - elevated to Critical: reaches a privileged operation on an unauthenticated endpoint  ·  **Component:** [C-06](#c-06) - Real-time WebSocket Channel  ·  **Location:** Multiple locations (4)

**Weakness:** [W-004](#w-004) - Endpoints are reachable without enforced authentication

**Instances (4):** 🟡 `frontend/src/app/Services/socket-io.service.ts:22`, 🟠 `lib/startup/registerWebsocketEvents.ts:24`, 🟡 `lib/startup/registerWebsocketEvents.ts:34`, 🟠 `lib/startup/registerWebsocketEvents.ts:42`

**Issue:** An anonymous internet client opens a Socket\.IO connection on port 3000 without presenting any JWT or session credential. The server accepts the connection unconditionally at `io.on('connection', ...)` and processes all subsequent events as if the client were a legitimate game participant.

Any unauthenticated internet attacker can connect to the real-time channel and immediately receive all pending challenge-solved notifications and submit challenge-verification events without any identity proof.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `io.on('connection', (socket: any) => { ... })` at `registerWebsocketEvents.ts:24` contains no authentication check; the server-level CORS restriction is set to `'http://localhost:4200'`, which is a development value that does not protect production deployments.

```typescript
lib/startup/registerWebsocketEvents.ts
  22 │   globalWithSocketIO.io = io
  23 │
→ 24 │   io.on('connection', (socket: any) => {
  25 │     if (firstConnectedSocket === null) {
  26 │       socket.emit('server started')
```

**Fix:** ◕ [M-031](#m-031) — Require authentication on every exposed endpoint (`registerWebsocketEvents.ts:24`)

**Classification:** Unauthenticated Management Plane · STRIDE: Spoofing · [CWE-306](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/306.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/) · walkthrough [Walkthrough §3.8](#38-unauthenticated-websocket-channel-in-real-time-websocket-channel)

<a id="t-026"></a><a id="f-026"></a>
#### F-026 · ZIP Slip Path Traversal in File Upload (routes/fileUpload.ts:44)

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-02](#c-02) - Express\.js REST API Backend  ·  **Location:** `routes/fileUpload.ts:44`

**Weakness:** [W-005](#w-005) - Input handling lacks enforced boundary validation

**Issue:** An unauthenticated attacker uploads a crafted ZIP archive whose entries contain path traversal sequences. The handler checks `absolutePath.includes(path.resolve('.'))` which only verifies the path starts within the application root, not within the uploads/complaints subdirectory.

An entry with path ../..`/ftp/legal.md` passes this check because the resolved application root is a prefix of the traversed path, allowing overwrite of files throughout the application directory tree. Attacker overwrites files anywhere within the application root including `ftp/legal.md`, server configuration, and other static content, enabling content tampering and potential command execution if a loadable file is overwritten.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `routes/fileUpload.ts:44`: if passes for paths like `/app/ftp/legal.md` because `path.resolve('.')` is `/app`. `routes/fileUpload.ts:45` then writes to 'uploads/complaints/' + `fileName` without sanitizing the `fileName`, enabling traversal. The `fileWriteChallenge` at line 43 confirms `ftp/legal.md` is reachable.

```typescript
  42 │                 const absolutePath = path.resolve('uploads/complaints/' + fileName)
  43 │                 challengeUtils.solveIf(challenges.fileWriteChallenge, () => { return absolutePath === path.resolve('ftp/legal.md') })
→ 44 │                 if (absolutePath.includes(path.resolve('.'))) {
  45 │                   entry.pipe(fs.createWriteStream('uploads/complaints/' + fileName).on('error', function (err) { next(err) }))
  46 │                 } else {
```

**Fix:** Resolve and normalise every constructed path and reject anything that escapes the intended base directory → ◕ [M-040](#m-040) — Constrain file paths to a safe base directory (`fileUpload.ts:44`)

**Classification:** Insecure File Handling · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-22](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/22.html) · [OWASP A06:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A06_2025-Insecure_Design/)

<a id="t-027"></a><a id="f-027"></a>
#### F-027 · LLM01 Prompt Injection Bypasses Coupon Discount Policy (routes/chat.ts:177)

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-02](#c-02) - Express\.js REST API Backend  ·  **Location:** `routes/chat.ts:177`

**Issue:** LLM01/ASI01/ASI02 - An attacker sends a chat message instructing the model to ignore the COUPON POLICY and generate a coupon with discount 100. The `generateCoupon` tool's `inputSchema` only declares `z.number()` with no maximum validator.

The system prompt policy is the only enforcement mechanism. Attacker obtains a valid coupon code for any discount percentage including 100%, bypassing the system prompt's 10% cap.

The coupon code generated by `security.generateCoupon` is cryptographically valid and redeemable at checkout.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `routes/chat.ts:177` defines the `generateCoupon` tool with `inputSchema` `z.object({ discount: z.number() })` - no .`max(10)` constraint. `routes/chat.ts:182` calls `security.generateCoupon(discount)` with the uncapped discount value. `challengeUtils.solveIf` at line 181 confirms discount >= 50 is achievable via greedy injection.
