```typescript
  176 │         inputSchema: z.object({
→ 177 │           discount: z.number().describe('The discount percentage for the coupon (maximum 10)') // vuln-code-snippet vuln-line chatbotPromptInjectionChallenge chatbotGreedyInjectionChallenge
  178 │         }),
  179 │         execute: async ({ discount }) => {
```

**Fix:** ◕ [M-041](#m-041) — Add server-side discount cap validation in generateCoupon tool execute handler (`chat.ts:177`)

**Classification:** Injection · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-1427](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/1427.html) · [OWASP A05:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A05_2025-Injection/)

<a id="t-028"></a><a id="f-028"></a>
#### F-028 · Client-Side Challenge Enforcement (registerWebsocketEvents.ts:41)

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-06](#c-06) - Real-time WebSocket Channel  ·  **Location:** `lib/startup/registerWebsocketEvents.ts:41`

**Issue:** An unauthenticated attacker connects to Socket\.IO and emits a 'verifyLocalXssChallenge' event whose data string contains '<iframe src="javascript:alert(`xss`)">' . The server at line 42 calls `challengeUtils.solveIf()` unconditionally with the attacker-supplied string, permanently marking the `localXssChallenge` as solved in the database without the attacker ever having injected that payload into an actual HTML page.

An attacker marks any of the three client-side challenge solutions (`localXssChallenge`, `xssBonusChallenge`, `svgInjectionChallenge`, `closeNotificationsChallenge`) as solved in the database without performing the required actions, corrupting game state permanently for that challenge key.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `socket.on('verifyLocalXssChallenge', (data: any) => { challengeUtils.solveIf(challenges.localXssChallenge, () => { return utils.contains(data, '<iframe src="javascript:alert">')  }) })` at `registerWebsocketEvents.ts:41-43` trusts the client to supply attacker-controlled data that directly drives the server-side game-state mutation without authentication or server-side observation of the actual XSS execution.

**Fix:** ◑ [M-042](#m-042) — Enforce authorization on the server (`registerWebsocketEvents.ts:41`)

**Classification:** Broken Access Control · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-602](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/602.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/)

<a id="t-031"></a><a id="f-031"></a>
#### F-031 · JWT stored in localStorage (login.component.ts:105)

**Severity:** 🟠 High - verified attack-chain keystone in AC-T-001 (Account Takeover via XSS + Token Hijacking); see [§9](#9-abuse-cases)  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Angular Web Application (SPA)  ·  **Location:** `frontend/src/app/login/login.component.ts:105`

**Weakness:** [W-011](#w-011) - Browser scripts retain reusable session credentials

**Issue:** Any cross-site scripting payload executing in the application origin reads `localStorage.getItem('token')` to extract the victim's JWT Bearer token, then exfiltrates it to an attacker-controlled endpoint for replay on authenticated API calls. A single XSS payload (of which six confirmed instances exist in this SPA) can steal the JWT from `localStorage` and grant the attacker persistent authenticated access to the victim's account until the token expires.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `login.component.ts:105` writes `authentication.token` to `localStorage`; `request.interceptor.ts:13` reads it on every API request via `localStorage.getItem('token')`; `localStorage` is accessible to any JavaScript running in the same origin, making the token trivially readable by XSS payloads.

**Fix:** ◕ [M-045](#m-045) — Store session tokens in HttpOnly, Secure cookies (`login.component.ts:105`)

**Classification:** Insecure Client-Side Storage · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-922](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/922.html) · [OWASP A04:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A04_2025-Cryptographic_Failures/)

<a id="t-033"></a><a id="f-033"></a>
#### F-033 · MD5 password hashing without salt enables offline (lib/insecurity.ts:43)

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-01](#c-01) - Authentication & Session Surface  ·  **Location:** `lib/insecurity.ts:43`

**Weakness:** [W-013](#w-013) - Security-sensitive data uses weak cryptographic primitives

**Issue:** An attacker who obtains the SQLite database file - by exploiting the SQL injection at `routes/login.ts:34` or via a directory traversal - recovers all user password hashes in `MD5` format; because `MD5` has no salt and is computed in microseconds per attempt on commodity hardware, all hashed passwords are recoverable via rainbow tables or GPU brute-force within hours. Every user password in the application is recoverable in plain text given only the Users table `MD5` hash column.

Recovered passwords enable direct account takeover on this application and credential-stuffing attacks on other services where users reuse passwords. The business context identifies `MD5`-hashed passwords as a declared sensitive asset.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `lib/insecurity.ts:43` exports hash = (data) => `crypto.createHash('md5').update(data).digest('hex')`; this function is the sole password hashing mechanism used in the login query and the 2FA setup check. `MD5` provides no work factor and no per-user salt.

**Fix:** Replace the broken hash with a salted password-hashing function (bcrypt/Argon2id) → ◕ [M-047](#m-047) — Hash passwords with a strong, salted algorithm (`insecurity.ts:43`)

**Classification:** Cryptographic Failures · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-916](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/916.html) · [OWASP A04:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A04_2025-Cryptographic_Failures/)

<a id="t-035"></a><a id="f-035"></a>
#### F-035 · Unauthenticated /encryptionkeys Exposes JWT Public Key (server.ts:277)

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-02](#c-02) - Express\.js REST API Backend  ·  **Location:** `server.ts:277`

**Issue:** An attacker fetches GET `/encryptionkeys/` to list the key files and GET `/encryptionkeys/jwt.pub` to retrieve the RSA public key. This public key enables the JWT algorithm confusion attack (express-api-001): the attacker uses it as an `HS256` HMAC secret to forge tokens.

The directory is served without authentication, requiring no credentials. Attacker retrieves the JWT public key enabling algorithm confusion attacks (express-api-001).

Additional key files present in the directory may expose further cryptographic material.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `server.ts:277-278` registers `serveIndex('encryptionkeys', ...)` and `serveKeyFiles()` on `/encryptionkeys` without any authentication middleware. The architecture context lists `/encryptionkeys` as an explicitly exposed interface.

```typescript
  275 │
  276 │   /* /encryptionkeys directory browsing */
→ 277 │   app.use('/encryptionkeys', serveIndexMiddleware, serveIndex('encryptionkeys', { icons: true, view: 'details' }))
  278 │   app.use('/encryptionkeys/:file', serveKeyFiles())
  279 │
```

**Fix:** ◕ [M-048](#m-048) — Disable public directory listings (`server.ts:277`)

**Classification:** Unauthenticated Management Plane · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-548](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/548.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/)

<a id="t-036"></a><a id="f-036"></a>
#### F-036 · Unauthenticated /support/logs Exposes HTTP Access Logs (server.ts:281)

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-02](#c-02) - Express\.js REST API Backend  ·  **Location:** `server.ts:281`

**Issue:** An unauthenticated attacker browses GET `/support/logs` to obtain a directory listing of rotating HTTP access log files. Each log file contains all HTTP requests in Combined Log Format including client IPs, request paths (which may include tokens, order IDs, and user IDs), user-agent strings, and response codes.

Downloading the logs reveals which user IP made which request, enabling session correlation and user tracking. Attacker enumerates all HTTP request paths, client IPs, and timing from the access logs.

This enables user tracking, session inference, and identification of API endpoints targeted by other users. If JWT tokens appear in query strings, the attacker can hijack those sessions.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `server.ts:281-283` registers `serveIndex('logs', ...)` and `serveLogFiles()` on `/support/logs` without any authentication middleware. The known threats context entry confirms POST `/file-upload` accessible without authentication at `server.ts:309`, and the access log disclosure pattern applies to `/support/logs` at lines 281-283.

```typescript
  279 │
  280 │   /* /logs directory browsing */ // vuln-code-snippet neutral-line accessLogDisclosureChallenge
→ 281 │   app.use('/support/logs', serveIndexMiddleware, serveIndex('logs', { icons: true, view: 'details' })) // vuln-code-snippet vuln-line accessLogDisclosureChallenge
  282 │   app.use('/support/logs', verify.accessControlChallenges()) // vuln-code-snippet hide-line
  283 │   app.use('/support/logs/:file', serveLogFiles()) // vuln-code-snippet vuln-line accessLogDisclosureChallenge
```

**Fix:** ◕ [M-049](#m-049) — Disable public directory listings (`server.ts:281`)

**Classification:** Unauthenticated Management Plane · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-548](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/548.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/)

<a id="t-037"></a><a id="f-037"></a>
#### F-037 · Unauthenticated access to all order records (routes/trackOrder.ts:18)

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-02](#c-02) - Express\.js REST API Backend  ·  **Location:** `routes/trackOrder.ts:18`

**Weakness:** [W-003](#w-003) - Authorization is implemented route by route

**Issue:** An unauthenticated caller exploits the `$where` injection in `trackOrder` (🟠 [F-001](#f-001) — NoSQL Injection in MarsDB Query Layer) to make the MarsDB predicate always true, receiving the full orders collection in the response. The route has no authentication middleware and returns order objects that include customer email addresses, basket contents, and delivery status.

All order records including customer email addresses (masked but recoverable by pattern) and order contents are exposed to any unauthenticated network caller who can trigger the always-true \`$where` predicate.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `trackOrder.ts:18` calls `db.ordersCollection.find({ $where: ... })` with a user-controlled expression. There is no `security.isAuthorized` or JWT middleware check before this route executes. The result set is returned directly to the caller at line 24.

```typescript
  16 │
  17 │     challengeUtils.solveIf(challenges.reflectedXssChallenge, () => { return utils.contains(id, '<iframe src="javascript:alert(`xss`)">') })
→ 18 │     db.ordersCollection.find({ $where: `this.orderId === '${id}'` }).then((order: any) => {
  19 │       const result = utils.queryResultToJson(order)
  20 │       challengeUtils.solveIf(challenges.noSqlOrdersChallenge, () => { return result.data.length > 1 })
```

**Fix:** ◕ [M-050](#m-050) — Enforce server-side authorization on every endpoint (`trackOrder.ts:18`)

**Classification:** Broken Access Control · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-862](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/862.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/)

<a id="t-038"></a><a id="f-038"></a>
#### F-038 · Unauthenticated CTF Flag Disclosure (registerWebsocketEvents.ts:31)

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-06](#c-06) - Real-time WebSocket Channel  ·  **Location:** `lib/startup/registerWebsocketEvents.ts:31`

**Issue:** An unauthenticated attacker connects to the Socket\.IO server. The server immediately emits all entries in the shared notifications array to the new socket.

Each notification object was built in `challengeUtils.ts:58-66` and includes the challenge name, description, and a pre-computed CTF flag value derived from `utils.ctfFlag(challenge.name)`. An attacker obtains all CTF flag values for challenges already solved in the current server session, undermining the integrity of competitive CTF scoring and allowing challenge solutions to be claimed without completing the required game tasks.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `notifications.forEach((notification: any) => { socket.emit('challenge solved', notification) })` at `registerWebsocketEvents.ts:30-32` iterates the shared notifications array and broadcasts each entry - which contains the flag field populated at `challengeUtils.ts:48` - to every newly connecting socket with no authentication prerequisite.

**Fix:** Restrict the response to the minimum fields needed and never echo secrets → ◕ [M-051](#m-051) — Stop exposing internal information to clients (`registerWebsocketEvents.ts:31`)

**Classification:** Error Information Disclosure · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-200](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/200.html) · [OWASP A02:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A02_2025-Security_Misconfiguration/)

<a id="t-040"></a><a id="f-040"></a>
#### F-040 · Unsalted MD5 password hashing enables offline dictionary (models/user.ts:77)

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-02](#c-02) - Express\.js REST API Backend  ·  **Location:** `models/user.ts:77`

**Issue:** The password setter calls `security.hash(clearTextPassword)` which computes `crypto.createHash('md5').update(data).digest('hex')` with no salt (`lib/insecurity.ts:43`). All `Users.password` values are therefore unsalted `MD5` hashes.

An attacker who obtains the password column - for example by exploiting the SQL injection at `routes/login.ts:34` which returns SELECT * - can crack all hashes simultaneously via precomputed rainbow tables or GPU-accelerated dictionary attacks at no per-user cost. Any attacker who obtains the Users table recovers plaintext passwords for all accounts.

Reuse of those credentials on external services extends the breach beyond the application boundary.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `models/user.ts:77` stores `security.hash(clearTextPassword)` where `security.hash` is `crypto.createHash('md5')` with no salt; the Users table holds identical hash values for identical passwords across all users.

```typescript
  75 │         type: DataTypes.STRING,
  76 │         set (clearTextPassword: string) {
→ 77 │           this.setDataValue('password', security.hash(clearTextPassword)) // vuln-code-snippet vuln-line weakPasswordChallenge
  78 │         }
  79 │       }, // vuln-code-snippet end weakPasswordChallenge
```

**Fix:** ◕ [M-053](#m-053) — Replace MD5 password hash with salted bcrypt or Argon2id in `models/user.ts` password setter (`user.ts:77`)

**Classification:** Cryptographic Failures · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-759](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/759.html) · [OWASP A04:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A04_2025-Cryptographic_Failures/)

<a id="t-041"></a><a id="f-041"></a>
#### F-041 · YAML Bomb Causes CPU Exhaustion in File Upload (routes/fileUpload.ts:117)

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-02](#c-02) - Express\.js REST API Backend  ·  **Location:** `routes/fileUpload.ts:117`

**Issue:** An unauthenticated attacker uploads a small YAML file containing deeply nested alias references (a YAML bomb). The `yaml.load(data)` call expands the anchors exponentially, consuming all available CPU and memory.

Unlike the XML handler which has a 2-second vm execution timeout (`routes/fileUpload.ts:83`), the YAML handler has no explicit execution timeout wrapper, allowing the expansion to run until the process crashes or the heap is exhausted. A ~1KB YAML bomb payload can cause the server process to exhaust memory or remain unresponsive for the duration of the timeout, denying service to all concurrent users.

Multiple concurrent uploads compound the effect.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `routes/fileUpload.ts:117` calls `vm.runInContext('JSON.stringify(yaml.load(data))', sandbox, { timeout: 2000 })`. While a vm timeout is present, the YAML parser (`js-yaml`'s `yaml.load`) performs the expansion synchronously inside the vm context. A sufficiently large billion-laughs payload can cause memory exhaustion before the timeout fires. The `yamlBombChallenge` at line 122-125 confirms this is an exploitable DoS path.

**Fix:** Bound the request rate and the per-request resource budget on this endpoint → ◕ [M-054](#m-054) — Bound parser and decompression resource limits (`fileUpload.ts:117`)

**Classification:** Denial of Service · STRIDE: Denial of Service · [CWE-400](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/400.html) · [OWASP A06:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A06_2025-Insecure_Design/)

<a id="t-042"></a><a id="f-042"></a>
#### F-042 · NoSQL \$where Injection Blocks Event Loop

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-02](#c-02) - Express\.js REST API Backend  ·  **Location:** Multiple locations (2)

**Instances (2):** `routes/showProductReviews.ts:36`, `routes/showProductReviews.ts:22`

**Issue:** An attacker sends GET `/rest/products/1`;`sleep(2000)``/reviews` to inject a JavaScript expression into the MarsDB \`$where` predicate. The `global.sleep` function defined at `routes/showProductReviews.ts:16-26` is a synchronous busy-wait loop that blocks the Node\.js event loop.

Since the \`$where` query runs in the same event loop thread, every concurrent request is delayed for up to 2 seconds per injection. Any unauthenticated attacker can stall the Node\.js event loop for 2 seconds per request, making the API unresponsive to all concurrent users.

Continuous requests maintain the DoS state without rate limiting.

**Evidence:** ◌ ambiguous - `routes/showProductReviews.ts:16-26` defines `global.sleep` as a synchronous busy-wait. `routes/showProductReviews.ts:31-36`: when `isChallengeEnabled` is true the id is `utils.trunc(req.params.id, 40)` and is concatenated into \`$where`, enabling injection of `sleep(2000)`. The `noSqlCommandChallenge` at line 38 confirms the 2000ms block is measurable.

**Fix:** Bound the request rate and the per-request resource budget on this endpoint → ◑ [M-005](#m-005) — Offload CPU-bound work and bound execution time (`showProductReviews.ts:36`) · ◕ [M-055](#m-055) — Rate-limit expensive requests and bound input size (`showProductReviews.ts:36`)

**Classification:** Denial of Service · STRIDE: Denial of Service · [CWE-400](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/400.html) · [OWASP A06:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A06_2025-Insecure_Design/)

<a id="t-043"></a><a id="f-043"></a>
#### F-043 · Unbounded WebSocket Connection Consumption (registerWebsocketEvents.ts:20)

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-06](#c-06) - Real-time WebSocket Channel  ·  **Location:** `lib/startup/registerWebsocketEvents.ts:20`

**Issue:** An unauthenticated attacker repeatedly opens Socket\.IO connections from many clients or in rapid succession. The Server instantiation at line 20 applies no per-IP connection limit, no rate limit on the upgrade handshake, and no maximum concurrent connection count.

Each connection allocates a socket object, registers event listeners, and iterates the notifications array; enough simultaneous connections exhaust file descriptors or heap memory and crash the Node\.js process. An attacker can exhaust server file descriptors or heap memory through unauthenticated high-volume WebSocket connections, making the application unavailable to legitimate users.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - new `Server(server, { cors: { origin: 'http://localhost:4200' } })` at `registerWebsocketEvents.ts:20` creates the Socket\.IO server with no `connectionStateRecovery` limit, no per-connection throttle, and no `maxHttpBufferSize` override; `io.on('connection', ...)` at line 24 executes per-connection work with no guard against simultaneous high-volume connections.

**Fix:** Bound the request rate and the per-request resource budget on this endpoint → ◕ [M-056](#m-056) — Rate-limit expensive requests and bound input size (`registerWebsocketEvents.ts:20`)

**Classification:** Denial of Service · STRIDE: Denial of Service · [CWE-400](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/400.html) · [OWASP A06:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A06_2025-Insecure_Design/)

<a id="t-044"></a><a id="f-044"></a>
#### F-044 · Security-question password reset without lockout (routes/resetPassword.ts:41)

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-01](#c-01) - Authentication & Session Surface  ·  **Location:** `routes/resetPassword.ts:41`

**Issue:** An attacker submits POST `/rest/user/reset-password` with a target email and guessed security answers; because the comparison at line 41 applies no request-rate limiting and no answer-attempt lockout, the attacker iterates answers from a targeted research effort (OSINT or dictionary) until one matches, then resets the victim's password and takes over the account. Any account, including admin, can be taken over by an attacker who can research or brute-force the answer to the security question.

The account takeover yields a full session token, bypassing password and 2FA requirements entirely for that account.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `resetPassword.ts:41` compares `security.hmac(answer)` === `data.answer` with no attempt counter, lockout, or rate-limit middleware visible in the handler or its imports. The security answer HMAC key is hardcoded (🟠 [F-032](#f-032) — Hardcoded HMAC key exposes security answer hashes to offline attack), allowing offline pre-computation of candidate answers.

```typescript
  39 │         }]
  40 │       })
→ 41 │       if ((data != null) && security.hmac(answer) === data.answer) {
  42 │         const user = await UserModel.findByPk(data.UserId)
  43 │         if (user) {
```

**Fix:** ◑ [M-057](#m-057) — Replace security-question reset with email-token flow and add attempt lockout (`resetPassword.ts:41`)

**Classification:** Broken Authentication · STRIDE: Elevation of Privilege · [CWE-640](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/640.html) · [OWASP A07:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A07_2025-Authentication_Failures/)

<a id="t-045"></a><a id="f-045"></a>

**Instances (28):** `server.ts:310`, `server.ts:311`, `server.ts:407`, `server.ts:419`, `server.ts:420`, `server.ts:421`, `server.ts:437`, `server.ts:440` … (+20 more)

<a id="t-049"></a><a id="f-049"></a>
#### F-049 · Missing authentication on key submission endpoint (routes/checkKeys.ts:6)

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-07](#c-07) - Web3 / Wallet / NFT Surface  ·  **Location:** `routes/checkKeys.ts:6`

**Weakness:** [W-004](#w-004) - Endpoints are reachable without enforced authentication

**Trust boundary gap:** [tb-1](#tb-1) 🌐 External - external → express-api · Authentication: This boundary authentication assumption requires `expressJwt` on state-changing routes; `checkKeys` is state-changing (marks challenge solved) but carries no such middleware

**Issue:** An unauthenticated attacker calls POST `/rest/web3/submitKey` directly without a JWT. The route handler at line 6 processes the submission and, given the hardcoded mnemonic (line 10), marks the `nftUnlockChallenge` as solved at line 15 without verifying caller identity.

The attacker obtains challenge completion status belonging to any user session without being authenticated. Any internet actor can trigger challenge completion without logging in, bypassing the intended authentication requirement for challenge participation and inflating challenge-solved statistics.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - The `checkKeys` handler starts at line 6 with no authentication middleware applied. Controls context explicitly states 'No authentication confirmed on `checkKeys` or redirect endpoints'. The trust-boundary assumption (tb-1 authentication leg) is not met for this route.

```typescript
  5 │
→ 6 │ export function checkKeys () {
  7 │   return async (req: Request, res: Response) => {
  8 │     try {
```

**Fix:** ◕ [M-062](#m-062) — Require authentication on every exposed endpoint (`checkKeys.ts:6`)

**Classification:** Unauthenticated Management Plane · STRIDE: Elevation of Privilege · [CWE-306](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/306.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/)

<a id="t-024"></a><a id="f-024"></a>
#### F-024 · Input compiled as template source (routes/userProfile.ts:87)

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-02](#c-02) - Express\.js REST API Backend  ·  **Location:** `routes/userProfile.ts:87`

**Weakness:** [W-012](#w-012) - Application data crosses into template source

**Issue:** Persisted record read reaches an executable expression. Observed path conditions: if (username) {. The unsafe path is observed; attacker control, access prerequisites, and active configuration require verification.

**Evidence:** ◌ ambiguous

```typescript
  85 │     try {
  86 │       const pug = (await import('pug')).default
→ 87 │       const fn = pug.compile(template)
  88 │       const CSP = `img-src 'self' ${user?.profileImage}; script-src 'self' 'unsafe-eval'`
  89 │
```

**Fix:** ◑ [M-004](#m-004) — Remove server-side evaluation of untrusted input (`userProfile.ts:87`) · ◕ [M-038](#m-038) — Remove server-side evaluation of untrusted input (`userProfile.ts:87`)

**Classification:** Injection · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-1336](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/1336.html) · [OWASP A05:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A05_2025-Injection/)

<a id="t-047"></a><a id="f-047"></a>
#### F-047 · Free Deluxe Membership (routes/deluxe.ts:24)

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-02](#c-02) - Express\.js REST API Backend  ·  **Location:** `routes/deluxe.ts:24`

**Weakness:** [W-003](#w-003) - Authorization is implemented route by route

**Issue:** An authenticated non-deluxe user sends POST `/rest/deluxe-membership` with `paymentMode` set to any value other than 'wallet' or 'card' (e.g., `paymentMode`: 'free'). The `upgradeToDeluxe` handler checks payment only if `paymentMode` equals 'wallet' or 'card'; no else clause enforces payment for other values.

The handler falls through to the `user.update` call, granting deluxe membership without any payment. Any customer can self-upgrade to deluxe membership for free, bypassing the 49 currency-unit payment requirement.

This directly impacts application revenue integrity.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `routes/deluxe.ts:24-43`: the two if blocks check `paymentMode` === 'wallet' and `paymentMode` === 'card' independently with no fallback rejection. If neither condition is true, execution reaches `user.update({ role: roles.deluxe })` at line 43 regardless. `challengeUtils.solveIf` at line 44-45 with `paymentMode` !== 'wallet' && `paymentMode` !== 'card' confirms this is an intentional challenge path.

```typescript
  22 │         return
  23 │       }
→ 24 │       if (req.body.paymentMode === 'wallet') {
  25 │         const wallet = await WalletModel.findOne({ where: { UserId: req.body.UserId } })
  26 │         if ((wallet != null) && wallet.balance < 49) {
```

**Fix:** ◕ [M-060](#m-060) — Enforce server-side authorization on every endpoint (`deluxe.ts:24`)

**Classification:** Broken Access Control · STRIDE: Elevation of Privilege · [CWE-862](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/862.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/)

<a id="t-048"></a><a id="f-048"></a>
#### F-048 · IDOR: review update without ownership check (routes/updateProductReviews.ts:16)

**Severity:** 🟠 High - elevated to Critical as a verified attack-chain keystone in AC-T-002 (Bulk Data Exfiltration via Broken Object Authorization); see [§9](#9-abuse-cases)  ·  **Component:** [C-02](#c-02) - Express\.js REST API Backend  ·  **Location:** `routes/updateProductReviews.ts:16`

**Weakness:** [W-003](#w-003) - Authorization is implemented route by route

**Issue:** An authenticated user submits a PUT request with a known review `_id` that belongs to another user. The route reads the caller's identity from the JWT but never checks that the review's `author` field matches before updating the `message`.

The attacker can forge any review to appear to say anything. Any authenticated user can overwrite the review content of any other user, enabling reputation damage, content injection into product pages, and circumvention of moderation controls.

**Evidence:** ◌ ambiguous - `updateProductReviews.ts:16` reads `user` from the JWT token. Lines 17-21 execute the update using `req.body.id` directly without any `author` equality guard between reading the user and writing the document.

```typescript
  14 │ export function updateProductReviews () {
  15 │   return (req: Request, res: Response, next: NextFunction) => {
→ 16 │     const user = security.authenticatedUsers.from(req) // vuln-code-snippet vuln-line forgedReviewChallenge
  17 │     db.reviewsCollection.update( // vuln-code-snippet neutral-line forgedReviewChallenge
  18 │       { _id: req.body.id }, // vuln-code-snippet vuln-line noSqlReviewsChallenge forgedReviewChallenge
```

**Fix:** Tie every object lookup to the requesting user's identity and reject cross-tenant references → ◑ [M-007](#m-007) — Enforce object-level (ownership) authorization (`updateProductReviews.ts:16`) · ◕ [M-061](#m-061) — Enforce object-level (ownership) authorization (`updateProductReviews.ts:16`)

**Classification:** Broken Access Control · STRIDE: Elevation of Privilege · [CWE-639](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/639.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/)

<a id="t-009"></a><a id="f-009"></a>
#### F-009 · OAuth CSRF (login.component.ts:152)

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Angular Web Application (SPA)  ·  **Location:** `frontend/src/app/login/login.component.ts:152`

**Weakness:** [W-007](#w-007) - OAuth sign-in requests are not bound to the user's browser session

**Issue:** An attacker obtains a Google `access_token` for their own account, constructs a URL containing the `access_token` fragment targeting the app's OAuth route, and tricks a victim into following it; the SPA processes the token and logs the victim in as the attacker's Google identity because no state nonce is validated. Victim's session is authenticated as the attacker's Google identity; the attacker can read the victim's account data and perform purchases for the duration of that linked session.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `googleLogin()` at line 152 redirects to Google with `response_type=token` and no state parameter; `oauth.component.ts` never verifies a state value before consuming the `access_token` from the URL fragment, leaving the implicit-flow OAuth callback open to CSRF injection.

**Fix:** Enforce a same-origin or signed CSRF token on every state-changing endpoint → ◕ [M-024](#m-024) — Add anti-CSRF protection to state-changing requests (`login.component.ts:152`)

**Classification:** Cross-Site Request Forgery (CSRF) · STRIDE: Spoofing · [CWE-352](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/352.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/)

<a id="t-017"></a><a id="f-017"></a>
#### F-017 · Stored XSS (about.component.ts:119)

**Severity:** 🟠 High - verified attack-chain keystone in AC-T-001 (Account Takeover via XSS + Token Hijacking); see [§9](#9-abuse-cases)  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Angular Web Application (SPA)  ·  **Location:** `frontend/src/app/about/about.component.ts:119`

**Weakness:** [W-009](#w-009) - Frontend rendering lacks enforced output encoding

**Issue:** An attacker submits a feedback entry with an HTML/script payload; when any user - including unauthenticated visitors - loads the About page, the gallery loop at lines 116-119 embeds `feedback.comment` verbatim in a template string and marks the result as safe with `bypassSecurityTrustHtml`, executing the payload for every visitor. Attacker's script executes in every About page visitor's browser, including unauthenticated users; for logged-in users, their JWT and session can be stolen.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `about.component.ts` lines 116-119 build an HTML string embedding feedbacks[i].comment and assign the result to `this.sanitizer.bypassSecurityTrustHtml`, bypassing Angular's sanitizer for user-submitted content displayed in the public gallery.

```typescript
  117 │             feedbacks[i].comment
  118 │           }</p><div class="feedback-stars">(${this.stars[feedbacks[i].rating]})</div></figcaption>`
→ 119 │           feedbacks[i].comment = this.sanitizer.bypassSecurityTrustHtml(
  120 │             feedbacks[i].comment
  121 │           )
```

**Fix:** Output-encode untrusted strings at every sink and remove all `bypassSecurityTrustHtml` calls → ◕ [M-032](#m-032) — Encode output instead of bypassing the framework sanitizer (`about.component.ts:119`)

**Classification:** Cross-Site Scripting (XSS) · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-79](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/79.html) · [OWASP A05:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A05_2025-Injection/)

<a id="t-018"></a><a id="f-018"></a>
#### F-018 · Stored XSS (administration.component.ts:73)

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Angular Web Application (SPA)  ·  **Location:** `frontend/src/app/administration/administration.component.ts:73`

**Weakness:** [W-009](#w-009) - Frontend rendering lacks enforced output encoding

**Issue:** An attacker registers an account with an email containing an HTML/script payload; when an administrator opens the admin panel, `findAllUsers()` loads all user records at line 70 and line 73 embeds each email verbatim inside a `bypassSecurityTrustHtml` string, executing the attacker's script in the admin session and enabling admin account takeover. Administrator's JWT and session are stolen; attacker gains full administrative access to user management, feedback deletion, and all privileged API endpoints.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `administration.component.ts:73` evaluates `this.sanitizer`.`bypassSecurityTrustHtml`('<span class="...">' + `user.email` + '</span>'); `user.email` comes from the API response at line 70 and is embedded directly without HTML encoding.

```typescript
  71 │         this.userDataSourceHidden = users
  72 │         for (const user of this.userDataSource) {
→ 73 │           user.email = this.sanitizer.bypassSecurityTrustHtml(`<span class="${this.doesUserHaveAnActiveSession(user) ? 'confirmation' : 'error'}">${user.email}</span>`)
  74 │         }
  75 │         this.userDataSource = new MatTableDataSource(this.userDataSource)
```

**Fix:** Output-encode untrusted strings at every sink and remove all `bypassSecurityTrustHtml` calls → ◕ [M-033](#m-033) — Encode output instead of bypassing the framework sanitizer (`administration.component.ts:73`)

**Classification:** Cross-Site Scripting (XSS) · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-79](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/79.html) · [OWASP A05:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A05_2025-Injection/)

<a id="t-019"></a><a id="f-019"></a>
#### F-019 · Stored XSS (administration.component.ts:91)

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Angular Web Application (SPA)  ·  **Location:** `frontend/src/app/administration/administration.component.ts:91`

**Weakness:** [W-009](#w-009) - Frontend rendering lacks enforced output encoding

**Issue:** An attacker submits a customer feedback entry containing an HTML/script payload via the publicly available feedback form; when an administrator views the admin panel, `findAllFeedbacks()` loads all feedback at line 89 and line 91 applies `bypassSecurityTrustHtml` to each comment, executing the payload in the admin session. Administrator's JWT and session are stolen via JavaScript execution in the admin panel, granting the attacker full administrative access.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `findAllFeedbacks()` at line 89 assigns all feedback to `feedbackDataSource`; the for loop at line 90 calls `this.sanitizer.bypassSecurityTrustHtml(feedback.comment)` at line 91 on each entry without encoding, marking user-submitted content as safe HTML.

```typescript
  89 │         this.feedbackDataSource = feedbacks
  90 │         for (const feedback of this.feedbackDataSource) {
→ 91 │           feedback.comment = this.sanitizer.bypassSecurityTrustHtml(feedback.comment)
  92 │         }
  93 │         this.feedbackDataSource = new MatTableDataSource(this.feedbackDataSource)
```

**Fix:** Output-encode untrusted strings at every sink and remove all `bypassSecurityTrustHtml` calls → ◕ [M-034](#m-034) — Encode output instead of bypassing the framework sanitizer (`administration.component.ts:91`)

**Classification:** Cross-Site Scripting (XSS) · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-79](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/79.html) · [OWASP A05:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A05_2025-Injection/)

<a id="t-020"></a><a id="f-020"></a>
#### F-020 · Stored XSS (search-result.component.ts:110)

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Angular Web Application (SPA)  ·  **Location:** `frontend/src/app/search-result/search-result.component.ts:110`

**Weakness:** [W-009](#w-009) - Frontend rendering lacks enforced output encoding

**Issue:** An attacker stores an HTML/script payload in a product description via the REST API; when any user browses the search results page, `trustProductDescription()` applies `bypassSecurityTrustHtml` to every description at line 110, causing Angular to render the payload as live HTML and executing it in every visitor's browser. Script executes in every browsing user's session, enabling mass JWT theft from `localStorage`, account takeover, and forced actions against all visitors including unauthenticated users.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `trustProductDescription()` at line 108 iterates all products and at line 110 replaces each description with `this.sanitizer.bypassSecurityTrustHtml(tableData[i].description)`, marking attacker-controlled API content as safe for unescaped HTML rendering.

```typescript
  108 │   trustProductDescription (tableData: any[]) { // vuln-code-snippet neutral-line restfulXssChallenge
  109 │     for (let i = 0; i < tableData.length; i++) { // vuln-code-snippet neutral-line restfulXssChallenge
→ 110 │       tableData[i].description = this.sanitizer.bypassSecurityTrustHtml(tableData[i].description) // vuln-code-snippet vuln-line restfulXssChallenge
  111 │     } // vuln-code-snippet neutral-line restfulXssChallenge
  112 │   } // vuln-code-snippet neutral-line restfulXssChallenge
```

**Fix:** Output-encode untrusted strings at every sink and remove all `bypassSecurityTrustHtml` calls → ◕ [M-035](#m-035) — Encode output instead of bypassing the framework sanitizer (`search-result.component.ts:110`)

**Classification:** Cross-Site Scripting (XSS) · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-79](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/79.html) · [OWASP A05:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A05_2025-Injection/)

<a id="t-021"></a><a id="f-021"></a>
#### F-021 · DOM XSS (search-result.component.ts:143)

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Angular Web Application (SPA)  ·  **Location:** `frontend/src/app/search-result/search-result.component.ts:143`

**Weakness:** [W-009](#w-009) - Frontend rendering lacks enforced output encoding

Attacker-supplied JavaScript executes in the victim's browser in the application origin, enabling JWT theft from `localStorage`, session hijacking, and arbitrary actions on behalf of the victim.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `filterTable()` at line 136 reads `this.route.snapshot.queryParams.q` directly; line 143 assigns `this.sanitizer.bypassSecurityTrustHtml(queryParam)` to `searchValue` without any HTML encoding, and Angular's template binds `searchValue` as trusted HTML.

```typescript
  141 │       }) // vuln-code-snippet hide-end
  142 │       this.dataSource.filter = queryParam.toLowerCase()
→ 143 │       this.searchValue = this.sanitizer.bypassSecurityTrustHtml(queryParam) // vuln-code-snippet vuln-line localXssChallenge xssBonusChallenge
  144 │       if (this.gridDataSourceSubscription) {
  145 │         this.gridDataSourceSubscription.unsubscribe()
```

**Fix:** Output-encode untrusted strings at every sink and remove all `bypassSecurityTrustHtml` calls → ◕ [M-036](#m-036) — Encode output instead of bypassing the framework sanitizer (`search-result.component.ts:143`)

<a id="t-025"></a><a id="f-025"></a>
#### F-025 · CI/CD Workflow Supply-Chain Risk

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-08](#c-08) - CI/CD Pipeline  ·  **Location:** Multiple locations (2)

**Weakness:** [W-008](#w-008) - Build pipeline trusts mutable third-party references

**Instances (2):** 🟠 `.gitignore:10`, 🟡 `.github/workflows/ci.yml:202`

**Issue:** `package-lock.json` is excluded from version control by `.gitignore` line 10. Every CI job runs `npm install` which resolves dependencies non-deterministically against the current registry state.

An attacker who publishes a malicious version of any transitive dependency within the semver range gets it installed across every build. A compromised transitive dependency executes arbitrary code in CI and on every developer workstation, enabling secret exfiltration, backdoored release artifacts, or persistent compromise.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `.gitignore` line 10 excludes `package-lock.json`. `package-lock.json` is absent from the repository. `ci.yml` uses `npm install` (not `npm ci`) in at least six separate jobs. Without a lockfile, semver ranges resolve to whatever version the registry currently serves.

```
.gitignore
   8 │ !uploads/complaints/.gitkeep
   9 │ ftp/legal.md
→ 10 │ package-lock.json
  11 │ i18n/*.json
  12 │ i18n/*.invalid
```

**Fix:** ◕ [M-039](#m-039) — Pin third-party dependencies to immutable versions (`.gitignore:10`)

**Classification:** Supply-Chain Integrity · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-829](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/829.html) · [OWASP A03:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A03_2025-Software_Supply_Chain_Failures/)

<a id="t-034"></a><a id="f-034"></a>
#### F-034 · Package Install Scripts Run as Root

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-08](#c-08) - CI/CD Pipeline  ·  **Location:** `Dockerfile:5`

**Issue:** Package Install Scripts Run as Root: `npm install --unsafe-perm` runs postinstall scripts as root, widening supply-chain compromise scope.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

```dockerfile
  3 │ WORKDIR /juice-shop
  4 │ RUN npm install -g typescript@~5.3.3
→ 5 │ RUN npm install --omit=dev --unsafe-perm
  6 │ RUN npm dedupe --omit=dev
  7 │ RUN rm -rf frontend/node_modules
```

**Fix:** ◕ [M-009](#m-009) — Drop unnecessary privileges in build and runtime (`Dockerfile:5`)

**Classification:** Supply-Chain Integrity · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-250](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/250.html) · [OWASP A03:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A03_2025-Software_Supply_Chain_Failures/)

<a id="t-046"></a><a id="f-046"></a>
#### F-046 · Missing Authorization

**Severity:** 🟠 High  ·  **Component:** [C-08](#c-08) - CI/CD Pipeline  ·  **Location:** Multiple locations (2)

**Weakness:** [W-003](#w-003) - Authorization is implemented route by route

**Instances (2):** 🟠 `.github/workflows/ci.yml:1`, 🟢 `.github/workflows/lint-fixer.yml:1`

**Issue:** `ci.yml` defines no top-level or job-level `permissions:` block. On repositories where the default token permission is read-write, a compromised CI step (malicious dependency, injected script, or vulnerable action) gains write access to repository contents, packages, and issues via the implicit `GITHUB_TOKEN`.

The attacker uses this to push backdoored commits, publish modified releases, or create malicious GitHub issues pointing to the compromised branch. A compromised CI step can write to the repository, publish releases, or access secrets beyond what the pipeline legitimately requires, enabling persistent code tampering or secret exfiltration under the pipeline's identity.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `ci.yml` has no `permissions:` key at the workflow or job level (verified by reading the full 405-line file). The workflow runs on push and `pull_request` triggers, executing `GITHUB_TOKEN`-authenticated steps including publishing Docker images (line 351) and coverage reports (line 202). `codeql-analysis.yml` correctly restricts permissions at job level (line 11); the CI/CD workflow does not.

```yaml
.github/workflows/ci.yml
→ 1 │ name: "CI/CD Pipeline"
  2 │ on:
  3 │   push:
```

**Fix:** ◕ [M-059](#m-059) — Enforce server-side authorization on every endpoint (`ci.yml:1`)

**Classification:** Broken Access Control · STRIDE: Elevation of Privilege · [CWE-862](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/862.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/)

### 🟡 Medium (23)

<a id="t-055"></a><a id="f-055"></a>
#### F-055 · Wildcard CORS Permits Credentialed Cross-Origin Requests (server.ts:183)

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-02](#c-02) - Express\.js REST API Backend  ·  **Location:** `server.ts:183`

**Issue:** A malicious web page loaded in the victim's browser sends cross-origin requests to the Juice Shop API, including the victim's authentication cookie or token. The wildcard CORS configuration (`app.use(cors())`) returns Access-Control-Allow-Origin: * and Access-Control-Allow-Credentials: true, allowing the malicious origin to read the API response including the victim's data and perform state-changing operations.

Cross-origin attacker can perform arbitrary state-changing API calls on behalf of a logged-in victim including order placement, address modification, and fund transfers. Combined with credential exposure, this enables account manipulation.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `server.ts:182` registers `app.options('*', cors())` and `server.ts:183` registers `app.use(cors())` with no origin restriction. The known-threats context confirms this is the wildcard CORS finding.

**Fix:** Replace the wildcard CORS origin with an explicit allow-list → ◑ [M-068](#m-068) — Restrict CORS to trusted origins (`server.ts:183`)

**Classification:** Broken Access Control · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-942](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/942.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/)

<a id="t-063"></a><a id="f-063"></a>
#### F-063 · Development Error Handler Returns Stack Traces in Production (server.ts:678)

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-02](#c-02) - Express\.js REST API Backend  ·  **Location:** `server.ts:678`

**Issue:** When any unhandled exception occurs in an Express route, the `errorhandler()` middleware returns the full stack trace, error message, and error object in the HTTP response body. An attacker deliberately triggers errors (malformed input, invalid IDs, injection attempts) to extract file paths, library versions, module names, and internal application state from the error responses.

Attacker learns exact file paths, Node\.js module names and versions, Sequelize query structure, and database error messages. This information significantly accelerates targeted exploitation of injection vulnerabilities.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `server.ts:678` registers `app.use(errorhandler())` which is the Express development error handler from the 'errorhandler' npm package. This package explicitly returns HTML or JSON responses containing the full `Error.stack`. The SQL injection in `routes/login.ts` and `search.ts` produce error objects that expose Sequelize internals when the query fails.

**Fix:** Replace developer error pages with a generic message in production responses → ◑ [M-070](#m-070) — Return generic error messages to clients (`server.ts:678`)

**Classification:** Error Information Disclosure · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-209](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/209.html) · [OWASP A02:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A02_2025-Security_Misconfiguration/)

<a id="t-065"></a><a id="f-065"></a>
#### F-065 · No rate limiting on login endpoint enables credential (routes/login.ts:32)

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-01](#c-01) - Authentication & Session Surface  ·  **Location:** `routes/login.ts:32`

**Issue:** An attacker sends unlimited POST `/rest/user/login` requests without any delay or lockout; each request triggers an `MD5` hash computation and a raw SQL query to SQLite, exhausting server-side I/O and CPU resources, and enabling automated credential stuffing or password brute-force against all accounts at a rate bounded only by network throughput. An attacker can exhaust server resources by flooding POST `/rest/user/login`, causing service unavailability for legitimate users.

In parallel, unlimited attempts enable automated credential stuffing with breach-database wordlists, allowing account takeover at scale. All user accounts are at risk given that passwords are hashed with `MD5` (trivially reversed offline after a DB dump).

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `routes/login.ts:32` defines the login route handler with no rate-limit or throttle middleware applied before the handler body. No import of any rate-limiting library (`express-rate-limit`, express-slow-down) is present in the file. The architecture context explicitly confirms: 'No brute-force protection on the login endpoint itself.'.

**Fix:** Apply rate limiting and lock-out thresholds on authentication endpoints → ◑ [M-072](#m-072) — Rate-limit and lock out repeated authentication attempts (`login.ts:32`)

**Classification:** Broken Authentication · STRIDE: Denial of Service · [CWE-307](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/307.html) · [OWASP A07:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A07_2025-Authentication_Failures/)

<a id="t-066"></a><a id="f-066"></a>
#### F-066 · No Rate Limiting or Account Lockout on Login Endpoint (server.ts:595)

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-02](#c-02) - Express\.js REST API Backend  ·  **Location:** `server.ts:595`

**Issue:** An unauthenticated attacker sends unlimited POST `/rest/user/login` requests with password candidates against a target email address. The endpoint has no rate limiting middleware, no CAPTCHA, and no account lockout.

Attacker can compromise any user account whose password is in a common wordlist, or carry out credential stuffing with leaked credential pairs at full network speed. The `MD5` password hashing (`lib/insecurity.ts:43`) further reduces the cost of offline cracking once a hash is recovered.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `server.ts:595` registers `app.post('/rest/user/login', login())` with no `rateLimit()` middleware. The `rateLimit` import at `server.ts:31` is used only for reset-password and 2FA endpoints (lines 343, 458, 465, 471). The login function at `routes/login.ts` has no lockout logic.

**Fix:** Apply rate limiting and lock-out thresholds on authentication endpoints → ◑ [M-073](#m-073) — Rate-limit and lock out repeated authentication attempts (`server.ts:595`)

**Classification:** Broken Authentication · STRIDE: Denial of Service · [CWE-307](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/307.html) · [OWASP A07:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A07_2025-Authentication_Failures/)

<a id="t-067"></a><a id="f-067"></a>
#### F-067 · LLM10 Unbounded Token Consumption on Chat Endpoint (routes/chat.ts:207)

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-02](#c-02) - Express\.js REST API Backend  ·  **Location:** `routes/chat.ts:207`

**Issue:** LLM10 - An unauthenticated attacker sends rapid successive POST `/rest/chat` requests with messages designed to trigger all 10 tool-call steps per request. The endpoint has no per-user or global rate limiting.

Unconstrained LLM API calls result in cost amplification and service degradation. A single attacker sending 100 requests/minute, each consuming 10 tool-call steps, can generate thousands of dollars in LLM API costs and exhaust the provider's rate limit, denying service to legitimate users.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `server.ts:637` registers `app.post('/rest/chat', utils.asyncHandler(chat()))` without any `rateLimit()` middleware. `routes/chat.ts:207` uses `stopWhen: stepCountIs`(10) to bound steps but no per-request token limit or per-user request frequency limit is configured. `routes/chat.ts:57-78` define Prometheus counters tracking token usage but no circuit-breaker or quota enforcement.

**Fix:** Bound the request rate and the per-request resource budget on this endpoint → ◑ [M-074](#m-074) — Rate-limit expensive requests and bound input size (`chat.ts:207`)

**Classification:** Denial of Service · STRIDE: Denial of Service · [CWE-400](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/400.html) · [OWASP A06:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A06_2025-Insecure_Design/)

<a id="t-068"></a><a id="f-068"></a>
#### F-068 · Unbounded wallet derivation per request without rate (routes/checkKeys.ts:9)

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-07](#c-07) - Web3 / Wallet / NFT Surface  ·  **Location:** `routes/checkKeys.ts:9`

**Issue:** An unauthenticated attacker floods POST `/rest/web3/submitKey` with concurrent requests. Each request triggers a full BIP39 `HDNodeWallet.fromPhrase()` derivation (line 9-13), a CPU-intensive PBKDF2-based operation.

Sustained request flood causes CPU saturation on the Node\.js process, increasing latency and causing timeouts on all co-hosted routes including authentication and purchase flows.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - Line 9 performs a dynamic import of ethers; lines 10-14 run `HDNodeWallet.fromPhrase(mnemonic)` on every request. Controls context confirms no authentication on this endpoint, and no rate-limiting middleware is visible in the handler.

**Fix:** Bound the request rate and the per-request resource budget on this endpoint → ◑ [M-075](#m-075) — Rate-limit expensive requests and bound input size (`checkKeys.ts:9`)

**Classification:** Denial of Service · STRIDE: Denial of Service · [CWE-400](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/400.html) · [OWASP A06:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A06_2025-Insecure_Design/)

<a id="t-069"></a><a id="f-069"></a>
#### F-069 · Client-side-only AdminGuard enforces no server authorization (app.guard.ts:52)

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Angular Web Application (SPA)  ·  **Location:** `frontend/src/app/app.guard.ts:52`

**Issue:** An attacker forges a JWT with role='admin' in `localStorage` (or obtains any valid admin JWT), browses to /#`/administration`, and the Angular AdminGuard at line 52 decodes the client-side token without signature verification and grants access; because the guard exists only in the browser, it imposes no barrier to direct API calls regardless of token validity. Any user who places a valid-or-crafted admin JWT in `localStorage` can navigate the admin UI; the real risk is that API-level access depends entirely on server-side authorization, which must be verified separately - the SPA guard provides a false sense of access control for the admin route.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `AdminGuard.canActivate()` at line 52 calls `this.loginGuard.tokenDecode()` which uses `jwtDecode` (client-only, no signature verification) and checks `payload.data.role` === `roles.admin` at line 54; the guard is declared in `app.routing.ts` but enforces nothing on API endpoints - it is a UI routing guard only.

**Fix:** ◑ [M-076](#m-076) — Enforce authorization on the server (`app.guard.ts:52`)

**Classification:** Broken Access Control · STRIDE: Elevation of Privilege · [CWE-602](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/602.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/)

<a id="t-070"></a><a id="f-070"></a>
#### F-070 · Unrated TOTP verify endpoint allows 2FA brute-force (routes/2fa.ts:31)

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-01](#c-01) - Authentication & Session Surface  ·  **Location:** `routes/2fa.ts:31`

**Issue:** An attacker who knows a 2FA-enrolled user's password authenticates at POST `/rest/user/login`, receives a `tmpToken`, then iterates all 1,000,000 six-digit TOTP codes against POST `/rest/2fa/verify` with no rate limiting; within the 30-second TOTP window an enumeration attack finds the valid code and receives a full session JWT, bypassing the second factor. The 2FA control is rendered ineffective against an attacker who already has the user's password: **** (9 chars) enumeration of all TOTP values within a 30-second window bypasses the second factor and yields a full session token with the user's role, including admin.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `routes/2fa.ts:31` calls `verifySync` to validate `totpToken` against the TOTP secret without any rate-limit or attempt-count check in the handler or its imports. The route accepts arbitrary numeric input for `totpToken` in `req.body` on each request.

**Fix:** Apply rate limiting and lock-out thresholds on authentication endpoints → ◑ [M-077](#m-077) — Rate-limit and lock out repeated authentication attempts (`2fa.ts:31`)

**Classification:** Broken Authentication · STRIDE: Elevation of Privilege · [CWE-307](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/307.html) · [OWASP A07:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A07_2025-Authentication_Failures/)

<a id="t-071"></a><a id="f-071"></a>
#### F-071 · UpdateAuthenticatedUsers re-inserts valid JWT bypassing (lib/insecurity.ts:191)

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium - elevated to Critical as a verified attack-chain keystone in AC-T-003 (Privilege Escalation to Admin via JWT Algorithm Confusion); see [§9](#9-abuse-cases)  ·  **Component:** [C-01](#c-01) - Authentication & Session Surface  ·  **Location:** `lib/insecurity.ts:191`

**Issue:** After a user logs out and their token is removed from the in-memory `authenticatedUsers` `tokenMap`, an attacker in possession of the user's previously issued JWT presents it in a subsequent request; the `updateAuthenticatedUsers` middleware calls `jwt.verify()` and, finding the token absent from the map, re-inserts it at line 193, restoring a full active session without any re-authentication. A logged-out JWT remains usable for its full 6-hour lifetime against any route protected by this middleware.

An attacker who obtains a victim's token (via XSS, network interception, or log exfiltration) retains access even after the victim explicitly logs out. The in-memory map also loses all sessions on server restart, meaning all previously-issued non-expired JWTs regain active status after a restart.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `lib/insecurity.ts:188-200` shows `updateAuthenticatedUsers()` calling `jwt.verify()`; on success, if `authenticatedUsers.get(token)` === undefined the token is re-inserted into the map via `authenticatedUsers.put()`. The check at line 193 treats map absence as justification for reinsertion rather than as a revocation signal.

**Fix:** ◑ [M-078](#m-078) — Treat tokenMap absence as revocation in updateAuthenticatedUsers; require re-login for (`insecurity.ts:191`)

**Classification:** Broken Authentication · STRIDE: Elevation of Privilege · [CWE-613](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/613.html) · [OWASP A07:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A07_2025-Authentication_Failures/)

<a id="t-064"></a><a id="f-064"></a>
#### F-064 · LLM02 Chatbot Returns Unfiltered User Order Data Including (routes/chat.ts:164)

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-02](#c-02) - Express\.js REST API Backend  ·  **Location:** `routes/chat.ts:164`

**Issue:** LLM02 - An authenticated attacker sends a chat message asking the bot to describe all order details for their order. The `getOrderById` tool returns a full order object from the MarsDB collection including the masked email pattern (vowels replaced with *), product list, total price, and bonus.

Authenticated user can extract their full order history with partial email information via chatbot responses. If the attacker has knowledge of other order IDs (e.g., from a predictable ID format), they may access other users' order data via the unverified JWT path (🟠 [F-015](#f-015) — Unverified JWT Decode in Chat Enables Order Impersonation (`routes/chat.ts:45`)).

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `routes/chat.ts:164-168`: the `getOrderById` execute function calls `db.ordersCollection.findOne({ orderId })` and returns the full order object to the model. No field filtering or PII scrubbing is applied before including the result in the LLM context. The model then surfaces all returned fields in its response.

**Fix:** ◑ [M-071](#m-071) — Filter order object fields before returning to the LLM context in (`chat.ts:164`)

**Classification:** Error Information Disclosure · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-359](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/359.html) · [OWASP A02:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A02_2025-Security_Misconfiguration/)

<a id="t-050"></a><a id="f-050"></a>
#### F-050 · Open Redirect (routes/redirect.ts:19)

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-07](#c-07) - Web3 / Wallet / NFT Surface  ·  **Location:** `routes/redirect.ts:19`

**Trust boundary gap:** [tb-1](#tb-1) 🌐 External - external → express-api · Authentication: Redirect request crosses external-to-express-api boundary; no authentication gate prevents unauthenticated actors from triggering the redirect<br>_⬆ Confirmed internet ingress at tb-1 raised this finding: Medium → Medium._

**Issue:** An attacker crafts a URL `https://juice-shop.example/rest/redirect?to=https://evil.com` that bypasses the `isRedirectAllowed` allowlist check. A victim who clicks this link is silently forwarded to the attacker-controlled domain, enabling phishing or OAuth token harvesting.

Victims are redirected to attacker-controlled sites under the trusted application domain, enabling credential phishing and exfiltration of session tokens passed as URL parameters in OAuth flows.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `redirect.ts:15` assigns `query.to` directly to `toUrl`; `redirect.ts:19` calls `res.redirect(toUrl)` after `isRedirectAllowed` check. The `redirectChallenge` challenge (line 18) is solved when `isUnintendedRedirect` returns true, confirming the allowlist can be bypassed. Known threat entry confirms this is an open redirect.

**Fix:** ◑ [M-063](#m-063) — Validate redirect targets against an allowlist (`redirect.ts:19`)

**Classification:** Open Redirect · STRIDE: Spoofing · [CWE-601](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/601.html) · [OWASP A01:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A01_2025-Broken_Access_Control/)

<a id="t-051"></a><a id="f-051"></a>
#### F-051 · Stored XSS (last-login-ip.component.ts:39)

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-03](#c-03) - Angular Web Application (SPA)  ·  **Location:** `frontend/src/app/last-login-ip/last-login-ip.component.ts:39`

**Weakness:** [W-009](#w-009) - Frontend rendering lacks enforced output encoding

**Issue:** An attacker logs in with a spoofed X-Forwarded-For header containing an HTML payload; the backend stores this value as `lastLoginIp` in the issued JWT; when the victim loads the Last Login IP page, `parseAuthToken()` decodes the JWT at line 36 and passes `lastLoginIp` to `bypassSecurityTrustHtml` at line 39, executing the payload in the browser. If the backend accepts attacker-controlled IP headers, an attacker can inject JavaScript into their own JWT claim that executes when they view the Last Login IP page, enabling self-XSS or chained exploitation if an admin views the page on behalf of a user.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `parseAuthToken()` at line 36 calls `jwtDecode(token)` and at line 39 applies `this.sanitizer`.`bypassSecurityTrustHtml`('<small>' + `payload.data.lastLoginIp` + '</small>') without any encoding of the IP value from the JWT claim.

**Fix:** Output-encode untrusted strings at every sink and remove all `bypassSecurityTrustHtml` calls → ◑ [M-064](#m-064) — Encode output instead of bypassing the framework sanitizer (`last-login-ip.component.ts:39`)

**Classification:** Cross-Site Scripting (XSS) · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-79](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/79.html) · [OWASP A05:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A05_2025-Injection/)

<a id="t-056"></a><a id="f-056"></a>
#### F-056 · Challenge-gated weak sanitization stores XSS HTML in Users (models/user.ts:50)

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-02](#c-02) - Express\.js REST API Backend  ·  **Location:** `models/user.ts:50`

**Weakness:** [W-009](#w-009) - Frontend rendering lacks enforced output encoding

**Issue:** When `persistedXssUserChallenge` is enabled, the username setter applies `security.sanitizeLegacy` instead of `security.sanitizeSecure`. `sanitizeLegacy` (`lib/insecurity.ts:61`) strips only patterns matching `<word-chars>``<non-word>``<word-char>` via a single regex pass; payloads that avoid this pattern (e.g. `<img src=x onerror=alert(1)>`) survive and are stored verbatim in the `Users.username` column.

Attacker stores a persistent XSS payload in `Users.username`; any page rendering usernames without encoding - including admin UI - executes the payload in the victim's browser, enabling session hijacking or account takeover.

**Evidence:** ◌ ambiguous - `models/user.ts:50` conditionally calls `security.sanitizeLegacy` whose regex does not neutralize all HTML injection vectors; the caller controls which sanitizer applies via a feature flag.

**Fix:** Output-encode untrusted strings at every sink and remove all `bypassSecurityTrustHtml` calls → ◑ [M-008](#m-008) — Encode output instead of bypassing the framework sanitizer (`user.ts:50`) · ◑ [M-069](#m-069) — Encode output instead of bypassing the framework sanitizer (`user.ts:50`)

**Classification:** Cross-Site Scripting (XSS) · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-79](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/79.html) · [OWASP A05:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A05_2025-Injection/)

<a id="t-002"></a><a id="f-002"></a>
#### F-002 · Missing Security Event Logging

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-01](#c-01) - Authentication & Session Surface  ·  **Location:** Multiple locations (7)

**Instances (7):** 🟡 `routes/login.ts:18`, 🟡 `server.ts:338`, 🟡 `routes/chat.ts:225`, 🟡 `routes/updateProductReviews.ts:17`, 🟢 `lib/challengeUtils.ts:30`, 🟢 `models/index.ts:42`, 🟢 `routes/checkKeys.ts:6`

**Issue:** An attacker performs repeated failed login attempts, a successful SQL-injection-based login, or a password reset on a victim account; because no append-only security audit log is written in any of the auth route handlers, the actions cannot be attributed, reconstructed, or raised as alerts during incident response. Successful and failed login events, 2FA verifications, and password resets leave no auditable record outside the mutable Users table.

An attacker who exploits the SQL injection or uses a forged JWT cannot be detected from logs after the fact; account-takeover forensics, compliance audits, and real-time alerting are all unavailable.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - No logging library import or security event write call exists in `routes/login.ts`, `routes/2fa.ts`, `routes/resetPassword.ts`, or `lib/insecurity.ts`. The only auth-related persistence is `lastLoginIp` written to the Users table, which is a mutable DB column rather than an append-only audit trail.

**Fix:** ◑ [M-017](#m-017) — Add security audit logging (`login.ts:18`)

**Classification:** Missing Audit Logging & Accountability · STRIDE: Repudiation · [CWE-778](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/778.html) · [OWASP A09:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A09_2025-Security_Logging_and_Alerting_Failures/)

<a id="t-052"></a><a id="f-052"></a>
#### F-052 · Unpinned GitHub Actions (codeql-analysis.yml:23)

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-08](#c-08) - CI/CD Pipeline  ·  **Location:** `.github/workflows/codeql-analysis.yml:23`

**Issue:** An attacker who gains control of the github/codeql-action or coverallsapp/github-action GitHub repositories pushes a new commit to the mutable tag (v3 or v2). The next workflow run fetches and executes the attacker-controlled code with access to CI secrets and `GITHUB_TOKEN`.

Attacker can execute arbitrary code in the CI environment with access to `GITHUB_TOKEN`, DockerHub credentials, and other CI secrets, enabling artifact backdooring or secret exfiltration.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `codeql-analysis.yml` lines 23, 34, 36 reference github/codeql-action/init@v3, autobuild@v3, and analyze@v3 without SHA pinning. `ci.yml` line 202 references coverallsapp/github-action@v2 without SHA pinning.

**Fix:** Replace the unmaintained dependency with a maintained equivalent or fork it under ownership → ◑ [M-065](#m-065) — Pin the container base image to an immutable digest (`codeql-analysis.yml:23`)

**Classification:** Supply-Chain Integrity · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-1104](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/1104.html) · [OWASP A03:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A03_2025-Software_Supply_Chain_Failures/)

<a id="t-053"></a><a id="f-053"></a>
#### F-053 · Unpinned container base image

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-08](#c-08) - CI/CD Pipeline  ·  **Location:** `Dockerfile:1`

**Issue:** An attacker who can influence the node:24 or gcr.io/distroless/nodejs24-debian13 image tags pushes a backdoored image layer. The next Docker build pulls the updated image and the backdoor is embedded in every Juice Shop container published to DockerHub.

A compromised base image introduces backdoors into every container build and distributes them to all Juice Shop container users via the public DockerHub registry.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `Dockerfile` line 1 uses `FROM node:24` (mutable tag, no digest). `Dockerfile` line 22 uses `FROM gcr.io/distroless/nodejs24-debian13` (mutable tag, no digest). Both base images are pulled without digest verification on each build.

**Fix:** Replace the unmaintained dependency with a maintained equivalent or fork it under ownership → ◑ [M-066](#m-066) — Pin the container base image to an immutable digest (`Dockerfile:1`)

**Classification:** Supply-Chain Integrity · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-1104](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/1104.html) · [OWASP A03:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A03_2025-Software_Supply_Chain_Failures/)

<a id="t-054"></a><a id="f-054"></a>
#### F-054 · Npm install --unsafe-perm in Docker build stage

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-08](#c-08) - CI/CD Pipeline  ·  **Location:** `Dockerfile:5`

**Weakness:** [W-008](#w-008) - Build pipeline trusts mutable third-party references

**Issue:** `Dockerfile` line 5 runs `npm install --omit=dev --unsafe-perm`. This flag causes npm to run install lifecycle scripts (preinstall, install, postinstall) as root instead of dropping to a lower-privilege UID.

A malicious production npm package can write to system paths, install backdoors into the built image, or corrupt application build artifacts before the final container layer is sealed.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified - `Dockerfile` line 5: `RUN npm install --omit=dev --unsafe-perm`. The flag bypasses npm's UID/GID dropping for install scripts, allowing lifecycle hooks to run as root in the build context.

**Fix:** ◑ [M-067](#m-067) — Set least-privilege CI workflow permissions (`Dockerfile:5`)

**Classification:** Supply-Chain Integrity · STRIDE: Tampering · [CWE-1357](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/1357.html) · [OWASP A03:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A03_2025-Software_Supply_Chain_Failures/)

<a id="t-057"></a><a id="f-057"></a>
#### F-057 · Container Runs as Root

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-08](#c-08) - CI/CD Pipeline  ·  **Location:** `test/smoke/Dockerfile:1`

**Issue:** Container Runs as Root: Containers running as root expand container-escape blast radius.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** ◑ [M-010](#m-010) — Drop unnecessary privileges in build and runtime (`Dockerfile:1`)

**Classification:** Supply-Chain Integrity · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-250](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/250.html) · [OWASP A03:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A03_2025-Software_Supply_Chain_Failures/)

<a id="t-058"></a><a id="f-058"></a>
#### F-058 · Missing Container Image Signing

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-08](#c-08) - CI/CD Pipeline  ·  **Location:** Multiple locations (15)

**Instances (15):** `.github/workflows/ci.yml:1`, `.github/workflows/codeql-analysis.yml:1`, `.github/workflows/frontend-bundle-analysis.yml:1`, `.github/workflows/lint-fixer.yml:1`, `.github/workflows/lock.yml:1`, `.github/workflows/pr-compliance.yml:1`, `.github/workflows/rebase.yml:1`, `.github/workflows/release.yml:1` … (+7 more)

**Issue:** Missing Container Image Signing: Unsigned container images cannot be verified for provenance - any registry intermediary could substitute them.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** Pin the signature algorithm explicitly and reject `alg:none` and unknown algorithms → ◑ [M-011](#m-011) — Sign and verify release artifacts (`ci.yml:1`)

**Classification:** Broken Authentication · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-347](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/347.html) · [OWASP A07:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A07_2025-Authentication_Failures/)

<a id="t-059"></a><a id="f-059"></a>
#### F-059 · Untrusted npm Install/Postinstall Scripts Enabled

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-08](#c-08) - CI/CD Pipeline  ·  **Location:** Multiple locations (2)

**Instances (2):** `Dockerfile:4`, `package.json:56`

**Issue:** Dependency Install Scripts Run at Build Time: Without `--ignore-scripts`, any compromised dependency publisher can execute postinstall code at build time.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** ◑ [M-012](#m-012) — Disable untrusted package install scripts (`Dockerfile:4`)

**Classification:** Supply-Chain Integrity · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-506](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/506.html) · [OWASP A03:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A03_2025-Software_Supply_Chain_Failures/)

<a id="t-060"></a><a id="f-060"></a>
#### F-060 · Incorrect Permission Assignment

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-08](#c-08) - CI/CD Pipeline  ·  **Location:** Multiple locations (15)

**Instances (15):** `.github/workflows/lock.yml:1`, `.github/workflows/pr-compliance.yml:1`, `.github/workflows/ci.yml:1`, `.github/workflows/codeql-analysis.yml:1`, `.github/workflows/frontend-bundle-analysis.yml:1`, `.github/workflows/lint-fixer.yml:1`, `.github/workflows/rebase.yml:1`, `.github/workflows/release.yml:1` … (+7 more)

**Issue:** Default `GITHUB_TOKEN` Scope Not Restricted: `contents: read` at workflow root forces per-job opt-in for write scopes - minimal privilege by default.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** ◑ [M-013](#m-013) — Apply least-privilege permissions (`lock.yml:1`)

**Classification:** Supply-Chain Integrity · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-732](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/732.html) · [OWASP A03:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A03_2025-Software_Supply_Chain_Failures/)

<a id="t-061"></a><a id="f-061"></a>
#### F-061 · Missing npm Lockfile (package-lock.json)

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-08](#c-08) - CI/CD Pipeline  ·  **Location:** `package-lock.json`

**Issue:** Missing npm Lockfile: Without a lockfile, `npm install` installs different transitive versions on each run - supply-chain attacks become undetectable.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** Replace the unmaintained dependency with a maintained equivalent or fork it under ownership → ◑ [M-014](#m-014) — Pin the container base image to an immutable digest (`package-lock.json:0`)

**Classification:** Supply-Chain Integrity · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-1104](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/1104.html) · [OWASP A03:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A03_2025-Software_Supply_Chain_Failures/)

<a id="t-062"></a><a id="f-062"></a>
#### F-062 · Unpinned Container Base Image

**Severity:** 🟡 Medium  ·  **Component:** [C-08](#c-08) - CI/CD Pipeline  ·  **Location:** `test/smoke/Dockerfile:1`

**Issue:** Unpinned Container Base Image: Tag-only base images (`FROM node:24`) can be silently substituted by a malicious publisher. Digest-pinning (`@sha256:`…) ensures the exact image bytes are used on every build.

**Evidence:** ✓ verified

**Fix:** Replace the unmaintained dependency with a maintained equivalent or fork it under ownership → ◑ [M-015](#m-015) — Pin the container base image to an immutable digest (`Dockerfile:1`)

**Classification:** Supply-Chain Integrity · STRIDE: Information Disclosure · [CWE-1104](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/1104.html) · [OWASP A03:2025](https://owasp.org/Top10/2025/A03_2025-Software_Supply_Chain_Failures/)

## 9. Abuse Cases

_Abuse cases connect findings into attack scenarios. The verdict records whether repository evidence confirms the path. Combined risk is the highest policy-rated risk of the linked findings in the [§8 Findings Register](#8-findings-register); verification adds no severity. Within each verification group, higher-risk cases appear first. Equal risks use the strongest finding's assessed reach, impact and likelihood. Case IDs remain stable and do not indicate priority._

**Confirmed attack paths**

| # | Scenario | Actor | Combined Risk | Verdict |
|--------|------------------------------------|-----------------|-------------|--------------|
| [AC-T-002](#ac-t-002) | Bulk Data Exfiltration via Broken Object<br/>Authorization | Internet Attacker | 🔴 Critical | ⚠ Fully viable |
| [AC-T-004](#ac-t-004) | Privilege Escalation via Mass-Assignment on<br/>Registration | Internet Attacker | 🔴 Critical | ⚠ Fully viable |
| [AC-T-006](#ac-t-006) | Remote Code Execution via Server-Side<br/>Injection | Internet Attacker | 🔴 Critical | ⚠ Fully viable |
| [AC-T-005](#ac-t-005) | Authentication Bypass via Exposed Secret<br/>Material | Internet Attacker | 🔴 Critical | ⚠ Fully viable |
| [AC-T-001](#ac-t-001) | Account Takeover via XSS + Token Hijacking | Internet Attacker | 🟠 High | ⚠ Fully viable |
| [AC-T-003](#ac-t-003) | Privilege Escalation to Admin via JWT<br/>Algorithm Confusion | Internet Attacker | 🔴 Critical | ⚠ Fully viable |

**Unresolved scenarios**

| # | Scenario | Actor | Combined Risk | Verdict |
|--------|------------------------------------|-----------------|-------------|--------------|
| [AC-T-007](#ac-t-007) | Privileged Action via Prompt Injection into<br/>a Tool-Calling Model | Internet Attacker | 🟠 High | ? Inconclusive |

_Verdict: ⚠ Fully viable - no effective control blocks this chain · ◐ Partially blocked - at least one step has a compensating control but the chain is not fully closed · ✓ Mitigated - chain is broken at a verified step · ? Inconclusive - could not be verified end-to-end. Step: ✗ Refuted - the matched pairing does not hold on the code._
