<a id="ac-t-002"></a>
### AC-T-002 — Bulk Data Exfiltration via Broken Object Authorization

> **Source:** mandatory · **Actor:** Internet Attacker - authenticated low-privilege user · **Combined Risk:** 🔴 Critical · **Verdict:** ⚠ Fully viable

**Goal:** Enumerate and exfiltrate other users' records, then escalate own permissions via unguarded mass assignment.

**Prerequisite:** Attacker holds a valid, non-privileged user account.

**Attack chain**

| Step | Finding | Outcome |
|--------|------------------------------------------------|----------------------|
| 1 | 🟠 [F-048](#f-048) — IDOR: review update without ownership check (`updateProductReviews.ts:16`) | Attacker enumerates and retrieves records<br/>for arbitrary object IDs; no ownership<br/>comparison is performed. |
| 2 | 🔴 [F-008](#f-008) — Mass Assignment Allows Admin Role Injection (`verify.ts:52`) | Update endpoint persists an unfiltered `role`<br/>(or equivalent) field supplied in the<br/>request body. |

**Scenario impact**

The ownership gap exposes every record, and the mass-assignment gap lets the same low-privilege actor self-elevate - together they turn a single compromised account into full tenant data access and role escalation.

**Blocking mitigations**

Implementing any single mitigation below severs the chain at the named step, so the end-to-end abuse can no longer complete:

- ◑ [M-007](#m-007) — Enforce object-level (ownership) authorization (**P3**): remediating 🟠 [F-048](#f-048) — IDOR: review update without ownership check breaks the chain at **Step 1**, removing the link the rest of the chain depends on.
- ◕ [M-061](#m-061) — Enforce object-level (ownership) authorization (**P2**): remediating 🟠 [F-048](#f-048) — IDOR: review update without ownership check breaks the chain at **Step 1**, removing the link the rest of the chain depends on.
- ● [M-023](#m-023) — Allowlist client-controlled fields (**P1**): remediating 🔴 [F-008](#f-008) — Mass Assignment Allows Admin Role Injection breaks the chain at **Step 2**, removing the link the rest of the chain depends on.

---

<a id="ac-t-004"></a>
### AC-T-004 — Privilege Escalation via Mass-Assignment on Registration

> **Source:** mandatory · **Actor:** Internet Attacker - unauthenticated external attacker · **Combined Risk:** 🔴 Critical · **Verdict:** ⚠ Fully viable

**Goal:** Obtain an administrator account without any existing privilege.

**Prerequisite:** Self-registration is open (one unauthenticated POST).

**Attack chain**

| Step | Finding | Outcome |
|--------|------------------------------------------------|----------------------|
| 1 | 🔴 [F-008](#f-008) — Mass Assignment Allows Admin Role Injection (`verify.ts:52`) | The account-creation handler persists the<br/>request body wholesale, so a client-supplied<br/>`role` (or `isAdmin`) field is written verbatim. |

**Scenario impact**

A single unauthenticated request mints an admin account when the registration handler trusts a client-supplied role field - the most direct full-compromise path in role-based apps with open sign-up.

**Blocking mitigations**

Implementing any single mitigation below severs the chain at the named step, so the end-to-end abuse can no longer complete:

- ● [M-023](#m-023) — Allowlist client-controlled fields (**P1**): remediating 🔴 [F-008](#f-008) — Mass Assignment Allows Admin Role Injection breaks the chain at **Step 1**, removing the link the rest of the chain depends on.

---

<a id="ac-t-006"></a>
### AC-T-006 — Remote Code Execution via Server-Side Injection

> **Source:** mandatory · **Actor:** Internet Attacker - unauthenticated external attacker · **Combined Risk:** 🔴 Critical · **Verdict:** ⚠ Fully viable

**Goal:** Execute arbitrary code in the application process.

**Prerequisite:** An input reaches a server-side interpreter / template / eval.

**Attack chain**

| Step | Finding | Outcome |
|--------|------------------------------------------------|----------------------|
| 1 | 🔴 [F-006](#f-006) — Server-Side Code Execution (`userProfile.ts:61`) | Attacker-controlled input is passed to `eval`,<br/>a server-side template engine, an unsafe<br/>sandbox, or an unsafe deserializer. |

**Scenario impact**

A single injection into a server-side interpreter yields code execution in the application process - the highest-impact outcome, granting full filesystem and network access from one unauthenticated request.

**Blocking mitigations**

Implementing any single mitigation below severs the chain at the named step, so the end-to-end abuse can no longer complete:

- ● [M-021](#m-021) — Remove server-side evaluation of untrusted input (**P1**): remediating 🔴 [F-006](#f-006) — Server-Side Code Execution breaks the chain at **Step 1**, removing the link the rest of the chain depends on.

<a id="ac-t-005"></a>
### AC-T-005 — Authentication Bypass via Exposed Secret Material

> **Source:** mandatory · **Actor:** Internet Attacker - unauthenticated external attacker · **Combined Risk:** 🔴 Critical · **Verdict:** ⚠ Fully viable

**Goal:** Forge trusted tokens / credentials and impersonate any user.

**Prerequisite:** Signing material or other secrets are reachable (committed to a public repo, served by an unauthenticated route, or in an exposed directory).

**Attack chain**

| Step | Finding | Outcome |
|--------|------------------------------------------------|----------------------|
| 1 | 🟠 [F-011](#f-011) — Hardcoded RSA private key enables offline JWT forgery (`insecurity.ts:23`) | A private key, signing secret, or credential<br/>file is committed to the source repository<br/>or served without authentication. |
| 2 | 🟠 [F-012](#f-012) — Missing JWT algorithm whitelist (`insecurity.ts:54`) | The exposed key/secret is the same one the<br/>server trusts, so a token signed with it (or<br/>the leaked credential) is accepted as<br/>authentic. |

**Scenario impact**

Exposed signing material collapses the entire authentication boundary: any attacker who reads the key can mint a valid token for any identity or role, with no credential ever required.

**Blocking mitigations**

Implementing any single mitigation below severs the chain at the named step, so the end-to-end abuse can no longer complete:

- ◕ [M-026](#m-026) — Move cryptographic keys to a managed secret store (**P2**): remediating 🟠 [F-011](#f-011) — Hardcoded RSA private key enables offline JWT forgery breaks the chain at **Step 1**, removing the link the rest of the chain depends on.
- ◕ [M-027](#m-027) — Enforce JWT signature and algorithm verification (**P2**): remediating 🟠 [F-012](#f-012) — Missing JWT algorithm whitelist breaks the chain at **Step 2**, removing the link the rest of the chain depends on.

---

<a id="ac-t-001"></a>
### AC-T-001 — Account Takeover via XSS + Token Hijacking

> **Source:** mandatory · **Actor:** Internet Attacker - unauthenticated external attacker · **Combined Risk:** 🟠 High · **Verdict:** ⚠ Fully viable

**Goal:** Obtain persistent authenticated access as an arbitrary user without valid credentials.

**Prerequisite:** Attacker can get markup rendered in a victim's session, through stored content (e.g. feedback, comments, profile fields) or a crafted link.

**Attack chain**

| Step | Finding | Outcome |
|--------|------------------------------------------------|----------------------|
| 1 | 🟠 [F-017](#f-017) — Stored XSS (`about.component.ts:119`) | Attacker JavaScript executes in the victim's<br/>browser session. |
| 2 | 🟠 [F-031](#f-031) — JWT stored in localStorage (`login.component.ts:105`) | Token exfiltrated from local/session storage<br/>via the Step 1 payload. |
| 3 | 🟠 [F-078](#f-078) — Token flow accepts a stolen bearer token without (`login.component.ts:152`) | Exfiltrated token accepted for a new<br/>session; absence of token binding / PKCE<br/>removes the last server-side revocation<br/>opportunity. |

**Scenario impact**

Individually the XSS sink and the web-readable token storage rate below Critical, but chained they form a repeatable credential-theft path: a single stored payload causes indefinite session compromise for every user who views the affected page.

**Blocking mitigations**

Implementing any single mitigation below severs the chain at the named step, so the end-to-end abuse can no longer complete:

- ◕ [M-032](#m-032) — Encode output instead of bypassing the framework sanitizer (**P2**): remediating 🟠 [F-017](#f-017) — Stored XSS breaks the chain at **Step 1**, removing the link the rest of the chain depends on.
- ◕ [M-045](#m-045) — Store session tokens in HttpOnly, Secure cookies (**P2**): remediating 🟠 [F-031](#f-031) — JWT stored in localStorage breaks the chain at **Step 2**, removing the link the rest of the chain depends on.
- ◕ [M-079](#m-079) — Harden the authentication flow (**P2**): remediating 🟠 [F-078](#f-078) — Token flow accepts a stolen bearer token without breaks the chain at **Step 3**, removing the link the rest of the chain depends on.

---

<a id="ac-t-003"></a>
### AC-T-003 — Privilege Escalation to Admin via JWT Algorithm Confusion

> **Source:** mandatory · **Actor:** Internet Attacker - unauthenticated external attacker · **Combined Risk:** 🔴 Critical · **Verdict:** ⚠ Fully viable

**Goal:** Forge an admin-role JWT without knowledge of the signing secret.

**Prerequisite:** Attacker can obtain any valid JWT issued by the system (e.g. by registering a free account).

**Attack chain**

| Step | Finding | Outcome |
|--------|------------------------------------------------|----------------------|
| 1 | 🟡 [F-071](#f-071) — UpdateAuthenticatedUsers re-inserts valid JWT bypassing (`insecurity.ts:191`) | Verifier accepts attacker-chosen `alg` (e.g.<br/>`none` or HMAC-with-public-key), allowing<br/>token re-signing without the secret. |
| 2 | _no matching finding_<br/>`lib/insecurity.ts:159` | Forged `role: admin` claim is accepted as<br/>authoritative because the role is not<br/>re-fetched from the database per request. |

**Scenario impact**

Algorithm confusion alone yields a forgeable token; trusting the in-token role claim turns that forgery into instant admin access - neither gap is Critical in isolation, but the chain is a full authentication bypass.

**Blocking mitigations**

Implementing any single mitigation below severs the chain at the named step, so the end-to-end abuse can no longer complete:

- ◑ [M-078](#m-078) — Treat tokenMap absence as revocation in updateAuthenticatedUsers; require re-login for (**P3**): remediating 🟡 [F-071](#f-071) — UpdateAuthenticatedUsers re-inserts valid JWT bypassing breaks the chain at **Step 1**, removing the link the rest of the chain depends on.

---

<a id="ac-t-007"></a>
### AC-T-007 — Privileged Action via Prompt Injection into a Tool-Calling Model

> **Source:** mandatory · **Actor:** Internet Attacker - unauthenticated external attacker · **Combined Risk:** 🟠 High · **Verdict:** ? Inconclusive

**Goal:** Make the model invoke a privileged tool or return data the attacker is not entitled to, using the application's own authority.

**Prerequisite:** Attacker can place text into a conversation, document, or record that the application later sends to a language model.

**Attack chain**

| Step | Finding | Outcome |
|--------|------------------------------------------------|----------------------|
| 1 | 🟠 [F-027](#f-027) — LLM01 Prompt Injection Bypasses Coupon Discount Policy (`chat.ts:177`) | The handler builds the prompt by<br/>interpolating request or stored text into<br/>the same string as the system instructions,<br/>so attacker text is read as instruction. |
| 2 | 🟠 [F-037](#f-037) — Unauthenticated access to all order records (`trackOrder.ts:18`) | Tools exposed to the model run with the<br/>application's authority; a manipulated model<br/>turn therefore performs actions the caller<br/>could not. |

**Scenario impact**

Text an attacker can place anywhere the application later feeds to the model becomes instruction, and the tools behind that model run with the application's authority rather than the caller's - so a comment or a stored record reaches actions the attacker was never entitled to.

**Blocking mitigations**

Implementing any single mitigation below severs the chain at the named step, so the end-to-end abuse can no longer complete:

- ◕ [M-041](#m-041) — Add server-side discount cap validation in generateCoupon tool execute handler (**P2**): remediating 🟠 [F-027](#f-027) — LLM01 Prompt Injection Bypasses Coupon Discount Policy breaks the chain at **Step 1**, removing the link the rest of the chain depends on.
- ◕ [M-050](#m-050) — Enforce server-side authorization on every endpoint (**P2**): remediating 🟠 [F-037](#f-037) — Unauthenticated access to all order records breaks the chain at **Step 2**, removing the link the rest of the chain depends on.

### P1 — Immediate

<a id="m-018"></a>
#### M-018 — Use parameterized database queries

**Addresses:**

- 🔴 [F-003](#f-003) — SQL injection (`login.ts:34`)

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-002](#w-002)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-89](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/89.html) - SQL Injection

**Priority:** P1 - Immediate · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `routes/login.ts:34`

**How:**

1. Replace the raw query at `routes/login.ts:34` with: `UserModel.findOne({ where: { email: req.body.email || '', password: security.hash(req.body.password || ''), deletedAt: null } })`
2. Remove the `models.sequelize.query` call and the direct template literal interpolation; the Sequelize ORM applies parameterized binding automatically.
3. Add an integration test that submits \"' OR '1'='1'--\" as the email and verifies HTTP 401 is returned rather than a successful authentication.

_Example implementation in `routes/login.ts:34`: it applies **Use parameterized database queries**. The ordered steps above remain authoritative._

```typescript
// Reject string interpolation; use parameter binding.
await sequelize.query(
  'SELECT * FROM Users WHERE email = :email AND password = :password',
  { replacements: { email, password: hash(password) }, type: QueryTypes.SELECT }
)
```

**Verification:** POST `/rest/user/login` with email=\"' OR '1'='1'--\" and any password; expect HTTP 401 and no JWT in the response body.

**Reference:** [OWASP Cheat Sheet: SQL Injection Prevention Cheat Sheet](https://cheatsheetseries.owasp.org/cheatsheets/SQL_Injection_Prevention_Cheat_Sheet.html)

---

<a id="m-019"></a>
#### M-019 — Enforce object-level (ownership) authorization

**Addresses:**

- 🔴 [F-004](#f-004) — Insecure Direct Object Reference (`address.ts:18`)

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-003](#w-003)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-639](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/639.html) - Authorization Bypass Through User-Controlled Key (IDOR)

**Priority:** P1 - Immediate · **Effort:** Medium · **File:** `routes/address.ts:18`

**How:** Replace `req.body.UserId`/`userId`/`ownerId` with `req.user.id` (or equivalent session-derived identity) in every WHERE/filter clause.

1. Add a regression test (or CI check) that fails on the vulnerable pattern and passes once the fix is in place.

_Example implementation in `routes/address.ts:18`: it applies **Enforce object-level (ownership) authorization**. The ordered steps above remain authoritative._

```typescript
// Ownership check before touching a resource.
const basket = await Basket.findByPk(req.params.id)
if (!basket || basket.UserId !== req.user.id) return res.status(403).end()
```

**Verification:** Re-run the AUTHZ-001 scanner check; it reports no match at `routes/address.ts:18`, and the regression test passes.

---

<a id="m-020"></a>
#### M-020 — Use parameterized database queries

**Addresses:**

- 🔴 [F-005](#f-005) — SQL Injection in Product Search (`search.ts:23`)

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-002](#w-002)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-89](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/89.html) - SQL Injection

**Priority:** P1 - Immediate · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `routes/search.ts:23`

**How:**

1. Replace the `models.sequelize.query` call in `routes/search.ts:23` with: `ProductModel.findAll({ where: { [Op.or]: [{ name: { [Op.like]: '%' + criteria + '%' }}, { description: { [Op.like]: '%' + criteria + '%' }}], deletedAt: null }, order: [['name', 'ASC']] })`
2. Add a test that sends a UNION SELECT payload and verifies the response contains only product fields

_Example implementation in `routes/search.ts:23`: it applies **Use parameterized database queries**. The ordered steps above remain authoritative._

```typescript
// Reject string interpolation; use parameter binding.
await sequelize.query(
  'SELECT * FROM Users WHERE email = :email AND password = :password',
  { replacements: { email, password: hash(password) }, type: QueryTypes.SELECT }
)
```

**Verification:** Send GET `/rest/products/search?q=`')) UNION SELECT email,password,NULL,NULL,NULL,NULL-- and verify the response contains no user data

**Reference:** [sequelize.org: Model Querying Finders](https://sequelize.org/docs/v6/core-concepts/model-querying-finders/)

---

<a id="m-021"></a>
#### M-021 — Remove server-side evaluation of untrusted input

**Addresses:**

- 🔴 [F-006](#f-006) — Server-Side Code Execution (`userProfile.ts:61`)

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-001](#w-001)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-94](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/94.html) - Code Injection

**Priority:** P1 - Immediate · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `routes/userProfile.ts:61`

**How:**

1. Remove lines 54-67 in `routes/userProfile.ts` that perform the #{...} pattern match and eval call
2. Ensure username is HTML-entity encoded before template interpolation using `entities.encode(username)`
3. Add a test that sets a username to #{`process.env.NODE_ENV`} and verifies GET `/profile` returns the literal string rather than an evaluated value

_Example implementation in `routes/userProfile.ts:61`: it applies **Remove server-side evaluation of untrusted input**. The ordered steps above remain authoritative._

```javascript
// Replace `eval()` / sandbox with a strict whitelist parser.
import { evaluate } from 'mathjs'
const allowed = /^[\d+\-*/().\s]+$/
if (!allowed.test(expr)) throw new Error('invalid expression')
return evaluate(expr)
```

**Verification:** Set username to #{`require('os').hostname()`} via profile update, then GET `/profile`; verify the literal string is displayed, not the hostname

**Reference:** [CWE-94: Improper Control of Generation of Code (Code Injection)](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/94.html)

---

<a id="m-022"></a>
#### M-022 — Disable XML external entity (XXE) resolution

**Addresses:**

- 🔴 [F-007](#f-007) — XXE (`fileUpload.ts:83`)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-611](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/611.html) - XML External Entity (XXE)

**Priority:** P1 - Immediate · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `routes/fileUpload.ts:83`

**How:**

1. Change `routes/fileUpload.ts:83` to use `noent:false`: `libxml.parseXml(data, { noblanks: true, noent: false, nocdata: true })`
2. Add `security.isAuthorized()` middleware to the `/file-upload` route in `server.ts`
3. Add a test that uploads an XML file with an external entity and verifies the entity is not resolved

_Example implementation in `routes/fileUpload.ts:83`: it applies **Disable XML external entity (XXE) resolution**. The ordered steps above remain authoritative._

```javascript
// libxmljs2 — disable external entities and network fetch.
const doc = libxmljs.parseXml(xmlBuf, { noent: false, nonet: true, recover: false })
if (doc.errors.length) throw new Error('xml rejected')
```

**Verification:** Upload an XML file containing an external entity referencing `file:///etc/passwd`; verify the response does not contain file content and returns HTTP 410 without the file data

**Reference:** [OWASP: XML External Entity (xxe) Processing](https://owasp.org/www-community/vulnerabilities/XML_External_Entity_(XXE)_Processing)

<a id="m-023"></a>
#### M-023 — Allowlist client-controlled fields

**Addresses:**

- 🔴 [F-008](#f-008) — Mass Assignment Allows Admin Role Injection (`verify.ts:52`)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-915](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/915.html)

**Priority:** P1 - Immediate · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `routes/verify.ts:52`

**How:**

1. Add middleware before the Finale-generated POST `/api/Users` handler in `server.ts` to delete `req.body.role` and `req.body.isActive`: `app.post('/api/Users', (req, res, next) => { delete req.body.role; next() })`
2. Configure the Finale resource to use an allowlist of writable fields excluding role and `isActive`
3. Add a test that POSTs with role='admin' and verifies the created user has role='customer'

_Example implementation in `routes/verify.ts:52`: it applies **Allowlist client-controlled fields**. The ordered steps above remain authoritative._

```typescript
// Whitelist mutable fields server-side; never trust the body.
const ALLOWED = ['email', 'username'] as const
const patch = Object.fromEntries(
  Object.entries(req.body).filter(([k]) => ALLOWED.includes(k as any))
)
await user.update(patch)
```

**Verification:** Send POST `/api/Users` with role:'admin'; verify GET `/api/Users/:newId` (as admin) shows the user has role 'customer' not 'admin'

**Reference:** [OWASP: 0xa3 Broken Object Property Level Authorization](https://owasp.org/www-project-api-security/editions/2023/en/0xa3-broken-object-property-level-authorization/)

- [CWE-250](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/250.html)

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `Dockerfile:5`

**How:** Remove `--unsafe-perm`; use non-root user - `npm install --unsafe-perm` runs postinstall scripts as root, widening supply-chain compromise scope.

1. Remove `--unsafe-perm`; use non-root user

**Verification:** Re-run the IAC-004 scanner check; it reports no match at `Dockerfile:5`, and the regression test passes.

<a id="m-016"></a>
#### M-016 — Use parameterized database queries

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-001](#f-001) — NoSQL Injection in MarsDB Query Layer (`showProductReviews.ts:36`)

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-006](#w-006)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-943](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/943.html) - Special-Element Injection in Data Query

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `routes/showProductReviews.ts:36`

**How:**

1. Always coerce `req.params.id` to Number and return 400 if it is NaN: const id = `Number(req.params.id)`; if (`Number.isNaN(id)`) return `res.status(400).json({error:'Invalid id'})`
2. Replace the \`$where` predicate with a MarsDB query object: `db.reviewsCollection.find({ product: id })`
3. Remove the `global.sleep` definition and the challenge-dependent code path

_Example implementation in `routes/showProductReviews.ts:36`: it applies **Use parameterized database queries**. The ordered steps above remain authoritative._

```javascript
// Reject `$where` and operator keys from user-controlled input.
function safeQuery(filter) {
  for (const k of Object.keys(filter)) {
    if (k.startsWith('$')) throw new Error('operator not allowed')
  }
  return collection.find(filter)
}
```

**Verification:** Send GET `/rest/products/1`;`sleep(2000)``/reviews` and verify the response arrives in under 100ms without the 2-second delay

**Reference:** [OWASP: 05.6 Testing For Nosql Injection](https://owasp.org/www-project-web-security-testing-guide/latest/4-Web_Application_Security_Testing/07-Input_Validation_Testing/05.6-Testing_for_NoSQL_Injection)

<a id="m-024"></a>
#### M-024 — Add anti-CSRF protection to state-changing requests

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-009](#f-009) — OAuth CSRF (`login.component.ts:152`)

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-007](#w-007)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-352](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/352.html) - Cross-Site Request Forgery (CSRF)

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `frontend/src/app/login/login.component.ts:152`

**How:**

1. In `googleLogin()`, generate state = `crypto.randomUUID()` and store it in `sessionStorage` before redirecting; append &state=\${state} to the `oauthProviderUrl` request.
2. In `oauth.component.ts` `parseRedirectUrlParams()`, extract the returned state and compare it against the value in `sessionStorage`; abort authentication on mismatch or absence.
3. Add a test that supplies a tampered or absent state parameter and asserts the component rejects the callback.

_Example implementation in `frontend/src/app/login/login.component.ts:152`: it applies **Add anti-CSRF protection to state-changing requests**. The ordered steps above remain authoritative._

```typescript
// Use double-submit cookie / SameSite=Strict for state-changing routes.
app.use(csrf({ cookie: { sameSite: 'strict', httpOnly: true, secure: true } }))
```

**Verification:** Open the network tab, initiate Google login, then craft a redirect URL with a different state value; confirm the SPA rejects authentication and does not store a token.

**Reference:** [datatracker.ietf.org: Rfc6749](https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/html/rfc6749#section-10.12)

<a id="m-025"></a>
#### M-025 — Replace email-derived password with server-generated random credential for OAuth accounts

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-010](#f-010) — Predictable password derived from Google OAuth email (`oauth.component.ts:30`)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-1391](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/1391.html)

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Medium · **File:** `frontend/src/app/oauth/oauth.component.ts:30`

**How:**

1. On the server, generate a cryptographically random secret for each OAuth-linked account and store only its salted hash; never return or transmit the plaintext.
2. Remove the `btoa(email.split('').reverse())` formula from `oauth.component.ts`; authenticate post-OAuth via a server-issued session token or short-lived one-time credential.
3. Verify that no existing OAuth-account passwords computed with the old formula are accepted after migration.

**Verification:** Register a new Google OAuth account, then attempt POST `/rest/user/login` with `btoa(email.split('').reverse().join(''))`; verify the server returns 401.

**Reference:** [OWASP Cheat Sheet: Authentication Cheat Sheet](https://cheatsheetseries.owasp.org/cheatsheets/Authentication_Cheat_Sheet.html)

<a id="m-026"></a>
#### M-026 — Move cryptographic keys to a managed secret store

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-011](#f-011) — Hardcoded RSA private key enables offline JWT forgery (`insecurity.ts:23`)

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-010](#w-010)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-321](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/321.html) - Use of Hard-coded Cryptographic Key

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Medium · **File:** `lib/insecurity.ts:23`

**How:**

1. Replace the string literal at `lib/insecurity.ts:23` with: const `privateKey` = `process.env.JWT_PRIVATE_KEY` || `fs.readFileSync(process.env.JWT_KEY_PATH || 'encryptionkeys/jwt.key', 'utf8')`
2. Rotate the RSA key pair; invalidate all existing tokens by restarting the service after deploying the new key.
3. Verify that a token signed with the old hardcoded key is rejected by the updated middleware in an integration test.

_Example implementation in `lib/insecurity.ts:23`: it applies **Move cryptographic keys to a managed secret store**. The ordered steps above remain authoritative._

```typescript
// Load the RSA private key from an environment variable / KMS — never
// the source tree. Rotate the prior key and revoke outstanding tokens.
const privateKey = process.env.JWT_PRIVATE_KEY
if (!privateKey) throw new Error('JWT_PRIVATE_KEY not set')
const token = jwt.sign(claims, privateKey, { algorithm: 'RS256' })
```

**Verification:** Sign a JWT with the removed hardcoded private key and send it to GET `/rest/basket/1`; expect HTTP 401 after key rotation.

**Reference:** [OWASP Cheat Sheet: JSON Web Token For Java Cheat Sheet](https://cheatsheetseries.owasp.org/cheatsheets/JSON_Web_Token_for_Java_Cheat_Sheet.html)

<a id="m-027"></a>
#### M-027 — Enforce JWT signature and algorithm verification

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-012](#f-012) — Missing JWT algorithm whitelist (`insecurity.ts:54`)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-347](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/347.html) - Improper Verification of Cryptographic Signature

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `lib/insecurity.ts:54`

**How:**

1. Change the `expressJwt` call at `lib/insecurity.ts:54` to: `expressJwt({ secret: publicKey, algorithms: ['RS256'] })`
2. Verify that a JWT signed with `HS256` using the public key is rejected with HTTP 401 by the middleware in an integration test.

_Example implementation in `lib/insecurity.ts:54`: it applies **Enforce JWT signature and algorithm verification**. The ordered steps above remain authoritative._

```typescript
// Always verify on the public key; never trust the unsigned header.
const decoded = jwt.verify(token, publicKey, { algorithms: ['RS256'] })
```

**Verification:** Send a request with a JWT where `header.alg`=`'HS256'` and the token is HMAC-signed with the public key bytes; expect HTTP 401 from any authenticated endpoint.

**Reference:** [OWASP Cheat Sheet: JSON Web Token For Java Cheat Sheet](https://cheatsheetseries.owasp.org/cheatsheets/JSON_Web_Token_for_Java_Cheat_Sheet.html)

<a id="m-032"></a>
#### M-032 — Encode output instead of bypassing the framework sanitizer

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-017](#f-017) — Stored XSS (`about.component.ts:119`)

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-009](#w-009)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-79](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/79.html) - Cross-site Scripting

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `frontend/src/app/about/about.component.ts:119`

**How:**

1. Render feedbacks[i].comment as a plain text binding inside the figcaption element; Angular's {{ interpolation }} auto-escapes HTML, removing the XSS surface.
2. If HTML formatting in feedback is required, apply `DomSanitizer.sanitize(SecurityContext.HTML, comment)` instead of bypassing the sanitizer.
3. Add a test that loads the About page after creating feedback with comment='`<script>``alert(1)``</script>`' and asserts no alert fires.

_Example implementation in `frontend/src/app/about/about.component.ts:119`: it applies **Encode output instead of bypassing the framework sanitizer**. The ordered steps above remain authoritative._

```typescript
// Never call bypassSecurityTrust*; let Angular sanitize.
// template:  <div [innerHTML]="product.description"></div>
// component: no DomSanitizer.bypassSecurityTrustHtml(...)
this.product.description = raw  // bound directly; Angular escapes
```

**Verification:** POST `/api/Feedbacks` with comment='`<img src=x onerror=alert(1)>`'; navigate to /#`/about`; confirm no alert fires after the fix.

**Reference:** [OWASP: Xss](https://owasp.org/www-community/attacks/xss/)

3. Add a test that uploads a ZIP with a traversal entry and verifies no file is written outside uploads/complaints

<a id="m-043"></a>
#### M-043 — Move secrets to a managed secret store

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-029](#f-029) — Hardcoded BIP39 mnemonic enables forged challenge (`checkKeys.ts:10`)

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-010](#w-010)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-798](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/798.html) - Use of Hard-coded Credentials

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `routes/checkKeys.ts:10`

**How:**

1. Remove the string literal mnemonic from `checkKeys.ts:10` and read the value from `process.env.NFT_MNEMONIC` at startup; fail closed if the variable is absent.
2. Rotate the wallet by generating a new mnemonic and never committing it to the repository.
3. Add a CI secret-scan step (e.g., `truffleHog` or gitleaks) that fails the build if BIP39 word sequences appear in committed code.

_Example implementation in `routes/checkKeys.ts:10`: it applies **Move secrets to a managed secret store**. The ordered steps above remain authoritative._

```typescript
// Read secrets from env / vault, not the source.
const hmacKey = process.env.ORDER_HMAC_KEY
if (!hmacKey) throw new Error('ORDER_HMAC_KEY not set')
const sig = createHmac('sha256', hmacKey).update(payload).digest('hex')
```

**Verification:** grep -r 'purpose betray marriage' . should return no results after the change; pre-commit hook or CI scan should block any BIP39 mnemonic literal.

**Reference:** [CWE-798: Use of Hard-coded Credentials](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/798.html)

<a id="m-045"></a>
#### M-045 — Store session tokens in HttpOnly, Secure cookies

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-031](#f-031) — JWT stored in localStorage (`login.component.ts:105`)

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-011](#w-011)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-922](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/922.html) - Insecure Storage of Sensitive Information

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Medium · **File:** `frontend/src/app/login/login.component.ts:105`

**How:**

1. Remove `localStorage.setItem('token', ...)` calls in `login.component.ts` and `oauth.component.ts`; rely solely on the HttpOnly, Secure, `SameSite=Strict` cookie set by the server.
2. Update `request.interceptor.ts` to remove the manual `Authorization` header injection - the HttpOnly cookie will be sent automatically by the browser.
3. Verify the server's `Set-Cookie` response includes HttpOnly and Secure flags before removing the `localStorage` fallback.

_Example implementation in `frontend/src/app/login/login.component.ts:105`: it applies **Store session tokens in HttpOnly, Secure cookies**. The ordered steps above remain authoritative._

```typescript
// Move the JWT out of localStorage into an httpOnly cookie.
res.cookie('session', token, {
  httpOnly: true, secure: true, sameSite: 'lax', maxAge: 3600_000
})
```

**Verification:** After login, open the browser DevTools console and run `localStorage.getItem('token')`; confirm it returns null.

**Reference:** [OWASP Cheat Sheet: JSON Web Token For Java Cheat Sheet](https://cheatsheetseries.owasp.org/cheatsheets/JSON_Web_Token_for_Java_Cheat_Sheet.html#where-to-store-your-jwt)

<a id="m-046"></a>
#### M-046 — Move cryptographic keys to a managed secret store

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-032](#f-032) — Hardcoded HMAC key exposes security answer hashes to offline attack (`insecurity.ts:44`)

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-010](#w-010)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-321](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/321.html) - Use of Hard-coded Cryptographic Key

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `lib/insecurity.ts:44`

**How:**

1. Replace the literal key in `lib/insecurity.ts:44` with: const `hmacKey` = `process.env.SECURITY_ANSWER_HMAC_KEY`; if (!`hmacKey`) throw new `Error('SECURITY_ANSWER_HMAC_KEY not set')`
2. Regenerate all stored security answer HMACs after deploying the new key, or invalidate existing answers and require users to re-enter them.
3. Verify that a request to POST `/rest/user/reset-password` with an HMAC computed using the old hardcoded key is rejected with HTTP 401.

_Example implementation in `lib/insecurity.ts:44`: it applies **Move cryptographic keys to a managed secret store**. The ordered steps above remain authoritative._

```typescript
// Load the RSA private key from an environment variable / KMS — never
// the source tree. Rotate the prior key and revoke outstanding tokens.
const privateKey = process.env.JWT_PRIVATE_KEY
if (!privateKey) throw new Error('JWT_PRIVATE_KEY not set')
const token = jwt.sign(claims, privateKey, { algorithm: 'RS256' })
```

**Verification:** Compute `hmac('Samuel', 'pa4qacea4VK9t9nGv7yZtwmj')` and send it as the answer to Jim's reset-password endpoint; expect HTTP 401 after the key rotation.

**Reference:** [OWASP Cheat Sheet: Cryptographic Storage Cheat Sheet](https://cheatsheetseries.owasp.org/cheatsheets/Cryptographic_Storage_Cheat_Sheet.html)

<a id="m-047"></a>
#### M-047 — Hash passwords with a strong, salted algorithm

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-033](#f-033) — MD5 password hashing without salt enables offline (`insecurity.ts:43`)

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-013](#w-013)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-916](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/916.html)

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Medium · **File:** `lib/insecurity.ts:43`

**How:**

1. Replace the hash function at `lib/insecurity.ts:43` with bcrypt (cost factor ≥ 12) or Argon2id: import bcrypt from 'bcryptjs'; export const hash = async (data: string) => `bcrypt.hash(data, 12)`
2. Update `routes/login.ts:34` to use an ORM query with `bcrypt.compare()` for password verification instead of embedding the hash in SQL.
3. Migrate existing `MD5`-hashed passwords to the new hash on next user login by re-hashing after successful `MD5` verification and then replacing the stored hash.

_Example implementation in `lib/insecurity.ts:43`: it applies **Hash passwords with a strong, salted algorithm**. The ordered steps above remain authoritative._

```typescript
// Replace md5/sha1 password hashes with bcrypt or argon2.
import bcrypt from 'bcrypt'
const hash = await bcrypt.hash(plaintext, 12)
const ok = await bcrypt.compare(plaintext, storedHash)
```

**Verification:** Obtain an `MD5` hash from the Users table via the test DB; attempt to verify it using the updated hash function and confirm it is not accepted as a valid password.

**Reference:** [OWASP Cheat Sheet: Password Storage Cheat Sheet](https://cheatsheetseries.owasp.org/cheatsheets/Password_Storage_Cheat_Sheet.html)

<a id="m-050"></a>
#### M-050 — Enforce server-side authorization on every endpoint

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-037](#f-037) — Unauthenticated access to all order records (`trackOrder.ts:18`)

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-003](#w-003)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-862](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/862.html) - Missing Authorization

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `routes/trackOrder.ts:18`

**How:**

1. Add `security.isAuthorized` middleware to the track-order route so unauthenticated callers receive a 401 before any database query executes.
2. After fixing the \`$where` injection (🟠 [F-001](#f-001) — NoSQL Injection in MarsDB Query Layer), filter results to the authenticated user's email: `ordersCollection.find({ orderId: id, email: maskedEmail })`.
3. Add an integration test asserting that an unauthenticated GET to `/rest/track-order` returns 401.

_Example implementation in `routes/trackOrder.ts:18`: it applies **Enforce server-side authorization on every endpoint**. The ordered steps above remain authoritative._

```typescript
// Add server-side role check on every admin route.
router.use('/admin/*', (req, res, next) => {
  if (req.user?.role !== 'admin') return res.status(403).end()
  next()
})
```

**Verification:** Send GET `/rest/track-order/any-id` without an `Authorization` header and confirm the response status is 401 and no order data is returned.

**Reference:** [CWE-862: Missing Authorization](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/862.html)

<a id="m-051"></a>
#### M-051 — Stop exposing internal information to clients

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-038](#f-038) — Unauthenticated CTF Flag Disclosure (`registerWebsocketEvents.ts:31`)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-200](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/200.html) - Exposure of Sensitive Information

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Medium · **File:** `lib/startup/registerWebsocketEvents.ts:31`

**How:**

1. Require JWT authentication on the Socket\.IO handshake (🟠 [F-016](#f-016) — Unauthenticated WebSocket Channel) before any notification is emitted to a connecting socket.
2. Scope notification delivery to the authenticated user's own challenge history rather than broadcasting the global notifications array to every new connection.
3. Consider omitting the flag field from broadcast notifications and only returning it through an authenticated HTTP endpoint after challenge completion.

_Example implementation in `lib/startup/registerWebsocketEvents.ts:31`: it applies **Stop exposing internal information to clients**. The ordered steps above remain authoritative._

```typescript
// Remove directory-listing middleware; require auth on management endpoints.
// app.use('/ftp', serveIndex(...))   // delete
app.use('/metrics', requireRole('admin'), promBundle())
```

**Verification:** Connect an unauthenticated WebSocket client; assert that no 'challenge solved' events are received and no flag values are included in any emitted message.

**Reference:** [OWASP: A01 2021 Broken Access Control](https://owasp.org/www-project-top-ten/2021/A01_2021-Broken_Access_Control)

2. Plan a migration: re-hash on successful login for existing users; force password reset for dormant accounts whose `MD5` hash cannot be verified against the new scheme.
3. Add a test that stores a new user password and asserts the stored value starts with '\$2b\$' (bcrypt) or '\$argon2id\$', not a 32-character hex string.

<a id="m-054"></a>
#### M-054 — Bound parser and decompression resource limits

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-041](#f-041) — YAML Bomb Causes CPU Exhaustion in File Upload (`fileUpload.ts:117`)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-400](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/400.html) - Uncontrolled Resource Consumption

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Medium · **File:** `routes/fileUpload.ts:117`

**How:**

1. Replace `yaml.load` with `yaml.load` using a schema that disallows aliased anchors, or validate the input size after decompression: if `JSON.stringify(parsed).length` > `MAX_SIZE` then reject
2. Add authentication to `/file-upload` so only logged-in users can trigger expensive file processing
3. Consider rejecting YAML uploads entirely if the B2B use case no longer requires them

**Verification:** Upload a 1KB YAML bomb file; verify the server returns HTTP 503 within the timeout window and remains responsive to other requests immediately after

**Reference:** [en.wikipedia.org: Billion Laughs Attack](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Billion_laughs_attack)

**Prevents CWEs:**

2. Delete the `global.sleep` definition from `routes/showProductReviews.ts`
3. Add a test verifying that the `noSqlCommandChallenge` cannot be triggered by a manipulated URL parameter

<a id="m-056"></a>
#### M-056 — Rate-limit expensive requests and bound input size

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-043](#f-043) — Unbounded WebSocket Connection Consumption (`registerWebsocketEvents.ts:20`)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-400](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/400.html) - Uncontrolled Resource Consumption

**Priority:** P2 - This Sprint · **Effort:** Medium · **File:** `lib/startup/registerWebsocketEvents.ts:20`

**How:**

1. Set a `maxHttpBufferSize` on the Server constructor to cap per-message payload size.
2. Integrate a per-IP connection rate limiter (e.g., using the socket\.io-rate-limiter package or a Redis-backed `express-rate-limit` applied to the upgrade route) before the Socket\.IO handshake completes.
3. Disconnect or reject connections that exceed the per-IP threshold to prevent socket exhaustion.
4. Add a load test that asserts the server remains responsive after 500 concurrent connection attempts from a single IP.

**Verification:** Send 1000 rapid WebSocket upgrade requests from a single IP; assert that connections beyond the configured limit are rejected with HTTP 429 and the application continues serving HTTP requests.

**Reference:** [OWASP: A05 2021 Security Misconfiguration](https://owasp.org/www-project-top-ten/2021/A05_2021-Security_Misconfiguration)

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-003](#w-003)

**Priority:** P3 - Next Quarter · **Effort:** Medium · **File:** `server.ts:310`

_Example implementation in `server.ts:310`: it applies **Enforce server-side authorization on every endpoint**. The ordered steps above remain authoritative._

```typescript

})
```

<a id="m-007"></a>
#### M-007 — Enforce object-level (ownership) authorization

**Addresses:**

- 🟠 [F-048](#f-048) — IDOR: review update without ownership check (`updateProductReviews.ts:16`)

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-003](#w-003)

**Priority:** P3 - Next Quarter · **Effort:** Medium · **File:** `routes/updateProductReviews.ts:16`

**How:** The evidence-verifier sample could not confirm or refute the claim from the cited snippet alone. Have a developer familiar with this code path read ±20 lines around the cited location (`routes/updateProductReviews.ts:16`) and decide whether to keep, downgrade, or remove this finding.

1. Before calling `reviewsCollection.update`, call `reviewsCollection.findOne({ _id: String(req.body.id) })` and confirm `review.author === user.data.email`; return 403 if the check fails.
2. Remove `{ multi: true }` from the update options so the operation is always scoped to a single document.
3. Add a test asserting that user A cannot modify user B's review, receiving a 403 response.

_Example implementation in `routes/updateProductReviews.ts:16`: it applies **Enforce object-level (ownership) authorization**. The ordered steps above remain authoritative._

```typescript
// Ownership check before touching a resource.
const basket = await Basket.findByPk(req.params.id)
if (!basket || basket.UserId !== req.user.id) return res.status(403).end()
```

**Verification:** Authenticate as user A, obtain the `_id` of a review written by user B, PUT that id with a new message, and assert the response is 403 and user B's review is unchanged.

**Reference:** [CWE-639: Authorization Bypass Through User-Controlled Key](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/639.html)

<a id="m-017"></a>
#### M-017 — Add security audit logging

**Addresses:**

- 🟡 [F-002](#f-002) — Missing Security Event Logging (`login.ts:18`)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-778](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/778.html) - Insufficient Logging

**Priority:** P3 - Next Quarter · **Effort:** Medium · **File:** `routes/login.ts:18`

**How:**

1. Introduce a security logger (e.g. winston with a file or syslog transport configured for append-only writes) and emit events for: successful login (user ID, IP, timestamp), failed login (email attempted, IP, reason), 2FA verify (success/failure), and password reset (user ID, IP).
2. Ensure the log transport is configured outside the application's own write path (syslog, SIEM, or write-once object storage) so attackers cannot alter or delete entries.
3. Add a test asserting that a login attempt emits at least one log entry with the correct event type and actor identity.

_Example implementation in `routes/login.ts:18`: it applies **Add security audit logging**. The ordered steps above remain authoritative._

```typescript
// Log authn / authz outcomes with stable correlation ids.
logger.info({ event: 'auth.login.fail', userId, ip: req.ip, reqId })
logger.info({ event: 'authz.deny', userId, route: req.path, reqId })
```

**Verification:** Trigger a failed login via POST `/rest/user/login` with invalid credentials and confirm a security event entry with failed-login type appears in the log output.

**Reference:** [OWASP Cheat Sheet: Logging Cheat Sheet](https://cheatsheetseries.owasp.org/cheatsheets/Logging_Cheat_Sheet.html)

```bash
# Pin and audit dependencies; fail CI on known vulns.
npm audit --omit=dev --audit-level=high
# Upgrade unmaintained packages in package.json, then:
npm install && npm test
```

**Verification:** Run `grep -rn 'uses:' .github/workflows/ | grep -v '#' | grep -v '@[0-9a-f]\{40\}'`; expect zero output.

**Reference:** [GitHub Docs: Security Hardening For Github Actions](https://docs.github.com/en/actions/security-guides/security-hardening-for-github-actions#using-third-party-actions)

---

<a id="m-066"></a>
#### M-066 — Pin the container base image to an immutable digest

**Addresses:**

- 🟡 [F-053](#f-053) — Unpinned container base image — `Dockerfile:1` (`Dockerfile:1`)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-1104](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/1104.html) - Use of Unmaintained Third-Party Components

**Priority:** P3 - Next Quarter · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `Dockerfile:1`

**How:**

1. Replace `FROM node:24` and `FROM gcr.io/distroless/nodejs24-debian13` with digest-pinned forms: `FROM node:24@sha256:<digest>` and `FROM gcr.io/distroless/nodejs24-debian13@sha256:<digest>`.
2. Enable Renovate or Dependabot with Docker datasource to update digest pins automatically when upstream publishes new releases.

_Example implementation in `Dockerfile:1`: it applies **Pin the container base image to an immutable digest**. The ordered steps above remain authoritative._

```bash
# Pin and audit dependencies; fail CI on known vulns.
npm audit --omit=dev --audit-level=high
# Upgrade unmaintained packages in package.json, then:
npm install && npm test
```

**Verification:** Run `grep '^FROM' Dockerfile` and confirm every line contains `@sha256:` followed by a 64-character hex digest.

**Reference:** [docs.docker.com: Dockerfile Best Practices](https://docs.docker.com/develop/develop-images/dockerfile_best-practices/#from)

---

<a id="m-067"></a>
#### M-067 — Set least-privilege CI workflow permissions

**Addresses:**

- 🟡 [F-054](#f-054) — Npm install --unsafe-perm in Docker build stage — `Dockerfile:5` (`Dockerfile:5`)

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-008](#w-008)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-1357](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/1357.html)

**Priority:** P3 - Next Quarter · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `Dockerfile:5`

**How:**

1. Remove `--unsafe-perm` from `Dockerfile` line 5: change to `RUN npm install --omit=dev`.
2. If specific build scripts legitimately require elevated access, isolate them into a separate RUN step with explicit invocation rather than relying on lifecycle hooks.

**Verification:** Run `grep -n 'unsafe-perm' Dockerfile` and confirm zero results. Build the image locally and confirm it succeeds.

**Reference:** [docs.npmjs.com: Npm Install](https://docs.npmjs.com/cli/v10/commands/npm-install#--unsafe-perm)

---

<a id="m-068"></a>
#### M-068 — Restrict CORS to trusted origins

**Addresses:**

- 🟡 [F-055](#f-055) — Wildcard CORS Permits Credentialed Cross-Origin Requests (`server.ts:183`)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-942](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/942.html)

**Priority:** P3 - Next Quarter · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `server.ts:183`

**How:**

1. Replace `app.use(cors())` in `server.ts` with `app.use(cors({ origin: config.get('server.baseUrl'), credentials: true }))` to restrict to the application origin
2. Add `SameSite=Strict` or `SameSite=Lax` to all cookies
3. Test that cross-origin preflight requests from a non-allowed origin receive HTTP 403

**Verification:** From a different origin, send a credentialed cross-origin request to POST `/rest/basket/:id/checkout`; expect HTTP 403 or CORS error

**Reference:** [OWASP Cheat Sheet: Cross Site Request Forgery Prevention Cheat Sheet](https://cheatsheetseries.owasp.org/cheatsheets/Cross-Site_Request_Forgery_Prevention_Cheat_Sheet.html)

---

<a id="m-069"></a>
#### M-069 — Encode output instead of bypassing the framework sanitizer

**Addresses:**

- 🟡 [F-056](#f-056) — Challenge-gated weak sanitization stores XSS HTML in Users (`user.ts:50`)

**Weaknesses addressed:** [W-009](#w-009)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-79](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/79.html) - Cross-site Scripting

**Priority:** P3 - Next Quarter · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `models/user.ts:50`

**How:**

1. In `models/user.ts` username setter, call `security.sanitizeSecure(username)` unconditionally and remove the `isChallengeEnabled` branch; relocate any challenge-detection logic to a route-level observer that does not weaken the storage-layer control.
2. Add a test that stores a username containing `<script>``alert(1)``</script>` and asserts the stored value contains no executable HTML tags.

_Example implementation in `models/user.ts:50`: it applies **Encode output instead of bypassing the framework sanitizer**. The ordered steps above remain authoritative._

```typescript
// Never call bypassSecurityTrust*; let Angular sanitize.
// template:  <div [innerHTML]="product.description"></div>
// component: no DomSanitizer.bypassSecurityTrustHtml(...)
this.product.description = raw  // bound directly; Angular escapes
```

**Verification:** Set username to '`<img src=x onerror=alert(1)>`' via the registration API; confirm the stored `Users.username` value contains no onerror attribute.

**Reference:** [OWASP Cheat Sheet: Cross Site Scripting Prevention Cheat Sheet](https://cheatsheetseries.owasp.org/cheatsheets/Cross_Site_Scripting_Prevention_Cheat_Sheet.html)

---

<a id="m-070"></a>
#### M-070 — Return generic error messages to clients

**Addresses:**

- 🟡 [F-063](#f-063) — Development Error Handler Returns Stack Traces in Production (`server.ts:678`)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-209](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/209.html)

**Priority:** P3 - Next Quarter · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `server.ts:678`

**How:**

1. Remove `app.use(errorhandler())` from `server.ts:678`
2. Add a production error handler: `app.use((err, req, res, next) => { logger.error(err); res.status(500).json({ error: 'An unexpected error occurred' }) })`
3. Test that sending a malformed request returns HTTP 500 with a generic message and no stack trace

**Verification:** Send a request that triggers a server error; verify the response body contains no file paths, stack traces, or library version strings

**Reference:** [expressjs.com: Error Handling](https://expressjs.com/en/guide/error-handling.html)

---

<a id="m-071"></a>
#### M-071 — Filter order object fields before returning to the LLM context in routes/chat.ts

**Addresses:**

- 🟡 [F-064](#f-064) — LLM02 Chatbot Returns Unfiltered User Order Data Including (`chat.ts:164`)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-359](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/359.html)

**Priority:** P3 - Next Quarter · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `routes/chat.ts:164`

**How:**

1. In `routes/chat.ts` inside `getOrderById` execute, return only the fields necessary for customer service: `orderId`, products (names only), `totalPrice`, eta, status
2. Exclude email, bonus, and internal identifiers from the returned object
3. Add a test verifying the tool response does not contain email or sensitive pricing fields

**Verification:** Trigger a `getOrderById` call in the chatbot and verify the event stream contains no email pattern or raw order fields beyond product names and delivery status

**Reference:** [OWASP: LLM02 2025 Sensitive Information Disclosure](https://owasp.org/www-project-top-10-for-large-language-model-applications/LLM02_2025_Sensitive_Information_Disclosure)

---

<a id="m-072"></a>
#### M-072 — Rate-limit and lock out repeated authentication attempts

**Addresses:**

- 🟡 [F-065](#f-065) — No rate limiting on login endpoint enables credential (`login.ts:32`)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-307](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/307.html) - Improper Restriction of Excessive Authentication Attempts

**Priority:** P3 - Next Quarter · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `routes/login.ts:32`

**How:**

1. Install `express-rate-limit` and apply it to the login route: const `loginLimiter` = `rateLimit({ windowMs: 15 * 60 * 1000, max: 20, standardHeaders: true, legacyHeaders: false })`; `app.post('/rest/user/login', loginLimiter, login())`
2. Apply the same limiter to POST `/rest/2fa/verify` to prevent TOTP brute-force after password authentication.
3. Verify that submitting more than 20 login requests within 15 minutes from one IP returns HTTP 429.

_Example implementation in `routes/login.ts:32`: it applies **Rate-limit and lock out repeated authentication attempts**. The ordered steps above remain authoritative._

```typescript
// Per-IP + per-account rate limiting on auth endpoints.
import rateLimit from 'express-rate-limit'
app.use('/rest/user/login',
  rateLimit({ windowMs: 60_000, max: 5, standardHeaders: true }))
```

**Verification:** Send 21 POST `/rest/user/login` requests from the same IP within a 15-minute window; the 21st response must be HTTP 429 with a Retry-After header.

**Reference:** [OWASP Cheat Sheet: Authentication Cheat Sheet](https://cheatsheetseries.owasp.org/cheatsheets/Authentication_Cheat_Sheet.html)

---

<a id="m-073"></a>
#### M-073 — Rate-limit and lock out repeated authentication attempts

**Addresses:**

- 🟡 [F-066](#f-066) — No Rate Limiting or Account Lockout on Login Endpoint (`server.ts:595`)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-307](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/307.html) - Improper Restriction of Excessive Authentication Attempts

**Priority:** P3 - Next Quarter · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `server.ts:595`

**How:**

1. In `server.ts`, add a rate limiter before the login route: `app.use('/rest/user/login', rateLimit({ windowMs: 15 * 60 * 1000, max: 10, skipSuccessfulRequests: true }))`
2. Track failed attempts per email in memory or Redis and lock the account for 15 minutes after 10 failures
3. Add a test that sends 11 login requests and verifies the 11th returns HTTP 429

_Example implementation in `server.ts:595`: it applies **Rate-limit and lock out repeated authentication attempts**. The ordered steps above remain authoritative._

```typescript
// Per-IP + per-account rate limiting on auth endpoints.
import rateLimit from 'express-rate-limit'
app.use('/rest/user/login',
  rateLimit({ windowMs: 60_000, max: 5, standardHeaders: true }))
```

**Verification:** Send 11 POST `/rest/user/login` requests with wrong passwords; verify the response to the 11th is HTTP 429 Too Many Requests

**Reference:** [OWASP Cheat Sheet: Authentication Cheat Sheet](https://cheatsheetseries.owasp.org/cheatsheets/Authentication_Cheat_Sheet.html#account-lockout)

---

<a id="m-074"></a>
#### M-074 — Rate-limit expensive requests and bound input size

**Addresses:**

- 🟡 [F-067](#f-067) — LLM10 Unbounded Token Consumption on Chat Endpoint (`chat.ts:207`)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-400](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/400.html) - Uncontrolled Resource Consumption

**Priority:** P3 - Next Quarter · **Effort:** Medium · **File:** `routes/chat.ts:207`

**How:**

1. Add a rate limiter to the chat endpoint in `server.ts: app.use`('/rest/chat', `rateLimit({ windowMs: 60 * 1000, max: 20 })`)
2. Add a per-user daily token budget and check it before forwarding the request to the LLM provider
3. Configure `max_tokens` in the `streamText` call at `routes/chat.ts:201` to limit per-request token consumption
4. Add a test verifying the 21st chat request within 1 minute returns HTTP 429

**Verification:** Send 21 POST `/rest/chat` requests within 60 seconds from the same IP; verify the 21st returns HTTP 429

**Reference:** [OWASP: LLM10 2025 Unbounded Consumption](https://owasp.org/www-project-top-10-for-large-language-model-applications/LLM10_2025_Unbounded_Consumption)

---

<a id="m-075"></a>
#### M-075 — Rate-limit expensive requests and bound input size

**Addresses:**

- 🟡 [F-068](#f-068) — Unbounded wallet derivation per request without rate (`checkKeys.ts:9`)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-400](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/400.html) - Uncontrolled Resource Consumption

**Priority:** P3 - Next Quarter · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `routes/checkKeys.ts:9`

**How:**

1. Apply an `express-rate-limit` middleware to `/rest/web3/submitKey` limiting each IP to a small number of requests per minute (e.g., 10/min).
2. Optionally cache the derived wallet values at module load time rather than recomputing them on every request, eliminating the per-request CPU cost regardless of rate limiting.

**Verification:** Send 20 rapid requests to POST `/rest/web3/submitKey` from a single IP; requests beyond the limit should receive 429 Too Many Requests.

**Reference:** [OWASP Cheat Sheet: Denial Of Service Cheat Sheet](https://cheatsheetseries.owasp.org/cheatsheets/Denial_of_Service_Cheat_Sheet.html)

---

<a id="m-076"></a>
#### M-076 — Enforce authorization on the server

**Addresses:**

- 🟡 [F-069](#f-069) — Client-side-only AdminGuard enforces no server authorization (`app.guard.ts:52`)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-602](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/602.html)

**Priority:** P3 - Next Quarter · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `frontend/src/app/app.guard.ts:52`

**How:**

1. Add a `canActivate` API call that fetches `/rest/user/whoami` and confirms the server returns an admin role before permitting navigation; reject if the server denies.
2. On the Express API side, ensure every `/api/Users`, `/api/Feedbacks` (admin operations), and similar privileged endpoints independently verify the JWT's role claim before responding.

**Verification:** Manually set `localStorage.setItem('token', forged_non_admin_jwt)`; navigate to /#`/administration`; confirm the server rejects privileged API calls with 403.

**Reference:** [OWASP: A01 2021 Broken Access Control](https://owasp.org/www-project-top-ten/2021/A01_2021-Broken_Access_Control)

---

<a id="m-077"></a>
#### M-077 — Rate-limit and lock out repeated authentication attempts

**Addresses:**

- 🟡 [F-070](#f-070) — Unrated TOTP verify endpoint allows 2FA brute-force (`2fa.ts:31`)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-307](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/307.html) - Improper Restriction of Excessive Authentication Attempts

**Priority:** P3 - Next Quarter · **Effort:** Low · **File:** `routes/2fa.ts:31`

**How:**

1. Apply a request rate limit to POST `/rest/2fa/verify`: at most 5 attempts per `tmpToken`, after which the `tmpToken` is invalidated and the user must re-authenticate with password.
2. Track attempt counts server-side in a short-lived store (e.g. Redis or the existing in-memory `tokenMap` with a counter field).
3. Add an integration test confirming that 6 incorrect TOTP attempts invalidate the `tmpToken` and return HTTP 401.

_Example implementation in `routes/2fa.ts:31`: it applies **Rate-limit and lock out repeated authentication attempts**. The ordered steps above remain authoritative._

```typescript
// Per-IP + per-account rate limiting on auth endpoints.
import rateLimit from 'express-rate-limit'
app.use('/rest/user/login',
  rateLimit({ windowMs: 60_000, max: 5, standardHeaders: true }))
```

**Verification:** Send 6 POST `/rest/2fa/verify` requests with an incorrect `totpToken` for the same `tmpToken`; the 6th must return HTTP 401 and subsequent valid TOTP codes must also be rejected.

**Reference:** [OWASP Cheat Sheet: Multifactor Authentication Cheat Sheet](https://cheatsheetseries.owasp.org/cheatsheets/Multifactor_Authentication_Cheat_Sheet.html)

---

<a id="m-078"></a>
#### M-078 — Treat tokenMap absence as revocation in updateAuthenticatedUsers; require re-login for

**Addresses:**

- 🟡 [F-071](#f-071) — UpdateAuthenticatedUsers re-inserts valid JWT bypassing (`insecurity.ts:191`)

**Prevents CWEs:**

- [CWE-613](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/613.html)

**Priority:** P3 - Next Quarter · **Effort:** Medium · **File:** `lib/insecurity.ts:191`

**How:**

1. Change the condition at `lib/insecurity.ts:193` from reinsertion to rejection: if the token passes `jwt.verify()` but is absent from `authenticatedUsers`, do not call `authenticatedUsers.put()`; instead, let the request proceed without a session context and require explicit re-authentication.
2. Persist the token map in a server-side store (e.g. Redis with TTL matching JWT expiry) so revocations survive server restarts.
3. Add an integration test: (1) obtain a JWT, (2) call the logout endpoint, (3) send the JWT again and confirm subsequent authenticated requests return HTTP 401.

**Verification:** Log in, call the logout endpoint, restart the server, then send the original JWT to GET `/rest/basket/1`; expect HTTP 401.

**Reference:** [OWASP Cheat Sheet: JSON Web Token For Java Cheat Sheet](https://cheatsheetseries.owasp.org/cheatsheets/JSON_Web_Token_for_Java_Cheat_Sheet.html)

## 11. Out of Scope

### Not Covered by This Method

An automated, AI-assisted threat model built from the repository's source code and configuration - static analysis only, no dynamic or penetration testing. It models the system as built rather than as designed. It does not replace a threat-modeling session with the team. Business context was taken from `docs/security/business-context.md`; design intent, runtime behaviour and production configuration are not covered, and each finding still needs confirmation in the deployed system.

- Design intent and the reasoning behind it - no design documents, ADRs or workshop context are read.
- Business processes and user journeys beyond the supplied business context.
- Runtime behaviour, deployment topology and production-only configuration.
- External and organizational controls.

Treat this report as review input, not sign-off.

### Excluded from This Assessment

The following items are **explicitly excluded** from this threat model. Findings against these areas should be tracked separately.

- Third-party hosted dependencies and SaaS endpoints
- Browser runtime vulnerabilities and end-user device security
- Operating system kernel and container runtime
- Underlying network infrastructure (DNS, BGP, ISP)
- Physical security of hosting facilities
- Opt-in threat actors, not assessed: malicious insiders with repository or pipeline access; malicious insiders with production access; attackers holding a user's device. Enable them with `enable:` in `.appsec/actors.yaml`.

<a id="appendix-a-vektor-taxonomy"></a>
## Appendix A — Vektor Taxonomy

This appendix defines the attacker-starting-position labels used in the Top Threats table and throughout [§8 Findings Register](#8-findings-register). Each label answers the question *what does the attacker need before the exploit begins?*

<a id="vektor-internet-anon"></a>
### Internet Anon

**Attacker position:** Unauthenticated attacker from the public internet · **Breach distance:** 1

**Preconditions:**

- Endpoint is reachable from the internet (no IP allowlist, no VPN)
- No authentication middleware blocks the request

**Typical CWEs:** [CWE-89](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/89.html) · [CWE-79](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/79.html) · [CWE-306](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/306.html) · [CWE-327](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/327.html) · [CWE-611](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/611.html) · [CWE-918](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/918.html)

**Typical OWASP Top 10:** A01:2025, A05:2025, A07:2025

<a id="vektor-internet-user"></a>
### Internet User

**Attacker position:** Any authenticated low-privilege user (valid JWT / session) · **Breach distance:** 2

**Preconditions:**

- Attacker has signed up or otherwise obtained a valid user session
- Endpoint is behind auth but not behind role/admin checks

**Typical CWEs:** [CWE-434](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/434.html) · [CWE-611](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/611.html) · [CWE-918](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/918.html) · [CWE-352](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/352.html) · [CWE-287](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/287.html)

**Typical OWASP Top 10:** A01:2025, A06:2025, A02:2025

<a id="vektor-internet-priv-user"></a>
### Internet Priv User

**Attacker position:** Authenticated admin-level user (JWT with admin role or equivalent) · **Breach distance:** 2

**Preconditions:**

- Attacker holds admin credentials or has elevated privileges
- Endpoint gated on admin role but still exploitable once reached

**Typical CWEs:** [CWE-862](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/862.html) · [CWE-79](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/79.html) · [CWE-94](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/94.html)

**Typical OWASP Top 10:** A01:2025

<a id="vektor-victim-required"></a>
### Victim-Required

**Attacker position:** Attacker needs victim interaction - social engineering, crafted link, or live session · **Breach distance:** 2

**Preconditions:**

- Victim must click a link, load a page, or have an active session
- Applies to XSS, CSRF, click-jacking, open redirect

**Typical CWEs:** [CWE-79](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/79.html) · [CWE-352](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/352.html) · [CWE-601](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/601.html) · [CWE-1021](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/1021.html)

**Typical OWASP Top 10:** A01:2025, A05:2025

<a id="vektor-build-time"></a>
### Build-Time

**Attacker position:** Attacker controls a build input - CI runner, dependency, base image, or external data fetched during build · **Breach distance:** 3

**Preconditions:**

- Compromise of a dependency, registry, or base image
- OR compromise of a CI runner with write access to artifacts

**Typical CWEs:** [CWE-506](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/506.html) · [CWE-829](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/829.html) · [CWE-1039](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/1039.html) · [CWE-1104](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/1104.html)

**Typical OWASP Top 10:** A08:2025

<a id="vektor-repo-read"></a>
### Repo-Read

**Attacker position:** Attacker gains read access to source repository (leaked clone, forked fork, insider, compromised developer workstation) · **Breach distance:** 3

**Preconditions:**

- Read access to the source tree at or after commit time
- No runtime exploit needed - the vulnerability is the content of the repo

**Typical CWEs:** [CWE-798](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/798.html) · [CWE-312](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/312.html) · [CWE-540](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/540.html)

**Typical OWASP Top 10:** A04:2025, A07:2025

<a id="vektor-n-a"></a>
### n/a

**Attacker position:** Architectural / meta-finding - no runtime entry point, the finding describes a design defect aggregating multiple code-level findings

**Preconditions:**

- Finding is AF-NNN (architectural) rather than F-NNN (code-level)
